# Conrad V2 Technical and Production Specification
Software Implementation and Research Source of Truth
# 1. Executive Overview, Boundaries and Terminology
Conrad V2 is an autonomous underwater infrastructure-inspection system that reconstructs persistent hidden world state from incomplete observations, identifies decision-relevant gaps in its knowledge, acquires additional evidence, and acts through a constrained robotics stack. Kerem, the software co-founder, owns the executable intelligence, simulation, training and integration program. Burak, the hardware owner, supplies the physical platform, measured parameters and device interfaces through RobotConfig and RobotHardwareInterface.
The architectural ownership rule is Twins = truth world; Model 2 = belief world; Model 1 = decision world. Twin 1 is scrapped. Unity V2 remains the robot-physics and sensor-interaction simulator. Twin2S owns canonical mission geometry; Twin2T owns structural condition; Twin2E owns ecological and environmental state. Unity instantiates and interacts with that shared world.
## Responsibility and Hardware-Software Interface Boundary
This is Kerem’s primary Technical and Production/R&D specification as software co-founder. It is intentionally complete about physical-hardware facts that software consumes, but it does not assign fabrication or physical-platform production to Kerem. Architecture expansion remains frozen.
BURAK - PHYSICAL HARDWARE OWNER. Burak owns the mechanical and electrical robot: physical architecture, frame/enclosure, waterproofing, buoyancy/ballast, propulsion hardware, thrusters/motor controllers, battery/BMS/power distribution, wiring/connectors, embedded hardware and compute selection/physical integration, sensor hardware/integration, underwater communications hardware, leak/failsafe/recovery hardware, modular hardware/custom SoC R&D where applicable, fabrication/assembly, bench testing, characterization, and physical test execution.
KEREM - SOFTWARE CO-FOUNDER, SOFTWARE AND R&D OWNER. Kerem owns all shared software infrastructure; schemas; evidence, belief, uncertainty and provenance; Model 2 Core (ECMER, association, PBA/Persistent Belief Graph, BUO, RBP, TBD, PMBL); 2T/TCDP/Twin2T/MCDE; 2E/CEFD/Twin2E/MEIFE; 2S/UAHSM/Twin2S/OCPWE; Model1/EGDC; MCBR; BAAC; Unity software; state estimation; navigation, trajectory, control, thruster allocation and safety software; training/evaluation; dataset adapters; experiment infrastructure; software HIL/integration; and research validation.
SHARED INTEGRATION. The parties jointly define and verify the hardware-software contract, execute HIL, integrate the physical robot with the software stack, manage sim-to-real transfer, run controlled physical validation and mission validation, and debug failures that cross the boundary. “Shared” never obscures the underlying deliverable owner stated below.
### Central Contract
BURAK PHYSICAL HARDWARE
          |
          v
RobotConfig + RobotCapabilities + Calibration Data
+ Characterization Data + RobotHardwareInterface
          |
          v
SHARED INTEGRATION BOUNDARY
          |
          v
KEREM - SOFTWARE CO-FOUNDER: INTELLIGENCE / AUTONOMY / SIMULATION / R&D
The upper software stack consumes the common RobotHardwareInterface and must not branch on UnityRobotHardware versus PhysicalRobotHardware except inside concrete adapters. The existing complete RobotHardwareInterface contract remains authoritative. At minimum, equivalent implementations expose get_imu(), get_depth(), get_camera(), get_sonar(), get_thruster_state(), get_power_state(), get_health(), and set_thruster_commands(...), with the full current APIs, message envelopes, timing, frame and health semantics retained in the interface chapters.
### Responsibility Matrix
Major subsystem | Owner | Support / required input from other party | Deliverable | Integration point
Physical vehicle, enclosure, buoyancy, propulsion, power, physical sensors, communications and recovery hardware | Burak | Software provides required data/interface constraints | Physical robot and documented operating envelope | RobotConfig, RobotCapabilities, calibration, characterization and driver-facing RHI implementation
Physical configuration and characterization | Burak | Software specifies schema, units, provenance and consumes records | Measured parameters, raw logs, uncertainty/range and calibration records | Unity parameters, sensor models, parameter identification, controller/allocation constraints and sim-to-real
RobotConfig, RobotCapabilities and software-side RobotHardwareInterface | Kerem | Burak supplies measured/configuration facts and physical adapter capability | Versioned validators, capability contract, software adapters and contract tests | Same interface for Unity and physical adapters
Unity V2, Twin2T/Twin2E/Twin2S, Model 2, Model1, MCBR and BAAC | Kerem | Burak supplies only declared physical constraints/measurements | Executable simulation, intelligence, autonomy and evaluation packages | RHI, configuration and characterization inputs
State estimation, planning, trajectory/control, allocation and safety software | Kerem | Burak supplies geometry, thrust limits/latency, sensor and power/health constraints | Software stack with tests and safety contracts | Commands/telemetry through RHI; physical envelope enforced jointly
HIL, sim-to-real and controlled physical validation | Shared | Burak owns physical hardware access, calibration and hardware debugging; Kerem owns software/simulation, execution logs and evaluation | Signed integration records, replayable logs, fault/timing evidence and acceptance report | Closed-loop runs across the common interface
### Hardware Handoff Required by Software
Burak’s hardware deliverable is PHYSICAL ROBOT + ROBOT CONFIGURATION + CALIBRATION + CHARACTERIZATION DATA + HARDWARE INTERFACE. The handoff contains, with units, frames, timestamps, ranges, uncertainty and provenance: mass, inertia, center of mass, center of buoyancy and displaced volume; thruster positions/orientations, allocation geometry, thrust curves, asymmetry, deadzones, saturation and latency; sensor noise, bias, drift, latency and calibration; communication bandwidth, latency, packet loss and BER; battery/power state and limits; compute availability/thermal limits; and measured health/failure characteristics. These artifacts feed Unity, parameter identification, navigation/control, sensor models, HIL, sim-to-real and physical validation. Unmeasured values remain OPEN rather than becoming nominal facts.
### Classification of Hardware Material
For a pure physical-hardware section or task, the following labels are normative:
HARDWARE OWNER: BURAK
SOFTWARE STATUS: REFERENCE / INTERFACE CONTEXT
Such material is retained because its measured outputs constrain software; it is not an instruction for the software co-founder to fabricate, wire, waterproof or physically commission the robot. Software-facing contracts, simulation, interfaces and evaluation remain software-owned. Any assignment not plainly physical, software or shared is retained and marked OPEN / HUMAN REVIEW rather than silently reclassified.
### HIL, Sim-to-Real and Physical Validation Split
Kerem owns the software/simulator/interfaces, HIL execution harness, logging, replay and evaluation. Burak owns hardware access, physical drivers as applicable, configuration/calibration/characterization, physical safety preparation and hardware debugging. Closed-loop timing, fault behavior, performance interpretation and mission validation are shared. A future Conrad V2 Hardware Engineering & Production Specification should be the production truth for physical build instructions; it must connect to, rather than remove software-needed material from, RobotConfig, RobotCapabilities, RobotHardwareInterface, Calibration, Characterization, HIL, Sim-to-Real and Physical Validation.
The production loop is:
SharedScenario -> Twin2T / Twin2E / Twin2S
                       |
                Unity / physical sensors
                       |
Observation -> ECMER -> Evidence -> Model2 Core + 2T / 2E / 2S
                                      |
                       Persistent Belief Graph / Belief Bus
                                      |
                        Model1 / EGDC -> InformationNeed
                                      |       |
                                      |      MCBR -> ObservationPlan
                                      |                    |
                                      +-----> Navigation -> Control
                                                              |
                           RobotHardwareInterface <- Allocator
                                      |
                               Unity / real robot

Belief changes -> BAAC -> constrained channel -> receiver beliefs
Safety Supervisor can override the execution path.
The first serious demonstration centers on 2S + 2T + persistent belief + active inspection. A partly hidden pipeline condition produces a specific unresolved belief; MCBR chooses a discriminating view; navigation executes it; new evidence changes the structural belief; Model1 makes a grounded decision; BAAC communicates the finding within the available link budget. Full 2E sophistication and advanced control research can develop in parallel.
## Status and Scope
Architecture expansion is frozen. Proposed mechanisms remain RESEARCH HYPOTHESES until their experiment programs establish measurable value. Equations that specify candidate update rules are implementable hypotheses, not validation results. Numerical architecture sizes and training settings are initial experimental configurations unless explicitly identified otherwise.
Status | Production meaning
CONCEPT FROZEN | Module responsibilities and truth/belief/decision boundaries are stable.
INTERFACE FREEZE V1 | Achieved at Phase 0 gate P0 after schema and contract tests; not implied by the existence of this document.
EXPERIMENTAL | Implement a configurable candidate and compare it against the specified baselines.
OPEN | A decision, parameter, dataset or threshold must be resolved by the named implementation or experimental work.
VALIDATED | Requires linked evidence at the appropriate D0-D8 maturity and claim level.
Goals are persistent hidden-state estimation; calibrated uncertainty; evidence lineage; active information acquisition; grounded decisions; constrained communication; and a simulation-to-hardware execution path. Non-goals are introducing a new major subsystem, resurrecting Twin1, treating Model2 as a renamed image classifier, allowing Model1 to invent domain truth, or treating synthetic accuracy as physical validation.
## Reading and Authority
The technical chapters preserve the detailed mathematical and computational content of the Conrad V2 design discussion. Each chapter identifies its source message. Later implementation specifications govern where earlier conceptual notation differs. The consolidated roadmap uses Phases 0-16; integration gates I0-I9 are milestones inside that roadmap. D0-D8 is a separate component-maturity ladder.
The contract-closure chapter explicitly labels proposed engineering clarifications where the discussion left production details unresolved. These clarify existing interfaces and do not introduce new major subsystems. The phase execution tables organize existing work into inputs, outputs, tasks, data, baselines, experiments, acceptance and dependencies. They do not assign invented deadlines, measured hardware capabilities or achieved results.
## Mathematical Target
For domain din,E,S, the twin generates hidden state S_t^d,true and the inference stack estimates a belief conditioned only on permitted observations and context:
Equation. B_t^d~= P(S_t^dmid O_1:t,a_1:t-1,C_1:t).
Here C contains permitted prior and cross-domain context, not privileged twin labels. Between observations the belief is predicted through physical time; on arrival of new evidence it is corrected:
Equation. B_t+Delta td,-=TBD_d(B_td,+,Delta t,C),qquad B_t+Delta t^d,+=BUO_d(B_t+Delta t^d,-,E).
Relational propagation augments this estimate through typed edges with explicit inference provenance. The persistent graph is G_t=(V_t,R_t); each belief cell carries state, uncertainty, temporal context, history, identity and provenance. The exposed uncertainty contract is U=(U_A,U_E,U_C,U_O): aleatoric, epistemic, contradiction and observational uncertainty.
The action layer operates on beliefs, mission context and robot resources. A proposed action is executable only after deterministic constraints accept it. Candidate observations are ranked by their expected contribution to the mission-relevant belief problem, subject to feasibility, risk, energy and time.
## Terminology
Term | Meaning / responsibility
PBA | Persistent Belief Architecture; the shared Model2 approach.
Persistent Belief Graph | Persistent entities/regions and typed relationships carrying belief state.
ECMER | Evidence Encoding + Cross-Modal Evidence Representation.
BUO | Belief Update Operator; evidence-conditioned correction.
RBP | Relational Belief Propagation; typed, uncertain, provenance-aware inference.
TBD | Temporal Belief Dynamics; prediction using elapsed physical time.
PMBL | Persistent Memory + Belief Lifecycle; creation, retrieval, merge/split, dormancy and archive.
TCDP / MCDE | Topology-Constrained Degradation Propagation / Mechanism-Coupled Degradation Engine.
CEFD / MEIFE | Coupled Entity-Field Dynamics / Multi-Scale Ecological Interaction Field Engine.
UAHSM / OCPWE | Uncertainty-Aware Hierarchical Spatial Memory / Observability-Controlled Procedural World Engine.
EGDC | Evidence-Grounded Deliberative Controller, the proposed Model1 mechanism.
MCBR | Mission-Conditioned Belief Resolution.
BAAC | Belief-Aware Adaptive Communication.
RGDA | Reality-gap decomposition/adaptation methodology; its research value is experimental.
UIR | Unsupported world claims divided by evaluated world claims.
Twin usefulness | Held-out real performance with twin-assisted training minus a matched real-only comparator.
# 2. Shared World, IDs, Time, Frames and Belief Bus
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 351639f6. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Four-plane architecture
SHARED SCENARIO
      │
      ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│             TRUTH PLANE             │
│ Twin 2T      Twin 2E      Twin 2S  │
│ structural   ecological   spatial   │
└────────────────┬────────────────────┘
                 │
                 ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│          OBSERVATION PLANE          │
│ Unity / sensors / timestamps / pose │
│ ECMER structured evidence           │
└────────────────┬────────────────────┘
                 │
                 ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│             BELIEF PLANE            │
│ Model 2T     Model 2E     Model 2S │
│      Shared Belief / World Bus      │
└────────────────┬────────────────────┘
                 │
                 ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│            DECISION PLANE           │
│              Model 1                │
│ Active Intelligence / Navigation    │
└─────────────────────────────────────┘
Critical rule:
Model 2/Model 1 can never read Twin ground truth during deployment/inference.
Twin truth exists only for simulation, training and evaluation.
## Global identifiers
Everything persistent gets a globally unique ID.
Conceptually:
scenario_id
mission_id
world_entity_id
belief_id
observation_id
evidence_id
sensor_id
robot_id
event_id
relationship_id
Example:
world_entity_id = asset.pipeline03.segment12
But IDs should be machine identifiers, not dependent on human-readable names.
Human-readable aliases are metadata.
## Shared World Entity
The basic cross-domain object:
Equation. W_i
represents something physically meaningful in the world.
WorldEntity
id
entity_type
parent_id
geometry_ref
reference_frame
created_at
retired_at
domain_ownership
metadata
Examples:
pipeline
weld
coral_patch
seafloor_region
rock
support
habitat_region
Not every domain needs a belief about every entity.
## Domain ownership
This prevents duplicated truth.
Example pipeline segment:
World Entity:
Pipeline Segment 12
Twin 2S owns:
    geometry
    position
    occupancy
Twin 2T owns:
    material condition
    corrosion
    degradation
Twin 2E owns:
    ecological colonization
    associated habitat/ecology
Thus:
one entity, multiple domain states
rather than three independent copies of the same pipe.
## Shared Scenario Definition
Every synthetic experiment starts from:
Scenario
│
├── metadata
├── coordinate_system
├── world_entities
├── environment
├── structural_state
├── ecological_state
├── spatial_state
├── events
├── robots
├── sensors
└── mission
Twin children read only the sections relevant to them plus explicitly shared context.
## Coordinate frames
We need this from Day 1.
Every spatial quantity carries:
frame_id
timestamp
position
orientation
spatial_uncertainty
Canonical hierarchy might conceptually be:
WORLD
 │
 ├── ASSET
 │    └── COMPONENT
 │
 └── ROBOT
      └── SENSOR
Transforms:
Equation. T_A<- B(t)
must be explicit.
Never silently assume two vectors are in the same frame.
## Time
Every time-varying object uses a common time representation.
At minimum:
timestamp
sequence_index
mission_time
and when relevant:
measurement_start
measurement_end
This matters because sensor evidence can be asynchronous.
## Truth-state object
Twin-only:
TruthState
scenario_id
world_entity_id
domain
timestamp
state
relationships
event_context
ground_truth_quality
generator_version
Domains:
TECHNICAL
ECOLOGICAL
SPATIAL
Again:
TruthState is inaccessible to inference modules.
Training infrastructure is allowed to compare predictions against it.
## Raw Observation
Physical/simulated sensor output:
Observation
observation_id
mission_id
sensor_id
modality
timestamp
sensor_frame
robot_pose_estimate
pose_uncertainty
payload_ref
sensor_health
calibration_ref
Examples of payload_ref:
RGB frame
sonar scan
depth frame
temperature reading
IMU sequence
## Evidence Object
ECMER converts observations into our standardized object:
Equation. E_k=(e_k,m_k,t_k,x_k,rho_k,u_k^A,v_k,p_k).
Computational schema:
Evidence
evidence_id
source_observation_id
modality
timestamp
embedding
spatial_support
entity_candidates
reliability
aleatoric_uncertainty
validity
sensor_context
provenance
One observation can generate:
Equation. O_k->_k1,…,E_kn.
## Provenance
This needs to be first-class.
Every belief claim should ultimately be traceable.
Define:
ProvenanceRecord
record_id
source_type
source_ids
operation
module
model_version
timestamp
parent_records
Source types:
DIRECT_OBSERVATION
RELATIONAL_INFERENCE
TEMPORAL_PREDICTION
CROSS_DOMAIN_CONTEXT
PRIOR
This creates a provenance DAG.
## Why provenance DAG matters
Suppose Model 1 receives:
Segment 12 may require reinspection.
We should eventually be able to trace:
Decision
   ↓
2T structural belief
   ↓
TCDP inference
   ↓
Weld 4 belief
   ↓
RGB evidence + sonar evidence
   ↓
raw observations
That is vastly more defensible than:
AI confidence = 87%.
## Belief Status
Every claim carries:
Equation. Kin  OBSERVED, INFERRED, PREDICTED, UNKNOWN .
I’d also allow:
MIXED
because a belief may contain both directly observed and inferred components.
But individual claims should retain their own status where possible.
## Shared uncertainty object
All children expose the same top-level uncertainty contract:
Uncertainty
aleatoric
epistemic
contradiction
observational
representation_type
calibration_metadata
The internal representation can differ.
Thus Model 1 can ask:
Why are you uncertain?
without knowing whether 2E uses an ensemble and 2S uses another probabilistic representation.
## Shared Belief Message
This is probably the most important interface.
Each Model 2 child exports:
BeliefMessage
belief_id
world_entity_id
domain
timestamp
state_summary
state_embedding
knowledge_status
uncertainty
evidence_support
evidence_conflicts
change_summary
prediction_summary
relationships
provenance_refs
model_version
This becomes the primary object on the Shared Belief Bus.
## Domain-specific payloads remain possible
We should not force all scientific information into one generic vector.
So:
BeliefMessage
│
├── common_header
│
└── domain_payload
For 2T:
TechnicalPayload
degradation
condition
structural relationships
...
2E:
EcologicalPayload
population
habitat
environmental field
...
2S:
SpatialPayload
geometry
occupancy
observability
...
## Shared Belief Bus
Conceptually:
Model 2T
                 │
                 ▼
         ┌───────────────┐
Model 2E →  BELIEF BUS   ← Model 2S
         └───────┬───────┘
                 │
        ┌────────┼─────────┐
        ▼        ▼         ▼
      Model 1  Dashboard  Active
                         Intelligence
This should eventually be asynchronous.
Children publish updates when their beliefs change materially.
## Children should NOT directly mutate each other
Critical rule:
Equation. 2Tnrightarrowwrite into 2E
and vice versa.
Instead:
2T publishes belief
       ↓
shared bus
       ↓
2E may consume it as
CROSS_DOMAIN_CONTEXT
2E then decides how much that context affects its own inference.
That preserves provenance and modularity.
## Cross-domain relationships
We need explicit cross-domain edges.
Example:
EcologicalRegion E14
       OCCUPIES
          ↓
PipelineSurface S8
or:
TurbidityField E3
       AFFECTS_OBSERVABILITY_OF
          ↓
SpatialRegion S12
Represent:
Equation. R_ij^cross = (type,source,target,uncertainty,provenance).
Cross-domain effects should only exist where justified.
## Example: marine growth affecting inspection
One physical situation:
### 2E
reports:
dense biological coverage
### 2S
reports:
surface visibility poor
### 2T
receives both as context.
It does not conclude:
corrosion exists.
Instead:
structural condition:
uncertain
observational uncertainty:
high
reason:
surface obscured
Then Model 1 may request another observation modality.
This is exactly the kind of cross-domain reasoning we want.
## Shared world vs shared belief
Keep these separate.
### Shared world
Simulation-side canonical reality:
Equation. mathcal W^true
### Shared belief
Inference-side current knowledge:
Equation. hatmathcal W_t
Never let:
Equation. mathcal W^true ->hatmathcal W
except through observations during inference.
## Twin interface
Each twin implements conceptually:
initialize(scenario)
step(dt, events)
get_truth(timestamp)
generate_observation(
    sensor,
    pose,
    timestamp
)
export_domain_state()
reset(seed)
But children differ internally.
This gives our training infrastructure one common simulator contract.
## Model 2 child interface
Each child implements:
initialize(context)
ingest(evidence)
update_beliefs()
predict(delta_t)
query(query_spec)
export_beliefs()
reset_working_memory()
Persistent memory is not deleted by resetting working memory.
## Query interface
Model 1 shouldn’t always need every belief.
It can request:
BeliefQuery
domain
region
entity_ids
time_range
requested_fields
uncertainty_threshold
include_provenance
include_predictions
Example:
Give me structural beliefs within 2 m of this weld with observational uncertainty > threshold.
That is far more scalable than dumping entire world state into Model 1.
## Model/Twin separation in code
Repository architecture should enforce separation.
Conceptually:
core/
    schemas/
    belief/
    evidence/
    uncertainty/
    provenance/
models/
    model2t/
    model2e/
    model2s/
twins/
    twin2t/
    twin2e/
    twin2s/
simulation/
    unity_bridge/
orchestration/
    belief_bus/
training/
evaluation/
No production inference import should casually expose get_truth().
## Versioning
Every important artifact carries versions:
schema_version
model_version
twin_version
encoder_version
scenario_version
dataset_version
calibration_version
Otherwise six months later we won’t know which synthetic worlds trained which checkpoint.
## Deterministic replay
Every experiment stores:
scenario seed
scenario version
twin versions
model versions
sensor parameters
events
trajectory
observations
config
Then:
Equation. Replay(seed,config)
should reproduce the experiment as closely as practical.
Essential for research.
## Shared scenario consistency
Eventually one scenario definition produces:
World Entity
             Pipeline P3
                  │
       ┌──────────┼──────────┐
       ▼          ▼          ▼
   Twin 2T     Twin 2E    Twin 2S
 corrosion    bio-growth   geometry
This eliminates contradictory simulation.
For example, if Twin 2S says the pipe doesn’t exist, Twin 2T cannot attach corrosion to it.
## Domain update rates can differ
Do not force synchronized inference.
For example:
Equation. f_2S>f_2T>f_2E
may often make sense.
2S may process spatial observations rapidly.
2T updates when useful inspection evidence arrives.
2E may operate over slower ecological timescales.
The belief bus therefore needs asynchronous timestamped messages.
## Failure isolation
If Model 2E crashes or becomes unavailable:
Equation. 2T+2S
should continue operating.
Likewise, missing sonar should not crash ECMER.
Modules expose:
AVAILABLE
DEGRADED
UNAVAILABLE
STALE
This becomes useful context for Model 1.
## Schema invariants
We should eventually enforce automated tests such as:
Equation. timestamp_evidencege timestamp_observation
every evidence object has provenance;
every belief has uncertainty;
every predicted claim is marked predicted;
every cross-domain inference identifies its source;
every spatial quantity specifies coordinate frame;
every persistent entity has unique ID;
Twin truth never enters inference API.
These become actual CI tests later.
## End-to-end example
Robot approaches pipeline.
### World truth
Twin 2S:
pipeline geometry
Twin 2T:
moderate material loss
Twin 2E:
dense marine growth
### Observation
RGB image + sonar.
### ECMER
Produces independent evidence objects.
### 2S
pipeline geometry observed
rear surface unknown
### 2E
high biological surface coverage
moderate uncertainty
### 2T
RGB structural evidence is weak because of occlusion/growth.
Sonar provides partial structural evidence.
Outputs:
material loss hypothesis: moderate
aleatoric uncertainty: moderate
observational uncertainty: high
contradiction: low
### Shared belief bus
All three publish.
### Model 1 later reasons
The structural question matters but evidence is insufficient.
### Active Intelligence later asks
Which next observation would reduce relevant uncertainty most?
That is the full architecture beginning to work as one system.
# 3. Model 2 Core: Schemas, Tensors, Modules and Training
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 752eb594. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Technology baseline
For the research stack, current default:
Python 3.12
PyTorch
NumPy
Pydantic
PyYAML
pytest
HDF5 / Zarr / Parquet where appropriate
Weights & Biases or MLflow
Git + Git LFS/DVC-style dataset manifests
We shouldn’t freeze experiment tracking/storage technology yet, but PyTorch + typed Python schemas is the sensible baseline.
Unity remains C#.
Robot embedded layer can later contain C++/Python depending compute requirements.

Rule:
models never own dataset-specific loading code.
Dataset adapters normalize public datasets into our schemas.
## Universal dimensions
We need defaults for implementation, but not permanent architectural commitments.
Start:
Equation. D_e=256
Evidence embedding.
Equation. D_z=256
belief state.
Equation. D_u=64
learned uncertainty representation.
Equation. D_t=64
temporal representation.
Equation. D_h=128
compact history representation.
Equation. D_r=64
relationship features.
These are experimental configuration parameters:
model2_core:
  evidence_dim: 256
  belief_dim: 256
  uncertainty_dim: 64
  temporal_dim: 64
  history_dim: 128
  relation_dim: 64
Never hard-code them into modules.
## Observation schema
Raw observations stay outside the neural representation.
class Observation:
    observation_id: UUID
    sensor_id: UUID
    robot_id: UUID
    modality: Modality
    timestamp_ns: int
    frame_id: str
    pose_estimate: Pose
    pose_uncertainty: PoseUncertainty
    calibration_id: UUID
    payload_ref: str
    sensor_health: SensorHealth
    metadata: dict
payload_ref points to image/sonar/etc.
Do not place 4K image arrays inside Pydantic messages traveling through the system.
## Evidence schema
ECMER output:
class Evidence:
    evidence_id: UUID
    observation_id: UUID
    modality: Modality
    timestamp_ns: int
    embedding: Tensor       # [De]
    spatial_support: SpatialSupport
    reliability: Tensor
    aleatoric: Tensor
    validity: float
    entity_candidates: list[EntityCandidate]
    provenance_id: UUID
Batch representation:
Equation. Einmathbb R^Kx D_e.
## Evidence ≠ belief
This remains a hard invariant.
Never allow:
belief.embedding = evidence.embedding
as architectural semantics.
Evidence says:
this observation contains this information.
Belief says:
given all evidence/history, this is my current estimate of world state.
Different objects.
## Uncertainty schema
Externally:
class Uncertainty:
    aleatoric: float
    epistemic: float
    contradiction: float
    observational: float
    calibration_version: str
Conceptually:
Equation. U=(U_A,U_E,U_C,U_O).
Internally the network may maintain:
Equation. u_iinmathbb R^64
plus explicit output heads:
Equation. h_A(u),h_E(u),h_C(u),h_O(u).
This gives us richer internal uncertainty while preserving interpretability.
## Provenance
Provenance cannot just be a string.
class ProvenanceNode:
    provenance_id: UUID
    source_type: ProvenanceType
    source_ids: list[UUID]
    model_version: str | None
    timestamp_ns: int
    operation: str
    confidence: float | None
Types:
DIRECT_OBSERVATION
RELATIONAL_INFERENCE
TEMPORAL_PREDICTION
CROSS_DOMAIN_CONTEXT
PRIOR
These form a DAG.
## Belief Cell
Persistent database representation:
class BeliefCell:
    belief_id: UUID
    world_entity_id: UUID | None
    domain: Domain
    state_embedding_ref: str
    uncertainty: Uncertainty
    knowledge_status: KnowledgeStatus
    created_at: int
    updated_at: int
    last_observed_at: int | None
    observation_count: int
    lifecycle: BeliefLifecycle
    provenance_root: UUID
    metadata: dict
The actual tensors needn’t be serialized through JSON every update.
## GPU belief representation
For active working graph of N beliefs:
Equation. Zinmathbb R^Nx256
Equation. Uinmathbb R^Nx64
Equation. Tinmathbb R^Nx64
Equation. Hinmathbb R^Nx128.
Relationships:
Equation. edge_indexinmathbb N^2x M
Equation. Rinmathbb R^Mx64.
This is what Model 2 actually computes over.
## Belief working batch
Conceptually:
@dataclass
class BeliefGraphBatch:
    z: Tensor
    u: Tensor
    temporal: Tensor
    history: Tensor
    edge_index: LongTensor
    edge_features: Tensor
    belief_ids: list[UUID]
    metadata: BeliefMetadataBatch
We can later use PyTorch Geometric if experiments justify it, but our schema should not depend on PyG.
## ECMER module interface
class EvidenceEncoder(nn.Module):
    def forward(
        self,
        observation_batch,
        sensor_context
    ) -> EvidenceBatch:
        ...
Internally:
observation
    ↓
modality encoder
    ↓
feature
    ↓
quality estimator
    ↓
spatial grounding
    ↓
Evidence
## Modality encoders
Separate:
RGBEncoder
SonarEncoder
DepthEncoder
IMUEncoder
EnvironmentalEncoder
StructuredMetadataEncoder
Each returns:
Equation. e_kinmathbb R^256.
They can have radically different architectures.
Only their output contract is shared.
## ECMER quality heads
For feature:
Equation. f_k
predict:
Equation. rho_k
reliability and:
Equation. U_A^k.
Potential output:
EvidenceQuality:
    reliability
    aleatoric
    validity
    ood_score
Important:
Equation. boxedreliability!= semantic confidence.
## ECMER training
Stage E1 - modality representation.
Use:
self-supervised
supervised where labels exist
temporal correspondence
cross-view correspondence
Stage E2 - degradation robustness.
Apply:
blur
noise
occlusion
turbidity
dropout
pose perturbation
with known corruption severity.
Stage E3 - quality estimation.
Train quality predictions against controlled degradation and downstream evidence usefulness.
Stage E4 - multimodal correspondence.
Learn which evidence objects plausibly concern same entity/region without early fusion.
## ECMER losses
Conceptually:
Equation. L_E= lambda_repL_rep + lambda_qL_q + lambda_spatialL_spatial + lambda_corrL_corr + lambda_oodL_ood.
Exact loss functions differ by modality.
## Association engine
Inputs:
Equation. Einmathbb R^Kx256
Equation. Zinmathbb R^Nx256.
But don’t compute every:
Equation. Kx N
pair if graph is large.
First deterministic candidate retrieval.
## Candidate retrieval
candidate_ids = retrieve_candidates(
    evidence,
    spatial_region,
    timestamp,
    entity_hint,
    domain
)
Use:
known entity identity
spatial proximity
topology
time
domain constraints
Then learned compatibility only among candidates.
This is both computationally better and more defensible.
## Association scorer
For candidate pair:
Equation. x_ik = [ W_z z_i; W_e e_k; u_i; rho_k; Delta t; c_ik ].
Then:
Equation. s_ik=MLP_assoc(x_ik).
Probability:
Equation. a_ik=sigma(s_ik)
or normalized candidate distribution depending association type.
Must support:
Equation. boxedNO MATCH
because new evidence may concern a new entity.
## Association loss
Where twin/public labels provide identity:
Equation. L_assoc = CE(A,A^true).
Need explicit negative/no-match examples.
Evaluate:
association accuracy
false association rate
new-entity recall
False association is particularly dangerous because it corrupts persistent memory.
## BUO computational module
For associated belief i and evidence k:
Equation. x_ik= [z_i,e_k,u_i,u_k^A,rho_k,tau_i,c_ik].
Heads:
Equation. g^trust_ik
Equation. g^innovation_ik
Equation. p^support_ik, p^conflict_ik, p^ambiguous_ik
Equation. n_ik
Equation. Delta z_ik.
## BUO class
class BeliefUpdateOperator(nn.Module):
    def forward(
        self,
        beliefs: BeliefTensorBatch,
        evidence: EvidenceTensorBatch,
        associations: AssociationBatch
    ) -> BeliefUpdate:
        ...
Return:
candidate_state
trust
innovation
compatibility
novelty
uncertainty_delta
provenance_updates
## Multiple evidence aggregation
Suppose m evidence objects update belief i:
Equation. E_i= _1,ldots,e_m.
Aggregation must be permutation invariant:
Equation. F(E_i)=F(pi(E_i)).
V1 candidates:
DeepSets;
weighted pooling;
Set Transformer;
structured cross-attention.
Default baseline first:
weighted pooling + MLP
Then compare more complex alternatives.
## BUO state update
Starting implementation hypothesis:
Equation. tildeDelta z_i = Aggregate( g^trust_ik g^innovation_ik Delta z_ik ).
Then:
Equation. z_i’=LayerNorm(z_i+tildeDelta z_i).
But this equation is explicitly:
RESEARCH HYPOTHESIS
not frozen.
## Contradiction update
Do not:
Equation. conflict=> trustdownarrow.
Instead:
high reliability + conflict
        ↓
credible contradiction
        ↓
UC increases
        ↓
belief may remain unresolved
Repeated independent evidence can later resolve it.
We need synthetic tests specifically for this.
## BUO training targets
Twin gives:
Equation. S_i^true(t).
Before evidence:
Equation. B_i^-.
After evidence:
Equation. B_i^+.
Train so:
Equation. d(B_i+,S_itrue) < d(B_i-,S_itrue)
when evidence is informative.
But not all evidence should improve belief.
Corrupted/uninformative evidence should produce minimal or appropriately uncertain updates.
## BUO losses
Core:
Equation. L_BUO = lambda_sL_state + lambda_cL_compat + lambda_uL_uncertainty + lambda_nL_novelty + lambda_calL_calibration.
Add child-specific state losses later.
## RBP computational module
For relationship:
Equation. (i,j,r_ij)
compute:
Equation. m_ij = M_theta( z_i,z_j,r_ij,u_i,u_j ).
Gate:
Equation. g_ij = sigma( G_theta(…) ).
Aggregate:
Equation. m_j = Aggregate_i(g_ijm_ij).
Update:
Equation. z_j’ = Update(z_j,m_j).
## RBP provenance
Critical implementation detail:
RBP output must create provenance:
RELATIONAL_INFERENCE
sources:
    belief_i
    belief_j
    relationship_ij
Never relabel relational inference as direct observation.
## RBP propagation depth
Config:
rbp:
  max_layers: 3
  adaptive_stop: true
Initial implementation can use fixed L=1,2,3.
Later adaptive stopping based on:
Equation. Delta information < epsilon.
## RBP anti-hallucination loss
Twin knows hidden states.
We should penalize propagation that makes unrelated beliefs confidently incorrect.
Conceptually:
Equation. L_unsupported = ConfidenceWrong(RelationalInference).
Especially important for TCDP.
## TBD interface
class TemporalBeliefDynamics(nn.Module):
    def forward(
        self,
        belief_state,
        uncertainty,
        temporal_state,
        context,
        delta_t
    ) -> PredictedBelief:
        ...
Input includes actual:
Equation. Delta t
in seconds - not merely sequence index.
## TBD baseline implementation
Before exotic architectures:
Equation. [z,u,t,c,Delta t] -> MLP/GRU -> [Delta z,Delta u].
This gives us a sanity baseline.
Then compare:
GRU
LSTM
SSM
RSSM
Neural ODE
domain-specific hybrid dynamics
## Predict/correct sequence
At t:
Equation. B_t^+.
No observation for Delta t:
Equation. B_t+Delta t^- = TBD(B_t^+,Delta t).
Observation arrives:
Equation. B_t+Delta t^+ = BUO(B_t+Delta t^-,E).
Persist both statuses:
PREDICTED
OBSERVED/INFERRED updated
## Temporal surprise
When evidence arrives:
Equation. delta_t = d( E_t, hat B_t ).
Store temporal surprise.
Potential uses:
change detection
anomaly detection
TBD calibration
Model1 attention
## PMBL implementation
Three storage levels:
### Working
GPU tensors.
### Persistent
Current beliefs + compact histories + relationships.
### Archive
Raw evidence/provenance.
Conceptually:
GPU
 ↕
BeliefStore
 ↕
EvidenceArchive
## Belief history
Do not store every old 256-D state forever inside each belief object.
Maintain:
current state
compact learned history
important checkpoints
event history
raw evidence refs
Raw evidence remains recoverable externally.
## Lifecycle engine
States:
CANDIDATE
CONFIRMED
ACTIVE
DORMANT
RETIRED
REJECTED
Operations:
CREATE
CONFIRM
MERGE
SPLIT
DORMANT
REACTIVATE
RETIRE
REJECT
Every transition logged.
## Model 2 Core forward sequence
Actual computational flow:
def update(observations):
    evidence = ecmer(observations)
    candidates = association.retrieve(
        evidence,
        belief_store
    )
    associations = association.score(
        evidence,
        candidates
    )
    lifecycle.handle_unmatched(evidence)
    working_graph = memory.retrieve_working_graph(
        associations
    )
    direct_update = buo(
        working_graph,
        evidence,
        associations
    )
    relational_update = rbp(
        direct_update
    )
    uncertainty = uncertainty_module(
        relational_update
    )
    memory.persist(
        relational_update,
        uncertainty
    )
    return export_beliefs()
Between observation events:
predicted = tbd(
    beliefs,
    delta_t
)
This is now implementation-level enough to code a skeleton.
## Core training sequence
We should not train everything end-to-end on Day 1.
### C0 - encoder initialization
ECMER.
### C1 - association
Known synthetic identity.
### C2 - BUO
Single belief + evidence.
### C3 - contradiction/uncertainty.
### C4 - RBP.
### C5 - TBD.
### C6 - sequential BUO/TBD.
### C7 - BUO + RBP + TBD.
### C8 - persistent long sequences.
### C9 - real-domain adaptation.
Only progressively unfreeze.
## Sequence training
Example training sequence:
Equation. S_0,O_1,S_1,O_2,…,O_T,S_T.
Loop:
belief = initialize()
for t in sequence:
    belief = TBD(belief, dt)
    evidence = ECMER(obs[t])
    belief = BUO(belief, evidence)
    belief = RBP(belief)
    losses += compare(
        belief,
        truth[t]
    )
Backpropagation through manageable windows.
No indefinite BPTT.
## Truncated persistent training
For long sequence:
1 ─────────────────────────────── 10,000
Train windows:
1-64
65-128
129-192
...
but pass detached persistent belief state between windows.
Thus:
Equation. B_64-> stopgrad-> B_65.
This approximates long-term persistent operation without impossible memory use.
## Core loss
First computational formulation:
Equation. boxed L_core = lambda_sL_state + lambda_aL_assoc + lambda_uL_unc + lambda_rL_relation + lambda_tL_temporal + lambda_cL_contradiction + lambda_pL_provenance
But provenance loss is primarily consistency/structural validation, not necessarily neural loss.
## Configuration system
Every experiment:
experiment:
  id: CORE_BUO_E003
  seed: 42
dataset:
  name: synthetic_belief_v1
  split: train
model:
  belief_dim: 256
buo:
  aggregator: weighted_pool
  trust_head: mlp
  innovation_head: mlp
training:
  batch_size: 32
  lr: 0.0003
  epochs: 50
loss:
  state: 1.0
  uncertainty: 0.25
  contradiction: 0.5
No hidden hyperparameters buried in Python.
## Checkpoint format
Checkpoint stores:
model state
optimizer state
scheduler state
epoch/step
config
schema version
dataset manifest hash
git commit
random seed
metrics
This matters enormously for research reproducibility.
## Dataset manifest
Example:
dataset_id: subpipe_v1
source: ...
version: ...
license: ...
modalities:
  - rgb
  - sonar
  - imu
  - depth
tasks:
  - spatial
  - association
split_strategy:
  type: sequence_level
transformations:
  - resize
  - normalization
corruptions: []
checksum: ...
Public data never gets silently transformed without recording it.
## Universal sample format
Dataset adapter outputs:
SequenceSample(
    scenario_id,
    timestamps,
    observations,
    entity_metadata,
    labels,
    relationships,
    truth_available,
)
For twin data:
truth_available = True
with complete state.
For public real data:
truth_available = PartialTruth(...)
Loss system only evaluates available supervision.
## Partial supervision
Suppose real dataset only provides:
segmentation
pose
but not:
true degradation state
Then:
Equation. L_degradation
is masked out.
Do not invent pseudo-ground-truth unless explicitly part of a weak-supervision experiment.
## Experiment naming
Every experiment gets stable ID:
CORE-ASSOC-E001
CORE-BUO-E001
CORE-RBP-E001
CORE-TBD-E001
2T-TCDP-E001
2E-CEFD-E001
2S-UAHSM-E001
ACTIVE-MCBR-E001
COMMS-BAAC-E001
This eventually connects directly to the paper/IP notebook.
## Testing layers
Every implementation package needs four test levels.
### Unit
Tensor shapes / schema behavior.
### Property
Example:
Equation. Permutation(E)
doesn’t change set aggregation.
### Behavioral
Reliable supporting evidence should generally improve appropriate belief.
### Integration
Observation → belief works end-to-end.
## Core invariant tests
Examples:
INV-001
No Model2 inference code imports Twin truth API.
INV-002
Every belief has provenance.
INV-003
Predicted state marked PREDICTED.
INV-004
Every spatial evidence object has frame + timestamp.
INV-005
No-match association supported.
INV-006
Cross-domain update marked CROSS_DOMAIN_CONTEXT.
INV-007
Uncertainty always contains UA/UE/UC/UO.
INV-008
Relationship inference cannot masquerade as direct observation.
CI should literally fail on violations where mechanically testable.
## Compute strategy
The design discussed an RTX 3050 Ti as an example development GPU. Verify the available machine before planning compute. Local development tasks include:
skeleton development
small experiments
unit tests
small encoders
Unity
but the full system - especially large multimodal training - is likely going to require external GPU compute.
Therefore architecture must support:
local debug
      ↓
remote training
      ↓
same config
      ↓
local evaluation/inference
No machine-specific paths.
# 4. ECMER: Evidence Encoding and Cross-Modal Representation
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source bd6cf7ce. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Overall evidence pipeline
I propose:
Equation. O_k -> Enc_m -> F_k -> Q_k -> A_k -> E_k
Conceptually:
RAW OBSERVATIONS
 RGB       SONAR       DEPTH       ENV       OTHER
  │          │           │          │
  ▼          ▼           ▼          ▼
modality-specific encoders
  │          │           │          │
  └──────────┴──────┬────┴──────────┘
                    ▼
          temporal / pose alignment
                    │
                    ▼
             quality estimation
                    │
                    ▼
           evidence construction
                    │
                    ▼
              EVIDENCE SET
                    │
                    ▼
             BUO / Model 2
Crucially, I would not fuse everything into one vector here.
ECMER should preserve modality identity and independent evidence sources.
## Standard Evidence Object
Let’s formalize the earlier E_k.
A richer version:
Equation. E_k= ( e_k, m_k, t_k, x_k, rho_k, u_k^A, v_k, p_k )
where:
e_k: learned evidence representation
m_k: modality/type
t_k: timestamp
x_k: spatial/entity localization information
rho_k: estimated reliability
u_k^A: aleatoric/measurement uncertainty
v_k: validity/observability metadata
p_k: immutable provenance reference
Potentially also:
Equation. E_k^raw_ref
pointing to original evidence in the archive.
So BUO never receives an anonymous embedding.
It receives:
what was observed + where + when + by what + how reliable + how uncertain + where the original evidence came from.
## Modality-specific encoders
We shouldn’t force sonar and RGB through the same architecture.
Instead:
Equation. Enc_RGB(O^RGB)-> e^RGB
Equation. Enc_sonar(O^sonar)-> e^sonar
Equation. Enc_depth(O^depth)-> e^depth
Equation. Enc_env(O^env)-> e^env
etc.
Each encoder can be specialized.
For example, sonar statistics and failure modes are fundamentally different from RGB.
This also lets us add future sensors without redesigning Model 2 Core.
## Evidence should be structured, not necessarily frame-level
A full RGB image should not automatically become one evidence object.
One observation may produce:
Equation. O_k->_k1,E_k2,…,E_kn.
Example:
RGB FRAME
 ┌──────────────────────────────┐
 │ pipeline                     │
 │      corrosion region        │
 │              weld            │
 └──────────────────────────────┘
            ↓
E1 = pipeline-region evidence
E2 = weld evidence
E3 = anomaly-region evidence
Similarly sonar could generate spatial regions/features rather than one global embedding.
The granularity will be child-dependent.
## Evidence alignment
Before combining evidence, we need to know whether observations actually refer to comparable parts of reality.
Every observation should therefore carry synchronization information:
timestamp
robot pose
sensor pose
sensor calibration
coordinate frame
estimated localization uncertainty
Then ECMER can transform observations toward a shared world reference.
Conceptually:
Equation. x_k=T_world<- sensor(O_k).
This becomes essential for 2S, but also matters greatly for 2T.
## Asynchronous sensing
Real underwater sensors won’t necessarily produce observations simultaneously.
Suppose:
Camera    ──●──●──●──●──●──●──●──
Sonar     ─────●────────●────────●─
Env       ●───────────────●────────
IMU       ●●●●●●●●●●●●●●●●●●●●●●
We shouldn’t pretend they all occurred at the same t.
Every evidence object maintains its own:
Equation. t_k.
Temporal alignment becomes probabilistic/contextual rather than simple array concatenation.
That helps prevent false multimodal agreement.
## Quality estimator
This connects directly to our uncertainty architecture.
Each modality gets a quality estimator:
Equation. Q_m(O_k,C_k)->(rho_k,u_k^A).
For RGB it might consider:
blur
backscatter
lighting
contrast
occlusion
range
view angle
sensor health
For sonar:
SNR
range
multipath
acoustic interference
resolution
incidence geometry
sensor health
The estimator can combine engineered measurements with learned quality prediction.
This is an excellent place for hybrid physics/domain knowledge + learning.
## Reliability is not confidence
We should preserve this distinction.
Suppose an RGB classifier says:
corrosion: 0.97
but the frame is extremely blurred.
The downstream system shouldn’t interpret 0.97 as evidence reliability.
Instead:
Equation. semantic prediction confidence!=rho_k.
Reliability answers:
How much should this observation be trusted as evidence?
That is much closer to what BUO needs.
## Cross-modal correspondence
Now suppose:
RGB E₁
SONAR E₂
DEPTH E₃
appear to concern the same region/entity.
We need a correspondence operator:
Equation. C(E_i,E_j) -> P(E_i<-> E_j).
It can use:
Equation. space + time + geometry + semantics + learned compatibility.
But we still don’t collapse them immediately.
Instead we create an Evidence Group:
Equation. G_j=_RGB,E_sonar,E_depth.
That preserves independence.
## Agreement and disagreement
This is where our architecture becomes much more useful than naïve fusion.
Suppose:
RGB   → anomaly
SONAR → anomaly
DEPTH → anomaly
Independent agreement should strengthen evidence.
But:
RGB   → anomaly
SONAR → normal
DEPTH → uncertain
should produce explicit disagreement.
Therefore evidence groups can expose:
Equation. A_G=agreement structure
rather than simply producing an averaged vector.
This feeds directly into BUO’s contradiction machinery.
## Missing modalities must be normal
We should train Model 2 assuming:
Equation. available modalitiessubseteqpossible modalities.
For example:
RGB ✓
Sonar ✓
Depth ✗
Environmental ✓
must remain valid.
No architecture should require a fixed sensor bundle.
This is especially important because the physical robot is modular.
## Sensor degradation awareness
ECMER should also maintain sensor-health context.
If camera quality gradually deteriorates:
Equation. rho_camera(t)downarrow
the system should notice that observations from that sensor have become systematically unreliable.
Potentially:
Camera reliability history
        ↓
sensor degradation detector
        ↓
Model 1 / operator
        ↓
"camera inspection recommended"
This is a secondary capability, but useful.
## Child specialization
Again, shared contract - not shared encoder architecture.
### 2T
Evidence may be strongly component-centric:
RGB defect evidence
sonar geometry
NDT measurement
component identity
inspection history
environment
2T may convert observations into evidence tied to:
Equation. component IDs / structural regions.
### 2E
Evidence may represent:
organism
population
habitat region
environmental measurement
ecological event
Cross-modal ecological evidence may be more distributed and less tied to rigid objects.
### 2S
Evidence is fundamentally spatial:
geometry
occupancy
semantic object
surface
free space
visibility
pose constraint
2S therefore probably uses the richest spatial metadata.
## Training ECMER
Following our rule:
architecture + training + validation.
### Stage A - modality representation learning
Use public + synthetic data.
Possible objectives include:
Equation. L_self
self-supervised representation learning,
Equation. L_temporal
same-world-region across nearby times,
Equation. L_spatial
same physical region across viewpoints,
and where labels exist:
Equation. L_task.
### Stage B - cross-modal alignment
Twins give us something extremely valuable:
exact knowledge of whether observations from different sensors came from the same physical state.
Generate:
RGB region A
Sonar region A
Depth region A
as positive correspondence.
And:
RGB region A
Sonar region B
as negative correspondence.
Train cross-modal correspondence/alignment accordingly.
Public synchronized multimodal datasets provide real validation where available.
### Stage C - quality/reliability training
Take observations and deliberately corrupt them.
Example RGB:
original
↓
blur σ = ...
backscatter = ...
brightness = ...
occlusion = ...
Because we know corruption severity, we obtain controlled supervision.
Twin sensor models provide further ground truth.
Then real degraded observations are used for adaptation/testing.
### Stage D - modality dropout
During training randomly remove:
RGB
sonar
depth
environment
individually and jointly.
This prevents the model from becoming dependent on one sensor combination.
## Validation
ECMER gets its own benchmarks.
We should measure:
Representation quality: Does encoded evidence retain information needed downstream?
Correspondence: Can it correctly identify evidence referring to the same world entity/region?
Quality calibration: Does predicted reliability actually correlate with downstream error?
Cross-modal robustness: Does one bad modality contaminate the others?
Missing-modality robustness: Performance as sensors disappear.
Asynchronous robustness: Performance as timestamp separation increases.
Domain shift: Synthetic → real, clear → turbid, known sensor → changed sensor characteristics.
## Baselines
Compare against:
single modality
raw feature concatenation
early multimodal fusion
late multimodal fusion
standard cross-attention fusion
ECMER structured evidence
Then ablate:
− reliability estimation
− explicit modality identity
− spatial alignment
− independent evidence preservation
− disagreement representation
− modality dropout
− provenance
Again, we only keep complexity that proves useful.
## One important architecture decision
I do not think ECMER should itself decide the final world state.
Its responsibility should stop here:
Equation. boxed raw sensor observation -> structured evidence
Then:
Equation. boxed structured evidence -> world belief
belongs to Model 2.
That separation is extremely important.
Otherwise our “evidence encoder” gradually becomes another giant end-to-end perception model and makes BUO redundant.
## Model 2 Core architecture is now almost closed
We now have:
ECMER
Raw observations → structured evidence.
BUO
Evidence → direct belief update.
RBP
Related beliefs → structured information propagation.
TBD
Current belief → temporally evolved/predicted belief.
PMBL
Persistent world memory + belief lifecycle.
And surrounding all of it:
Decomposed Uncertainty
plus our:
Training + Validation Architecture
The flow is now:
RAW WORLD OBSERVATIONS
          │
          ▼
       ECMER
 structured evidence
          │
          ▼
         BUO
          │
          ▼
   updated beliefs
          │
          ▼
         RBP
          │
          ▼
  relationally informed
       beliefs
          │
          ▼
         TBD
          │
          ▼
 predicted/evolved beliefs
          │
          ▼
         PMBL
 persistent world state
          │
          └──────────► next observation → ECMER → BUO ...
# 5. BUO: Trust, Innovation, Contradiction and Update Mechanics
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 1df20739. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## The problem BUO solves
At time t, belief cell i already contains:
Equation. B_i^t-1=(z_i,u_i,q_i,tau_i,p_i)
Then new evidence E_t arrives.
We need:
Equation. boxedB_i^t=mathcal U_theta(B_i^t-1,E_t,C_t)
A naive neural network could concatenate everything and run an MLP/Transformer.
I don’t want that.
The update should explicitly reason through:
Does this evidence concern me?
        ↓
How reliable is it?
        ↓
Does it agree with existing belief?
        ↓
How old is existing belief?
        ↓
Is this genuinely new information?
        ↓
How much should belief change?
        ↓
How should uncertainty change?
That structure itself becomes part of Model 2’s inductive bias.
## Evidence object
After modality-specific encoders, every observation becomes a standardized evidence object:
Equation. E_k=(e_k,rho_k,t_k,s_k,l_k,m_k)
where:
e_k: learned evidence embedding
rho_k: estimated reliability
t_k: timestamp
s_k: sensor/modality identity
l_k: spatial/entity association information
m_k: metadata
For example:
Evidence #719
Embedding:      [...]
Sensor:         RGB
Timestamp:      14:32:07
Location:       ...
Associated:     Weld W17
Quality:        0.81
View angle:     ...
Visibility:     ...
Provenance:     frame #1831
Importantly, reliability isn’t just detector confidence.
It can depend on blur, turbidity, range, incidence angle, sensor health, OOD indicators, etc.
## Evidence association
Before updating anything, determine:
Equation. a_ik=P(E_k<-> B_i)
Essentially:
How likely is this evidence to concern belief cell i?
This could combine learned semantic similarity with explicit geometry/identity constraints.
For 2T, known asset topology may dominate.
For 2S, spatial correspondence may dominate.
For 2E, entity tracking/region association may dominate.
Evidence below an association threshold should not modify that cell.
## Evidence compatibility
Now we ask something different:
Does this evidence agree with what I currently believe?
Define:
Equation. c_ik=C_theta(B_i,E_k)
But I wouldn’t force compatibility into a single scalar internally.
We want at least three conceptual outcomes:
support / contradiction / ambiguity
Example:
Current belief:
W17 probably moderately degraded
Evidence A:
consistent with moderate degradation
→ SUPPORT
Evidence B:
strong evidence of severe degradation
→ CONTRADICTION
Evidence C:
almost completely obscured
→ AMBIGUOUS
Contradiction is especially important.
We don’t want the system to quietly average conflicting evidence.
## Information novelty
Two almost identical images shouldn’t count as two independent pieces of evidence.
So introduce:
Equation. n_ik=N_theta(E_k,mathcal E_i)
representing how much new information the observation contributes relative to existing evidence.
This prevents:
same viewpoint × 100
from creating artificial certainty.
That’s potentially a very useful component for repeated robotic inspections.
## Temporal relevance
Old beliefs should not simply disappear, but their relevance can change.
Define:
Equation. d_i=f(Delta t_i,domain state)
Crucially, decay shouldn’t mean:
old = wrong.
Instead it means:
lack of recent observation may increase uncertainty about the current state when the underlying phenomenon can evolve.
For a static seabed rock, ten days might matter very little.
For an actively degrading component or dynamic ecosystem, it could matter substantially.
This will therefore be child-specific.
## The Trust Gate
Now combine these signals.
I’d investigate a learned Evidence Trust Gate:
Equation. g_ik = sigma ( G_theta( a_ik, rho_k, c_ik, n_ik, d_i, u_i, C_t ) )
where:
Equation. 0<= g_ik<=1.
Conceptually:
association ─────┐
reliability ─────┤
compatibility ───┤
novelty ─────────┼──→ TRUST GATE ──→ update strength
time ────────────┤
uncertainty ─────┤
context ─────────┘
But there’s a catch.
Contradictory high-quality evidence should not necessarily receive low trust.
If five-year-old evidence says healthy and today’s excellent sonar strongly indicates change, contradiction may be exactly what we need to learn from.
Therefore compatibility and reliability must remain distinct.
## Proposed dual update
Instead of one gate, I think Model 2 should potentially have two learned signals:
### Evidence trust
Equation. g^trust_ik
How credible is this evidence?
### Belief innovation
Equation. g^innovation_ik
How strongly does this evidence indicate that our existing belief needs revision?
That gives:
EVIDENCE
                     │
           ┌─────────┴─────────┐
           ▼                   ▼
     TRUST ESTIMATOR      INNOVATION ESTIMATOR
           │                   │
           └─────────┬─────────┘
                     ▼
               BELIEF UPDATE
This is much better conceptually.
## Latent-state update
We can then calculate an evidence-conditioned candidate state:
Equation. tilde z_i^t = F_theta(z_i^t-1,e_k,C_t)
and update:
Equation. z_i^t = z_i^t-1 + g^trust_ik g^innovation_ik Delta z_ik.
Not necessarily this exact equation - we’ll experiment.
The important architectural idea is:
new evidence proposes a state change; trust and innovation control whether/how strongly that change enters persistent belief.
## Uncertainty gets its own update
I don’t want uncertainty to simply be:
MLP → confidence score
We need uncertainty dynamics.
Consider four cases:
Situation | Expected behavior
reliable supporting evidence | uncertainty ↓
unreliable evidence | uncertainty ≈ or ↑
reliable contradictory evidence | uncertainty initially ↑
repeated independent evidence resolving contradiction | uncertainty ↓
That gives very interesting behavior.
Example:
W17
t1:
probably healthy
uncertainty = moderate
t2:
good evidence says healthy
uncertainty ↓
t3:
good evidence says degraded
CONTRADICTION
uncertainty ↑
t4:
independent sonar confirms degradation
belief changes
uncertainty ↓
That’s much closer to how an inspection-intelligence system should behave.
## Contradiction memory
I’d explicitly preserve unresolved contradictions.
Something like:
Equation. K_i=_a,E_b,ldots

rather than forcing the network to immediately choose one interpretation.
Conceptually:
BELIEF W17
│
├── current latent belief
├── uncertainty
├── supporting evidence
└── unresolved contradiction
       ├── RGB #183
       └── sonar #91
Model 1 or Active Intelligence can later see:
This component has unresolved high-quality contradictory evidence.
That’s a perfect reason to reinspect it.
## Belief update pipeline
So our proposed BUO becomes:
NEW EVIDENCE
     │
     ▼
Evidence Association
     │
     ▼
Reliability Estimation
     │
     ├───────────────┐
     ▼               ▼
Compatibility     Novelty
Analysis          Analysis
     │               │
     └───────┬───────┘
             ▼
       Temporal Context
             │
             ▼
    ┌──────────────────┐
    │ TRUST ESTIMATOR  │
    │ INNOVATION EST.  │
    └────────┬─────────┘
             ▼
      Candidate Update
             │
             ▼
      Latent State Update
             │
             ├───────────────┐
             ▼               ▼
    Uncertainty Update   Contradiction Memory
             │               │
             └───────┬───────┘
                     ▼
               UPDATED BELIEF
Now Model 2 is starting to look like an actual architecture rather than a branding concept.
## How we train this without collecting data
This architecture is especially suited to synthetic training.
The twins can give us:
Equation. S_t
the true state.
Then deliberately generate evidence with known properties:
accurate observation
blurred observation
corrupted observation
contradictory observation
redundant observation
missing observation
OOD observation
old observation
multi-sensor agreement
multi-sensor disagreement
And train Model 2 to recover:
Equation. hat S_t~= S_t.
Public datasets provide the real-data side.
Training objectives can eventually include:
Equation. mathcal L= lambda_sL_state +lambda_uL_uncertainty +lambda_tL_temporal +lambda_cL_consistency +lambda_pL_prediction +ldots
We shouldn’t lock those losses yet.
## How we validate whether BUO is actually worthwhile
This is critical for Conrad and eventually a paper.
Compare against:
Latest observation only
Simple averaging
GRU/LSTM memory
Transformer history
state-space model
standard attention memory
Bayesian/filtering baseline where appropriate
Then progressively remove our components:
Full BUO
− novelty mechanism
− contradiction memory
− trust gate
− innovation gate
− temporal handling
− explicit uncertainty
− persistent belief
If removing a proposed proprietary mechanism doesn’t hurt relevant performance, we don’t get to claim that mechanism matters.
The strongest experiment might be something like:
As observations become increasingly sparse, noisy, redundant and contradictory, how well does each architecture reconstruct the true evolving hidden state while remaining calibrated about its uncertainty?
That’s almost perfectly aligned with the actual underwater problem.
# 6. Uncertainty: Decomposition, Supervision and Calibration
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 8a2f4b20. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Model 2 should know why it is uncertain
I would explicitly separate at least four uncertainty sources:
Equation. boxedU_i=(U_A,U_E,U_C,U_O)
Aleatoric U_A - the observation itself is noisy/ambiguous: turbidity, sonar noise, blur, etc.
Epistemic U_E - the model doesn’t know: unfamiliar structure/ecosystem/environment, OOD state, insufficient training coverage.
Contradiction U_C - credible evidence disagrees.
Observational U_O - we simply haven’t observed enough of the relevant world recently or from sufficient viewpoints.
Example:
Weld W17
State:
    likely degraded
Uncertainty:
    sensor/aleatoric       LOW
    epistemic              MEDIUM
    contradiction          HIGH
    observational          MEDIUM
That’s far more useful than:
confidence = 63%.
## Uncertainty belongs inside the belief
Each cell becomes approximately:
Equation. B_i= (z_i,U_i,tau_i,q_i,p_i)
with:
Equation. U_i= (u_iA,u_iE,u_iC,u_iO)
But these don’t necessarily have to be four scalar numbers. Internally some could be distributions, covariance structures, ensembles or learned uncertainty representations.
We should choose the exact mathematical implementation experimentally.
## Aleatoric uncertainty
This concerns the evidence.
Imagine:
Clear RGB
    ↓
low observation uncertainty
Turbid + blurred RGB
    ↓
high observation uncertainty
The evidence encoder should output both:
Equation. e_k
and an estimate of observation uncertainty:
Equation. u^A_k.
So:
Equation. Encoder(O_k)->(e_k,u^A_k)
The BUO Trust Gate receives both.
Importantly, this can be modality-specific.
A terrible RGB frame doesn’t imply sonar is terrible.
## Epistemic uncertainty
This answers:
Has the model encountered anything sufficiently similar to this?
Suppose 2T was trained mainly on pipelines and suddenly encounters an unusual subsea mechanical assembly.
It should ideally say:
I can process this observation
BUT
my learned model is outside familiar territory.
Potential implementations we’ll test include ensembles, approximate Bayesian approaches, latent-density/OOD methods, evidential methods, disagreement between predictors, etc.
I would not lock us into one yet.
The architectural requirement is:
Model ignorance must be measurable separately from sensor noise.
## Contradiction uncertainty
This one is particularly Model-2-specific.
Suppose:
Equation. E_1=> S=A
while:
Equation. E_2=> S=B.
If both are credible:
Evidence A ──┐
             ├──► CONTRADICTION STATE
Evidence B ──┘
Don’t immediately average them.
Maintain contradiction:
Equation. u_iC=f(E_isupport,E_i^conflict)
and preserve the conflicting evidence in provenance memory.
This integrates directly with BUO.
## Observational uncertainty
This answers:
How much relevant evidence do I actually have?
This is different from epistemic uncertainty.
Suppose the model understands pipelines extremely well but has only seen 5% of this particular pipeline.
That’s not:
“the neural network doesn’t understand pipelines.”
It’s:
“we haven’t inspected enough of this one.”
2S especially needs this distinction.
We can derive it from things such as:
coverage
viewpoint diversity
observation age
number of independent observations
sensor diversity
occlusion
unseen regions
This will later connect directly to Active Intelligence.
## Uncertainty flows through BUO
Recall:
NEW EVIDENCE
      ↓
BUO
Now BUO receives:
Equation. (E_k,U^A_k)
plus existing:
Equation. U_i.
Examples:
### Reliable supporting evidence
U_A low
evidence supports belief
independent viewpoint
        ↓
U_C ↓
U_O ↓
overall belief sharpens
### Bad observation
U_A high
        ↓
little state update
U_O may remain high
### Strong contradiction
U_A low
evidence conflicts
        ↓
U_C ↑
state potentially changes
Exactly the behavior we wanted earlier.
## Uncertainty flows through RBP
Suppose:
B1 ───── B2
and B1 is highly uncertain.
RBP must not propagate B1’s latent information as though it were fact.
So messages become:
Equation. M_ij=(m_ij,U_ij^msg)
and the receiving belief can distinguish:
direct high-quality evidence
vs
uncertain relational inference.
That’s crucial.
## Uncertainty flows through TBD
TBD predicts:
Equation. P(S_t+Delta t|B_t)
rather than only:
Equation. hat S_t+Delta t.
As prediction horizon grows, dynamics uncertainty generally changes.
Therefore:
CURRENT BELIEF
      ↓
Temporal Dynamics
      ↓
Future belief
+
future uncertainty
Then when reality is observed again, temporal surprise tells us whether our dynamics model was appropriately calibrated.
## The model should be allowed to say “I don’t know”
This should be a fundamental output state.
For example:
Equation. U_E>theta_E
or
Equation. U_O>theta_O
could eventually produce:
INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE
rather than forcing a confident prediction.
Model 1 can then decide whether that uncertainty matters enough to request more information.
That creates:
Model 2
"I don't know this."
      ↓
Model 1
"Does knowing it matter?"
      ↓
Active Intelligence
"What observation should we collect?"
Very clean architecture.
## Training the uncertainty system
This is where our twins become extremely valuable.
We can deliberately generate controlled situations:
Case A
clean familiar observation
Case B
same state + severe sensor noise
Case C
unseen/OOD world configuration
Case D
two credible contradictory observations
Case E
only 5% world coverage
Case F
90% coverage from diverse sensors
Case G
very long observation gap
Because we control the generation process, we know why information is missing or unreliable.
This gives us supervised/weakly-supervised signals for disentangling the uncertainty channels.
Public real data then tests whether those behaviors transfer outside the simulator.
## Validation
Calibration becomes just as important as state accuracy.
We’ll use appropriate metrics such as calibration error, NLL/Brier-type probabilistic scores where applicable, coverage-vs-error curves, selective prediction/risk-coverage, OOD detection metrics and child-specific probabilistic metrics.
But the really important experiments are behavioral.
For example:
Equation. sensor noiseuparrow => U_Auparrow
while ideally not unnecessarily increasing:
Equation. U_E.
Similarly:
Equation. world noveltyuparrow => U_Euparrow
and:
Equation. coveragedownarrow => U_Ouparrow.
That tests whether our decomposition actually means anything.
## Baselines and ablations
Compare:
single confidence score
standard predictive variance
ensemble uncertainty
our decomposed uncertainty architecture
Then:
Full system
− aleatoric channel
− epistemic channel
− contradiction channel
− observational channel
If decomposing uncertainty doesn’t improve calibration, decision utility, OOD behavior or Active Intelligence later, we shouldn’t claim it as a contribution.
## Model 2 Core now looks like this
OBSERVATION
                          │
                          ▼
                 Evidence Encoding
                          │
                    evidence + UA
                          │
                          ▼
                ┌──────────────────┐
                │       BUO        │
                │ belief updating  │
                └────────┬─────────┘
                         ▼
                ┌──────────────────┐
                │       RBP        │
                │ relationships    │
                └────────┬─────────┘
                         ▼
                CURRENT BELIEF
                         │
             ┌───────────┴───────────┐
             │                       │
             ▼                       ▼
      UNCERTAINTY STATE             TBD
      UA UE UC UO              temporal dynamics
             │                       │
             └───────────┬───────────┘
                         ▼
                 PERSISTENT BELIEF
We’re getting close to completing Model 2 Core.
The next original-flow task should be Persistent Memory + Belief Lifecycle: how belief cells are created, identified, stored, merged, split, retired, retrieved, and connected to the external evidence/provenance store. That’s what turns these equations into an architecture capable of maintaining a world over months or years.
# 7. Temporal Belief Dynamics
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 5f8d2c40. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## The fundamental problem
Suppose we inspect something at t_0:
t₀                           t₁
│                             │
OBSERVED                  OBSERVED
│                             │
▼                             ▼
B(t₀) ───── ???????? ───── B(t₁)
A conventional inspection database essentially leaves ???????? empty.
Model 2 shouldn’t.
It should maintain:
Equation. P(S_t+Delta tmid B_t,C_t:t+Delta t)
Importantly, that is a prediction, not an observation.
## Three different kinds of state
I think every belief cell should distinguish:
### Observed state
Supported by direct evidence.
### Inferred state
Produced through relationships/other evidence.
### Predicted state
Produced through temporal dynamics.
Conceptually:
BELIEF CELL
Observed evidence
       ↓
Current inferred belief
       ↓
Temporal model
       ↓
Predicted future belief
And provenance must preserve these distinctions.
Model 1 can therefore know:
“This state estimate is mostly based on a six-month prediction rather than recent observation.”
Very useful.
## Learned latent dynamics
Each belief has latent state:
Equation. z_i(t)
We introduce a dynamics operator:
Equation. hat z_i(t+Delta t) = D_theta ( z_i(t), C_i(t:t+Delta t), Delta t )
where C contains domain context.
But the dynamics architecture should not be identical for T/E/S.
Model 2 Core defines the interface.
The children implement the actual dynamics.
## Uncertainty must grow when appropriate
This is crucial.
Suppose 2T hasn’t observed a component for one year.
Even if its best estimate remains:
Equation. hat z_t+1year
we should generally expect uncertainty to evolve:
Equation. u_t+Delta t = U_theta(u_t,Delta t,D,C).
Conceptually:
last observation
certainty
██████████
+ 1 month
████████░░
+ 6 months
██████░░░░
+ 2 years
████░░░░░░
Not necessarily linearly.
And not universally.
A static object in 2S may retain certainty much longer than a dynamic ecological state in 2E.
## But uncertainty ≠ temporal decay
We should not simply implement:
Equation. u=u_0+kDelta t.
Instead uncertainty growth should depend on predicted dynamics.
If:
Equation. Var(S_t+Delta t|S_t)
is low, uncertainty barely increases.
If dynamics are chaotic/highly variable, it increases rapidly.
So Model 2 learns:
How predictable is this part of reality over this time horizon?
That’s much more meaningful.
## Event-conditioned dynamics
Time alone isn’t enough.
Imagine 2T:
Pipeline state
     +
12 months
versus:
Pipeline state
     +
12 months
     +
high-current environment
     +
loading events
Those should produce different trajectories.
Therefore:
Equation. D_theta(B_t,C_t,Delta t)
where context can include external/environmental variables.
This is particularly important for 2E.
## Multi-hypothesis futures
I don’t want Model 2 to always predict one future.
Sometimes several futures are plausible.
Instead:
Equation. P(S_t+kmid B_t)
could be multimodal.
Conceptually:
Current belief
                       │
              ┌────────┼────────┐
              ▼        ▼        ▼
           Future A Future B Future C
             55%      30%      15%
We don’t necessarily need explicit three-branch outputs; distributions/ensembles/latent stochastic dynamics may represent this.
But the architecture should support multiple plausible futures rather than averaging them into an unrealistic middle.
## New observations correct prediction
This is where TBD connects beautifully to BUO.
Suppose:
t₀
Observation
   ↓
Belief
   ↓
TBD
   ↓
predicted t₁ belief
   ↓
NEW OBSERVATION AT t₁
   ↓
BUO
   ↓
corrected t₁ belief
Mathematically:
### Prediction
Equation. B_t+1^-=D_theta(B_t)
### Correction
Equation. B_t+1^+ = U_theta(B_t+1^-,E_t+1)
This resembles the classical predict → correct philosophy of state estimation, but we’re designing it for learned structured persistent world beliefs.
That’s also something we’ll need to compare against classical filtering/state-space methods.
## Model 2T temporal dynamics
For 2T:
Equation. D_T
models structural/degradation evolution.
Inputs could include:
component state
component type
material
structural relationships
environment
loading context
inspection history
Δt
Output:
predicted structural state
+
uncertainty distribution
Twin 2T becomes especially important here because it can generate complete:
Equation. S_0,S_1,ldots,S_n
degradation trajectories.
Public longitudinal structural-health/degradation datasets provide real validation where available.
## Model 2E temporal dynamics
Probably the most complex.
Equation. D_E
needs interaction between:
Equation. entities/populations <-> environmental fields.
So:
Eco state(t)
     +
Environmental field(t)
     +
Disturbances
     ↓
Coupled Entity-Field Dynamics
     ↓
Eco state(t+Δt)
     +
Environment(t+Δt)
Twin 2E gives us controlled temporal ecosystem histories.
Existing ecological/oceanographic time series provide external validation.
## Model 2S temporal dynamics
2S is different again.
Much of the world is approximately static.
So 2S must distinguish:
static vs dynamic world state
For example:
seabed → mostly static
pipeline → mostly static geometry
fish → dynamic
sediment → potentially dynamic
robot/object → dynamic
temporary obstruction → dynamic
This means 2S shouldn’t gradually “forget” static geometry simply because time passes.
Its dynamics operator learns/estimates state persistence.
That’s potentially an important feature of UAHSM.
## Temporal surprise
Here’s another mechanism worth testing.
When the next real observation arrives, compare:
Equation. hat B_t
with what was actually observed.
Define a temporal innovation/surprise signal:
Equation. delta_t = d(E_t,hat B_t).
Large surprise means:
The world evolved differently from our model.
That can trigger:
belief correction,
increased uncertainty,
anomaly/change detection,
dynamics-model learning,
possible Active Intelligence request.
So:
PREDICT
   ↓
OBSERVE
   ↓
COMPARE
   ↓
SURPRISE
   ↓
┌────────┬───────────┬────────────┐
▼        ▼           ▼            ▼
update  anomaly   uncertainty   learn
belief            adjustment    dynamics
I particularly like this because BUO’s innovation mechanism and TBD’s temporal prediction become connected rather than independent algorithms.
## Training without our own data
The twins become ideal temporal teachers.
Generate:
S₀ → S₁ → S₂ → ... → Sₙ
but only reveal:
O₀      O₂           O₅
│       │            │
▼       ▼            ▼
Model 2 has to infer everything between them.
We can randomize:
observation interval
missing observations
environmental events
rate of change
sensor quality
disturbances
unseen transitions
Then supplement with public real longitudinal datasets.
## Validation
The key TBD experiments should include:
Forecasting: How accurately does it predict hidden future state?
Long-gap inference: What happens as observation gaps increase?
Calibration: Does uncertainty increase appropriately?
Change detection: Can temporal surprise detect unexpected changes?
Static preservation: Does 2S avoid unnecessarily degrading confidence in genuinely static states?
OOD dynamics: What happens under an evolution pattern absent from training?
And compare against:
no temporal model
last-state persistence
linear extrapolation
classical state-space/filtering
GRU/LSTM
Transformer temporal model
modern learned state-space model
Then ablate our custom mechanisms.
## Model 2 Core now has a proper cycle
We’re no longer designing a conventional feed-forward network.
OBSERVE
                      │
                      ▼
              ┌──────────────┐
              │     BUO      │
              │ direct update│
              └──────┬───────┘
                     ▼
              ┌──────────────┐
              │     RBP      │
              │ relationships│
              └──────┬───────┘
                     ▼
              CURRENT BELIEF
