Multi-timescale dynamics
2E-DATA-01
Ecological adapters
2E-DATA-02
Environmental time-series adapters
2E-TRAIN-01
Training curriculum
2E-EVAL-01
Benchmark suite
## Completion gates
### 2E-D1
Entity representation works on real ecological data.
### D2
Environmental field representation/dynamics works.
### D3
Persistent ecological beliefs outperform latest-only under sparse observations.
### D4
Uncertainty responds appropriately to missing/noisy/OOD evidence.
### D5
CEFD provides measurable coupling benefit without unacceptable unsupported inference.
### D6
Multi-scale representation earns its complexity.
### D7
Twin2E pretraining improves at least defined held-out real tasks.
### D8
Twin2E → Unity → ECMER → Model2E operates without truth leakage.
## What is frozen
Architecturally:
heterogeneous entity + field world
multi-timescale
partial observation
explicit coupling rather than naïve fusion
Twin truth / Model belief separation
2S owns canonical geometry
## What remains research hypothesis
Equation. boxedCEFD
Equation. boxedMEIFE
factorized ecological latent
bidirectional entity-field coupling
multi-scale representation
All must earn their place experimentally.
## One data issue still to lock
Our ecological imagery side is already strong.
The remaining 2E data job is specifically:
environmental field + longitudinal oceanographic datasets
with usable combinations of:
location;
depth;
time;
temperature;
salinity;
oxygen;
current;
turbidity/light where available.
That’s much narrower than our earlier “find 2E data” problem.
# 13. Twin2E: Ecological and Environmental World Design
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source c8e21db3. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Fundamental architecture
I propose five major layers:
TWIN 2E
       Habitat / Spatial Layer
                │
                ▼
       Environmental Fields
                │
                ↕
       Ecological Entities
                │
                ↕
      Interaction / Dynamics
                │
                ▼
          Hidden State
                │
        ┌───────┴───────┐
        ▼               ▼
   Ground Truth     Observation Model
                         │
                         ▼
                      Model 2E
Formally:
Equation. S_t^Twin2E = (H,Z_t,Phi_t,R_t,Theta)
where H is habitat structure, Z ecological state, Phi environmental fields, R ecological relationships and Theta scenario parameters.
## Habitat layer
Every ecosystem needs somewhere to exist.
Twin 2E should generate/import:
seafloor geometry
substrate type
depth
habitat regions
structural surfaces
sheltered/exposed areas
Potential habitat categories depend on our eventual scope.
This should ultimately share geometry with Twin 2S rather than generating contradictory worlds.
For independent early training, Twin 2E can use its own simplified spatial grid/mesh.
## Environmental field engine
Represent environmental variables as spatial-temporal fields:
Equation. Phi(x,t)= [ T(x,t), S(x,t), V(x,t), Q(x,t),… ].
Potential variables:
temperature
salinity
current/flow
turbidity
oxygen
light
nutrient/chemical variables
Only include variables for which we can provide scientific grounding.
Field evolution:
Equation. Phi_t+Delta t = F_Phi(Phi_t,B_t,C_t,Delta t) + epsilon_Phi.
Here B_t can represent boundary/external forcing.
## Real environmental conditioning
A very important improvement over purely invented environments:
Twin 2E should be able to ingest public real oceanographic time series/field data.
Conceptually:
PUBLIC OCEAN DATA
      │
      ▼
environmental initialization
      +
boundary conditions
      +
statistical calibration
      │
      ▼
TWIN 2E FIELD ENGINE
So synthetic ecosystems can exist under environmental regimes grounded in actual measurements.
## Ecological entity engine
Represent ecological state at several levels:
individual
colony
population
community
habitat patch
Not every simulation needs individuals.
For large-scale training:
Equation. Z_i(t)
may represent a population or ecological patch.
For local high-resolution scenarios, it could represent organisms/colonies.
This gives us scalable fidelity.
## Entity state
For ecological entity i:
Equation. Z_i= [ type_i, x_i, n_i, c_i, h_i, a_i ]
conceptually:
ecological type/identity;
spatial distribution;
abundance/coverage;
condition;
habitat association;
activity/state.
Again, the exact variables depend on ecological domain.
## Ecological relationships
Twin 2E maintains ground-truth simulation relationships such as:
occupies
associated_with
competes_with
predates_on
depends_on
exposed_to
part_of
But the simulator must distinguish:
mechanistic relationship
from:
Equation. boxedstatistical correlation.
Only scientifically justified interactions should directly drive dynamics.
## Twin 2E’s candidate proprietary mechanism
For 2T we proposed MCDE.
For 2E, I think the strongest candidate is a Multi-Scale Ecological Interaction Field Engine.
Temporary name:
MEIFE
The research hypothesis:
Ecological synthetic worlds become more useful and realistic when discrete ecological agents/populations and continuous environmental fields evolve jointly across multiple spatial and temporal scales rather than being simulated independently.
## MEIFE architecture
Two coupled states:
Equation. Z_t
and:
Equation. Phi_t.
Evolution:
Equation. Z_t+Delta t = F_Z(Z_t,Phi_t,R_t,H,Delta t) +epsilon_Z
Equation. Phi_t+Delta t = F_Phi(Phi_t,Z_t,H,C_t,Delta t) +epsilon_Phi.
But now introduce multiple scales:
Equation. Z_tlocal,Z_tpopulation,Z_t^community
and:
Equation. Phi_tlocal,Phi_tregional.
So information can move:
REGIONAL ENVIRONMENT
        ↓
LOCAL ENVIRONMENT
        ↓
POPULATIONS
        ↓
LOCAL ENTITIES
and selected justified effects
can propagate upward again.
This aligns well with 2E’s hierarchical memory.
## Hybrid mechanistic + stochastic architecture
We absolutely should not make MEIFE one giant neural network.
Use:
Equation. boxed F= F_mechanistic + F_statistical + F_learned-residual
where appropriate.
For example:
known physical field process
        +
ecological mechanistic model
        +
stochastic variation
        +
optional learned residual
Learned residuals should only be introduced when public real data supports them.
## Disturbances
Twin 2E should support controlled ecological/environmental events.
Examples:
temperature anomaly
turbidity spike
pollution-like disturbance
physical habitat disturbance
current regime change
light reduction
population disturbance
invasive/new entity introduction
These allow us to train Model 2E on transitions rather than only equilibrium states.
## Recovery
Again, change should not mean:
everything gets progressively worse.
Twin 2E needs:
disturbance
     ↓
impact
     ↓
adaptation / recovery / continued decline
depending on scenario dynamics.
This allows 2E to distinguish trajectories.
## Stochasticity
Two identical environments should not necessarily evolve identically.
Use:
Equation. Z_t+Delta t = F_Z(…) + epsilon_Z
with scientifically plausible stochastic processes.
This gives Model 2E distributions over futures rather than deterministic rules.
## Observation generation
Hidden ecological state becomes sensor-visible evidence:
Equation. O_t^m = H_m(S_t,V_t,eta_m).
Possible observations:
RGB imagery
video
sonar
environmental sensor readings
depth
habitat geometry
Again, Twin 2E doesn’t need to own photorealistic rendering permanently.
Unity/2S can eventually supply spatial viewpoint/rendering.
## Observation fidelity levels
Same useful curriculum as Twin 2T.
### Level 1 - abstract
true coral coverage = 0.43
observed estimate =
0.43 + measurement noise
### Level 2 - feature-level
Generate state-conditioned ecological/environmental features.
### Level 3 - sensor-level
RGB/sonar/environmental observations generated through higher-fidelity sensor models and eventually Unity.
This lets us train belief reasoning before the full graphics stack exists.
## Partial ecological surveys
Twin 2E deliberately hides most of reality.
Example:
TRUE ECOSYSTEM
████████████████████████████████
ROBOT OBSERVES
███       ████              ██
Generate different:
survey paths
coverage percentages
viewpoints
sensor combinations
sampling intervals
Model 2E must reconstruct belief without pretending unseen regions were observed.
## Environmental sparsity
Environmental sensors are also sparse.
The twin knows:
Equation. Phi(x,t)
everywhere.
Model 2E may receive only:
Equation. Phi(x_1,t_1),Phi(x_2,t_2),…
This lets us train environmental-field inference under realistic sparse sensing.
## Deliberate confounders
This is especially important.
Suppose ecological condition and temperature are correlated in training.
If every scenario obeys:
high temperature → poor condition
Model 2E could learn a fake shortcut.
So procedural generation should deliberately include:
high temperature + stable ecology
normal temperature + disturbed ecology
multiple interacting causes
same ecological outcome from different contexts
where scientifically plausible.
This helps prevent simplistic causal hallucination.
## Dataset generated by Twin 2E
Each scenario could produce:
scenario/
│
├── habitat/
│
├── environmental_fields/
│   ├── temperature
│   ├── current
│   └── ...
│
├── ecological_truth/
│   ├── entities
│   ├── populations
│   └── habitats
│
├── relationships/
│
├── disturbances/
├── recovery/
│
├── observations/
│   ├── RGB
│   ├── sonar
│   └── sensors
│
├── visibility/
├── observation_quality/
│
└── provenance/
Everything is temporally synchronized.
## Training Model 2E from it
Twin generates:
Equation. (Z_0,Phi_0),…,(Z_T,Phi_T).
But Model 2E gets:
Equation. O_0, ?, O_2, ?, ?,O_5.
This directly trains:
BUO-E;
CEFD;
TBD-E;
uncertainty;
PMBL;
entity/field association;
partial-observation reasoning.
## How Twin 2E itself gets calibrated
Three data sources:
### Published mechanistic models
For known ecological/environmental processes.
### Public observational datasets
Estimate distributions, parameter ranges and environmental regimes.
### Public longitudinal data
Compare simulated trajectories against observed temporal patterns.
No self-collected dataset required.
## Twin validation - environmental fields
For Phi, compare simulated:
means
variance
spatial correlation
temporal autocorrelation
extremes
gradients
against appropriate public oceanographic observations.
This tests whether the environment is statistically plausible.
## Twin validation - ecological dynamics
For Z, compare where data permits:
population distributions
coverage distributions
temporal change
response to environmental conditions
disturbance/recovery patterns
spatial clustering
against published ecological observations/models.
We should be explicit about what has and hasn’t been validated.
## Interaction validation
For MEIFE, compare:
Equation. independent entity simulator + field simulator
against:
Equation. coupled MEIFE.
Does coupling improve agreement with held-out real temporal ecological data?
If not, we simplify.
## Downstream validation
Potentially the most important test:
### Train 2E using:
Equation. Twin 2E.
### Evaluate on:
Equation. held-out public real ecosystem data.
Then compare different twin configurations.
If MEIFE produces visually/scientifically impressive simulations but doesn’t improve real-world transfer, its value as a training twin is questionable.
## Reality-gap analysis
We should quantify three gaps separately:
Equation. D_environment
Equation. D_ecology
Equation. D_sensor.
That tells us whether failure comes from:
bad environmental dynamics;
bad ecological dynamics;
unrealistic observation generation.
Much more actionable than one vague “sim-to-real gap.”
## Twin 2E baselines
Compare:
random ecological generator
static habitat generator
independent entity dynamics
independent entity + environmental field dynamics
mechanistic ecological simulator
mechanistic + stochastic
MEIFE
And ablate:
− entity↔field coupling
− multi-scale dynamics
− real environmental conditioning
− disturbances
− stochasticity
− recovery
− learned residual
## Shared-world integration later
Eventually:
SHARED SCENARIO
                  │
       ┌──────────┼──────────┐
       ▼          ▼          ▼
   Twin 2T    Twin 2E    Twin 2S
 structural   ecology     spatial
    truth       truth       truth
       └──────────┼──────────┘
                  ▼
               Unity V2
                  │
                  ▼
             observations
For example, Twin 2S might define:
there is a pipeline surface at coordinates x,y,z.
Twin 2T defines:
that surface has 18% material loss.
Twin 2E defines:
that surface supports a particular ecological community and exists under environmental field Phi.
One physical world; three domain truths.
## What we should NOT claim
Even after implementation:
“Twin 2E accurately simulates marine ecosystems”
would require enormous evidence.
A defensible statement would be closer to:
Twin 2E is a controlled synthetic ecological/environmental world generator whose individual mechanisms are calibrated and evaluated against selected public observations and published models.
Then report actual validation results.
That scientific discipline will make the project stronger, not weaker.
### Model 2E ↔ Twin 2E pair
We now have:
Equation. boxed Twin 2E: (Z_ttrue,Phi_ttrue)
Equation. downarrow partial multimodal observations
Equation. boxed Model 2E: P(Z_t,Phi_tmid O_1:t)
with the paired research hypotheses:
CEFD - Model 2E
learned persistent entity↔field belief dynamics,
and:
MEIFE - Twin 2E
multi-scale scientifically constrained entity↔field synthetic world dynamics.
We now have T and E complete at architecture level.
# 14. Model 2S and Twin2S: Full Implementation Specification
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 47895faf. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Exact target
Twin 2S owns:
Equation. S_t^S,true = (G_t,O_t,M_t,D_t)
representing true geometry, occupancy, semantic/world structure and dynamics.
Twin 2S additionally owns:
Equation. boxedmathcal V_t=observability truth
Model 2S estimates:
Equation. boxed B_t^S ~= P(S_t^S,truemid O_1:t,X_1:t^robot)
without access to truth.
## Three separate spatial objects
Never collapse these:
### World truth
Equation. W^true
what actually exists.
### Observability truth
Equation. V^true
what the robot could have observed.
### Belief
Equation. B^S
what Model2S thinks exists.
This separation is central to the entire research design.
## Canonical truth representation
Twin2S should not use UAHSM as its ground-truth format.
Otherwise evaluation becomes biased toward our own representation.
Use high-resolution canonical geometry:
mesh / primitives
object transforms
collision geometry
semantic IDs
material/surface IDs
dynamic entities
Then derive occupancy, depth, point clouds, sonar, etc.
## World entity
Each spatially meaningful object uses our shared:
WorldEntity:
    world_entity_id
    entity_type
    geometry_ref
    frame_id
    parent_id
    domain_ownership
Thus the same:
PipelineSegment17
is referenced by 2S, 2T and potentially 2E.
## Twin2S truth API
class Twin2S:
    def initialize(self, scenario): ...
    def step(self, dt, events): ...
    def get_truth(self, timestamp): ...
    def visibility(
        self,
        sensor,
        pose,
        timestamp
    ): ...
    def generate_observation(
        self,
        sensor,
        pose,
        timestamp
    ): ...
Again:
get_truth()
is forbidden from inference/deployment code.
## OCPWE
Twin2S candidate mechanism:
Observability-Controlled Procedural World Engine
Its job isn’t merely:
randomly generate underwater maps.
It jointly generates:
Equation. boxedworld+trajectory+information availability
so we can deliberately control what the robot can know.
## Procedural world schema
SpatialScenario:
    world_bounds
    terrain
    infrastructure
    obstacles
    debris
    biological_geometry
    dynamic_entities
    environmental_context
    robot_spawn
    mission_regions
## Procedural infrastructure
This deserves strong support because infrastructure inspection is the target mission.
Generators:
pipeline
pipeline network
joint
weld
support
frame
platform member
cable
anchor
foundation
Parameters:
diameter
length
curvature
burial
orientation
support spacing
joint spacing
surface complexity
Twin2T attaches technical state to these entities.
## Seafloor generation
Generate:
Equation. z=f(x,y)
using combinations of:
smooth terrain
slopes
ridges
sediment
rocks
trenches
local roughness
But world families must be held out during OOD tests.
## Occluders
Deliberately generate:
rocks
terrain
infrastructure
marine growth
debris
other structures
because observability is meaningless without occlusion.
## Dynamic objects
Support:
moving marine entities
floating debris
moving equipment
temporary obstacles
and persistent environmental changes.
Model2S must distinguish:
Equation. static
from:
Equation. dynamic/change.
## Canonical geometry resolution
Truth resolution must be substantially finer than the model’s ordinary working representation.
If 2S uses:
Equation. 20cm
cells, Twin truth shouldn’t also magically be 20 cm.
Otherwise sub-cell uncertainty disappears.
## Observability engine
For world point/region x:
Equation. V(x,s,p,t)
depends on:
Equation. sensor, pose, orientation, range, FOV, occlusion, environment.
Return more than Boolean visibility.
## Observability score
Conceptually:
Equation. Vin[0,1].
Potential factors:
Equation. V= f( LOS, range, incidence, sensor, environment, resolution ).
Do not lock exact multiplicative formula.
Store factor decomposition so we know why observability was poor.
## Visibility mask
For candidate observation a:
Equation. M_vis^a(x)in[0,1].
Twin2S can compute exact synthetic:
visible
occluded
out of FOV
out of range
poor incidence
sensor-limited
environment-limited
This is gold-standard supervision for U_O.
## Cumulative observability
After observations:
Equation. V_1:t(x).
But don’t simply sum visibility.
Store:
observation count
best quality
viewpoint diversity
sensor diversity
last observation
cumulative coverage
Repeated identical views shouldn’t count as ten independent observations.
## Viewpoint diversity
For region x:
Equation. D_V(x) = f( v_1,hat v_2,ldots ).
This becomes useful for determining whether geometry is genuinely constrained.
## Three-mask ground truth
OCPWE should explicitly produce:
Equation. M_observed
Equation. M_inferable
Equation. M_unknown.
This is one of our most important 2S contributions.
## Observed
A region is OBSERVED if sufficient direct sensor evidence exists under predefined sensor-specific conditions.
It doesn’t mean:
one ray touched it.
Quality thresholds matter.
## Unknown
UNKNOWN means available evidence cannot adequately constrain the state.
This must be a valid output.
## The difficult category: inferable
We must not arbitrarily label:
hidden but nearby = inferable.
Instead use counterfactual ambiguity.
## Counterfactual worlds
Construct:
Equation. W_A!= W_B
but:
Equation. P(O_1:tmid W_A) ~= P(O_1:tmid W_B).
If current observations cannot discriminate them, the differing region should remain:
Equation. boxedUNKNOWN
or multi-hypothesis.
This is extremely powerful.
## Example
Robot sees front half of pipe.
Behind it:
### World A
pipe continues normally.
### World B
pipe bends sharply.
Current observation:
Equation. O_A~= O_B.
Therefore 2S must not confidently fill the hidden geometry simply because continuation is statistically likely.
It may output:
likely continuation
status = INFERRED
high UO
multiple hypotheses
or UNKNOWN depending evidence.
## Discriminating viewpoint
OCPWE can know which future observation a^* distinguishes:
Equation. W_A,W_B.
This gives exact supervision for Active Intelligence later.
That’s why Twin2S and MCBR are deeply connected.
## Model 2S representation
UAHSM:
Uncertainty-Aware Hierarchical Spatial Memory
needs three scales:
Equation. mathcal S_t = ( S_t^global, S_t^regional, S_t^local ).
## Global level
Contains:
major regions
infrastructure topology
large obstacles
mission connectivity
coarse occupancy
Low resolution, large coverage.
Used heavily by global navigation.
## Regional level
Contains:
structure geometry
local topology
semantic regions
coverage
uncertainty
Main persistent map.
## Local level
High-resolution temporary/active working memory around:
robot
inspection target
high-uncertainty region
Used for:
local navigation;
detailed inspection;
sensor fusion.
## Spatial cell belief
Conceptually:
Equation. B_j^S= [ z_j^geom, z_j^occ, z_j^sem, z_j^dyn, U_j, V_j ].
Starting latent hypothesis:
Equation. D_S=256.
Example:
Equation. 96+32+64+64=256.
Again configurable.
## Explicit spatial heads
From latent:
occupancy probability
surface/geometry
semantic state
dynamic probability
change probability
knowledge status
UA
UE
UC
UO
coverage
observation age
Not everything stays hidden.
## Representation technology
This is intentionally not frozen.
Candidates:
voxel grid;
sparse voxel/octree;
point-based memory;
Gaussian primitives;
occupancy/SDF;
neural implicit field;
hybrid.
For V1 implementation:
sparse voxel/octree-like baseline
is sensible because it gives us explicit spatial cells, occupancy and hierarchy without needing to solve neural-field research first.
## Why not immediately NeRF/Gaussian splatting?
Because our research question is not primarily photorealistic reconstruction.
We care about:
Equation. geometry+occupancy+knowledge state +uncertainty+persistence+navigation usefulness
A beautiful hallucinated surface is actively dangerous.
Neural fields remain a baseline/candidate later.
## Adaptive resolution
Resolution:
Equation. r(x) = f( geometry, uncertainty, mission relevance, observation density ).
High detail where:
inspection target
geometry complex
uncertainty high
mission important
Lower detail elsewhere.
## Refinement operation
Cell:
Equation. C
can split:
Equation. C->_1,ldots,C_8

for octree-style representation.
Refinement triggers should initially be deterministic.
Later learned scoring can be compared.
## Coarsening
Inactive regions can merge.
But coarsening must preserve:
uncertainty
coverage
provenance refs
important geometry
Don’t destroy inspection history just to save memory.
## Spatial evidence
ECMER-S outputs:
SpatialEvidence:
    embedding
    timestamp
    sensor_origin
    spatial_support
    pose_estimate
    pose_uncertainty
    measurement_uncertainty
    reliability
    modality
Critical distinction:
Equation. U_pose!= U_measurement.
## Pose uncertainty
Suppose sonar return is precise but robot pose is uncertain.
The return should smear spatially.
Model2S must not treat it as a precise world coordinate.
Therefore spatial update consumes:
Equation. P(x_worldmid measurement,pose).
Initially approximate covariance propagation is fine.
## UAHSM direct update
For spatial evidence E_k:
transform into world belief coordinates;
determine affected cells;
account for pose uncertainty;
update direct evidence;
update coverage/observability;
preserve provenance.
This is spatial specialization of BUO.
## Free-space evidence
Very important.
Sensor ray does not only tell us:
object at endpoint.
It may provide:
free-space evidence along the ray
depending modality.
2S must explicitly model free-space observations for navigation.
## Unknown ≠ occupied
Navigation cannot interpret unknown space as:
Equation. P(occupied)=1
inside the map representation.
Instead:
occupancy estimate
+
knowledge status
+
uncertainty
are separate.
Safety policy decides how conservatively to treat UNKNOWN.
## Spatial relational inference
UAHSM may infer hidden structure using:
continuity
surface geometry
known topology
object identity
structural priors
But inferred cells receive:
status = INFERRED
and provenance.
Never OBSERVED.
## Multi-hypothesis spatial state
For ambiguous region:
Equation. mathcal H_x=  (H_1,p_1), (H_2,p_2), … .
We don’t need this for every voxel.
Use only where ambiguity matters.
This is especially useful for:
occluded continuation;
ambiguous sonar;
incomplete structure.
## Spatial TBD
Predict:
Equation. B_t^S -> hat B_t+Delta t^S.
Static geometry should generally persist.
Dynamic entities:
Equation. x_t+Delta t = F_dyn(x_t,Delta t,C).
Unknown dynamic state increases uncertainty over time.
## Change detection
Observation conflicts with persistent geometry:
Equation. delta(x) = d( O_t(x), hat B_t^S(x) ).
Possible explanations:
sensor noise
pose error
dynamic object
persistent world change
previous belief wrong
Don’t immediately overwrite the map.
This is another BUO contradiction problem.
## Localization interface
Navigation estimator provides:
PoseEstimate:
    position
    orientation
    covariance
    timestamp
2S consumes it.
2S returns:
landmarks
map context
spatial belief
for localization.
This feedback loop needs versioned timestamps to avoid circular nonsense.
## Training Stage S1 - spatial representation
Start with synthetic full observations.
Train:
occupancy
geometry
semantics
No missing data yet.
## S2 - partial reconstruction
Mask observations.
Train:
Equation. Observed/Inferred/Unknown.
This is where OCPWE becomes essential.
## S3 - observability
Train model to estimate:
coverage
view quality
observation support
UO
against Twin2S exact observability.
## S4 - counterfactual ambiguity
Present ambiguous worlds.
Penalize confident unsupported reconstruction.
This is one of the most important training stages.
## S5 - hierarchy
Introduce:
Equation. local+regional+global.
Compare with flat representation.
## S6 - adaptive resolution
Train/test refinement policies.
Compare:
fixed high resolution
fixed low resolution
hand-engineered adaptive
learned adaptive
Measure both performance and memory/compute.
## S7 - temporal world
Add:
dynamic entities
changed obstacles
moving debris
persistent changes
Train TBD-S/change reasoning.
## S8 - pose uncertainty
Inject:
Equation. U_pose.
Test whether spatial confidence correctly degrades.
This is crucial for underwater robotics.
## S9 - modality robustness
Train with:
RGB
depth
sonar
combinations
dropout
Don’t require all sensors simultaneously.
## S10 - synthetic→real
Pretrain with OCPWE/Unity.
Adapt/evaluate using our real 2S stack:
Equation. SubPipe+AQUALOC+UVVID+sonar/etc.
Real evaluation becomes mandatory.
## 2S loss
Initial formulation:
Equation. L_2S=& lambda_G L_geometry +lambda_O L_occupancy &+lambda_S L_semantic +lambda_K L_knowledge-status &+lambda_U L_uncertainty +lambda_V L_observability &+lambda_T L_temporal +lambda_C L_consistency &+lambda_X L_unsupported.
## Knowledge-status loss
Twin provides:
Equation. y^K(x)in  OBSERVED, INFERRED, UNKNOWN .
Then:
Equation. L_K=CE(hat yK,yK).
But counterfactual cases are especially important for determining UNKNOWN.
## Unsupported-confidence loss
Define region:
Equation. Omega_U = :y^K(x)=UNKNOWN.
Penalize high-confidence geometric claims:
Equation. L_UC = frac1|Omega_U| sum_xinOmega_U ConfidenceClaim(x).
This operationalizes our earlier:
Equation. boxedUnsupported Confidence
metric.
## Main 2S metric
Equation. UC= P( high confidence spatial claim mid insufficient evidence ).
This may be more important for our architecture than simply maximizing reconstruction IoU.
## Standard metrics
Also measure:
occupancy IoU
precision/recall
surface distance
Chamfer distance where appropriate
depth error
semantic IoU
trajectory/localization error
depending representation/task.
## Coverage calibration
If 2S says:
Equation. coverage=80%
that estimate should correspond meaningfully to Twin observability truth.
Measure:
Equation. E_coverage = |hat V-V^true|.
## Hidden-region error
Separate:
Equation. E_observed
from:
Equation. E_hidden.
Otherwise performance can look great simply because visible geometry is easy.
## Ambiguity calibration
For counterfactual world pairs:
If observations cannot distinguish them:
Equation. U_Ouparrow
and confidence in differing regions should remain low.
After discriminating observation:
Equation. U_Odownarrow.
This is a beautiful controlled experiment.
## OCPWE oracle information gain
Because Twin knows truth, for candidate view a:
Equation. IG^oracle(a) = L(B_t,S^true) - L(B_t+1a,Strue).
Store:
candidate pose
visibility
new coverage
discriminated hypotheses
oracle belief improvement
This becomes training data for MCBR.
## OCPWE trajectory generation
Don’t only generate random robot paths.
Generate:
redundant trajectory
poor trajectory
coverage trajectory
information-seeking trajectory
counterfactual-discriminating trajectory
This prevents Active Intelligence training data from being dominated by one trajectory style.
## Baselines
Required:
S-B0 classical occupancy map
S-B1 classical SLAM/map representation
S-B2 dense voxel memory
S-B3 octree/sparse voxel
S-B4 learned flat spatial memory
S-B5 temporal learned spatial memory
S-B6 neural field / implicit representation
S-B7 persistent memory without uncertainty
S-B8 UAHSM without hierarchy
S-B9 UAHSM without knowledge status
S-B10 full UAHSM
Not every baseline applies to every metric, but this is the comparison family.
## OCPWE baselines
fixed hand-built worlds
random procedural worlds
procedural worlds without observability control
OCPWE without counterfactual pairs
OCPWE without trajectory control
full OCPWE
Downstream question:
Does observability-controlled generation actually produce better uncertainty/active-perception behavior?
## Core experiments
### 2S-E01 - coverage
Equation. 100,50,20,5%.
### E02 - viewpoint redundancy
### E03 - occlusion
### E04 - counterfactual ambiguity
### E05 - pose uncertainty
### E06 - sonar degradation
### E07 - RGB degradation
### E08 - modality dropout
### E09 - dynamic world
### E10 - hierarchy
### E11 - adaptive resolution
### E12 - unseen geometry
### E13 - unseen infrastructure
### E14 - synthetic→real
### E15 - navigation usefulness
### E16 - active-information usefulness
## Navigation usefulness
A spatial model can have good reconstruction metrics and still be terrible for robotics.
Therefore evaluate:
Equation. NavigationSuccess(B^S).
Compare navigation using maps from different 2S variants.
Metrics:
collision
goal success
path length
planning failures
unknown-space violations
This is essential.
## Active Intelligence usefulness
Likewise:
Equation. ActiveGain(B^S).
Can MCBR select better observations using UAHSM than using ordinary occupancy uncertainty?
This tests whether our richer spatial belief actually matters downstream.
## Memory efficiency
Hierarchy must justify itself.
Measure:
Equation. MemoryPerMappedVolume
Equation. ComputePerUpdate
Equation. Latency
against accuracy/uncertainty.
If flat sparse representation performs equally well within compute budget, hierarchy doesn’t earn its complexity.
## Twin usefulness
Equation. TU_2S = Performance_real(Twin+Real) - Performance_real(RealOnly).
Evaluate across:
reconstruction;
uncertainty;
localization/navigation;
active perception where feasible.
## Cross-domain 2T interface
Twin2S:
PipelineSegment17 geometry
Twin2T:
PipelineSegment17 degradation
Model2S estimates geometry/location.
Model2T estimates technical state.
If corrosion changes geometry sufficiently, Twin2T sends state change through shared scenario mechanics and Twin2S canonical geometry can reflect the physically justified change.
No duplicate entities.
## Cross-domain 2E interface
Twin2E may create ecological occupancy:
marine growth
coral
vegetation
with geometry registered into the shared spatial world where physically relevant.
Twin2S owns the canonical resulting spatial representation.
2E retains ecological meaning.
## Unity boundary
Very important:
### Twin2S
owns:
what geometry actually exists
### Unity
owns:
robot physics + sensor interaction/rendering
Unity instantiates Twin2S geometry.
It does not independently invent mission-world truth.
## Example full 2S loop
OCPWE
creates pipeline world
        ↓
Twin2S
canonical truth
        ↓
Unity
robot moves + camera/sonar
        ↓
ECMER
spatial evidence
        ↓
Model2S
UAHSM
        ↓
Belief:
pipe observed here
continuation inferred here
region unknown here
        ↓
MCBR
requests discriminating view
        ↓
Navigation
moves robot
        ↓
Unity
new observation
        ↓
Model2S
uncertainty resolves
That’s the exact behavior we want.

## Completion gates
### 2S-D1
Basic spatial representation reconstructs fully observed synthetic worlds.
### D2
Partial-observation system distinguishes OBSERVED/INFERRED/UNKNOWN.
### D3
Counterfactual worlds do not produce unjustified confident reconstruction.
### D4
Pose/sensor uncertainty propagates appropriately.
### D5
Persistent map handles temporal change.
### D6
Hierarchy/adaptive resolution provides measurable compute/memory-performance benefit.
### D7
Synthetic training improves defined held-out real tasks.
### D8
Navigation successfully consumes 2S beliefs.
### D9
MCBR successfully consumes observability/uncertainty.
### D10
Twin2S → Unity → ECMER → Model2S closes without truth leakage.
## What is now frozen
Architecturally:
truth / observability / belief separation
Observed / Inferred / Unknown
hierarchical persistent spatial memory
pose uncertainty separate from sensor uncertainty
counterfactual ambiguity as a training/evaluation tool
Twin2S canonical geometry; Unity interaction
## What remains experimental
Equation. boxedUAHSM
Equation. boxedOCPWE
and specifically:
sparse voxel vs other representation;
hierarchy structure;
adaptive-resolution policy;
multi-hypothesis representation;
learned spatial propagation;
exact observability estimator.
These must win experiments.
# 15. Twin2S: Observability-Controlled World Design
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 71644636. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Fundamental contract
At time t:
Equation. S_t^S,true = (G_t,O_t,M_t,D_t)
containing true:
geometry;
occupancy;
semantics/objects;
dynamic state.
For sensor configuration/viewpoint V_t:
Equation. mathcal V_t = Visibility(S_t,V_t)
defines what is physically observable.
Then:
Equation. O_t=Sensor(S_t,mathcal V_t,eta_t).
Therefore Twin 2S gives us three separate truths:
Equation. boxed WORLD TRUTH
Equation. boxed OBSERVABILITY TRUTH
Equation. boxed SENSOR OBSERVATION
This separation is extremely powerful.
## Twin architecture
TWIN 2S
        Procedural World Generator
                  │
                  ▼
            True 3D World
                  │
       ┌──────────┼──────────┐
       ▼          ▼          ▼
   Geometry    Semantics   Dynamics
       │          │          │
       └──────────┼──────────┘
                  ▼
          Visibility Engine
                  │
                  ▼
           Sensor Generator
                  │
        ┌─────────┴─────────┐
        ▼                   ▼
  Ground Truth          Observations
        │                   │
        │                   ▼
        │                 ECMER
        │                   │
        │                   ▼
        └──────────────► Model 2S
## World representation
Twin 2S needs an exact canonical representation independent of whatever representation Model 2S uses.
That’s important.
If Model 2S uses voxels, we shouldn’t make Twin 2S’s truth simply be those same voxels.
Otherwise evaluation becomes biased toward our architecture.
Twin truth could be represented using:
meshes
analytic geometry
object poses
semantic scene graph
continuous surfaces
and rasterized/converted into whatever evaluation representation is needed.
## Procedural underwater world generator
We cannot train on one static scene.
Define:
Equation. Wsim P(Theta_W).
Generate:
seafloor
terrain
rocks
sediment
infrastructure
pipes
supports
frames
debris
biological structures
open water
obstacles
with randomized but constrained geometry.
Eventually these worlds come from the shared scenario schema used by Twins 2T/E/S.
## Infrastructure generation
For Conrad, infrastructure should be particularly strong.
Procedurally generate:
pipelines
pipe bends
junctions
welds
supports
risers
frames
platform-like structures
cables
inspection targets
with randomized:
dimensions;
orientation;
topology;
burial/occlusion;
clutter;
complexity.
Twin 2T later attaches structural state to the same objects.
## Semantic truth
Every spatial element gets semantic truth:
Equation. M(x)=class/entity/region.
Example:
object_018
    type: pipeline_segment
    geometry: ...
    pose: ...
    parent: pipeline_03
Twin 2S knows exact identity.
Model 2S does not.
## Topological truth
Geometry isn’t enough.
Twin 2S should derive/store:
connected regions
navigable passages
obstacles
containment
adjacency
accessibility
asset topology
This gives us ground truth for global/regional spatial reasoning.
## Dynamic objects
Twin world shouldn’t be frozen.
Objects can have:
Equation. x_i(t),R_i(t),v_i(t).
Potential dynamic elements:
marine organisms
suspended debris
moving vegetation
robot-disturbed material
temporary obstacles
while infrastructure geometry remains mostly static.
This lets TBD-S learn persistence correctly.
## Structural changes
Twin 2S also needs occasional persistent changes:
debris appears
object moves
sediment shifts
component displaced
obstacle removed
These differ from moving objects because they change the persistent map.
## Visibility Engine
This should be one of Twin 2S’s most important modules.
Given:
Equation. S_t,V_t
compute:
Equation. mathcal V_t(x)in[0,1].
Visibility can depend on:
sensor pose
orientation
field of view
range
occlusion
water visibility
sensor modality
surface orientation
Thus Twin 2S knows whether every region was:
clearly observable
weakly observable
not observable
from a given measurement.
## Cumulative observability
Across mission history:
Equation. mathcal V_1:t(x) = Aggregate( mathcal V_1(x),…,mathcal V_t(x) ).
This provides ground truth for Model 2S’s:
Equation. U_O
observational uncertainty.
That’s something normal mapping datasets rarely give us perfectly.
## Viewpoint diversity
Ten images from nearly the same position should not equal ten independent observations.
Twin knows sensor poses exactly.
So calculate ground-truth metrics such as:
Equation. D_V(x) = f( view directions, ranges, modalities ).
This can supervise/validate whether Model 2S understands evidence diversity.
## Candidate proprietary mechanism
For Twin 2T we have MCDE.
For Twin 2E, MEIFE.
For Twin 2S, I propose:
Observability-Controlled Procedural World Engine - OCPWE
The important research idea isn’t simply procedural generation.
It’s:
Generate worlds and observation trajectories jointly to control exactly what information about the world is available to the learner.
Instead of:
Equation. World-> random camera.
We generate:
Equation. (World, DesiredInformationCondition) -> ObservationSequence.
## OCPWE
Suppose we want a training example where:
pipeline:
80% observed
weld:
15% observed
rear surface:
0% observed
same-view redundancy:
high
sonar coverage:
moderate
OCPWE searches/generates viewpoints and sensor configurations satisfying those information conditions.
Formally:
Equation. V_1:T = Planner( S, C_information ).
Then:
Equation. O_1:T=Sensor(S,V_1:T).
This gives us controlled epistemic experiments, not merely random synthetic scenes.
## Why this matters
For UAHSM we need examples distinguishing:
### Case A
Unobserved region happens to be easy to infer.
### Case B
Unobserved region has multiple plausible geometries.
### Case C
Region has many observations but all are redundant.
### Case D
Few observations from highly informative viewpoints.
### Case E
Sensor measurement good, localization poor.
### Case F
Two modalities disagree.
Random simulation may generate too few of these.
OCPWE deliberately generates them.
## Ambiguous worlds
Twin 2S should deliberately generate worlds where observations are compatible with multiple realities.
Example:
OBSERVATION
pipe ───── [occlusion] ───── pipe
Hidden region could contain:
A: straight pipe
B: curved pipe
C: junction
If evidence cannot distinguish them, Model 2S should remain uncertain.
This is an excellent test of whether it hallucinates.
## Counterfactual world pairs
Even better:
Generate:
Equation. W_A!= W_B
such that:
Equation. O(W_A)~= O(W_B)
for currently observed regions.
Then a good 2S should produce similar uncertain beliefs over the hidden region rather than confidently identify which world exists.
Once a discriminating viewpoint arrives:
Equation. O^*
the beliefs should diverge.
This could become one of the strongest experiments in the entire Model 2 project.
## Sensor models
Twin 2S should support progressively:
RGB
depth
sonar
range
and provide pose context.
Each:
Equation. O_m=H_m(S,V,eta_m).
Sensor corruption includes:
noise
blur
backscatter
low light
range limits
sonar artifacts
dropout
calibration error
pose error
## True pose vs reported pose
Very important.
Twin knows:
Equation. P_t^true.
But Model 2S may receive:
Equation. P_tobs=P_ttrue+epsilon_P.
Therefore we can independently control:
sensor uncertainty
and:
Equation. boxedlocalization uncertainty.
This tests whether 2S handles them correctly.
## Three fidelity levels
Again:
### Level 1 - abstract geometry
Ray casts / occupancy observations.
Very fast.
### Level 2 - synthetic geometric sensor data
Depth, point clouds, simplified sonar.
### Level 3 - high-fidelity sensor simulation
RGB/sonar + underwater optical effects + Unity integration.
This means UAHSM development can start far before Unity V2 is finished.
## Generated training episode
Conceptually:
scenario/
│
├── world_truth/
│   ├── geometry
│   ├── occupancy
│   ├── semantics
│   ├── topology
│   └── dynamic_objects
│
├── trajectory/
│   ├── true_pose
│   └── reported_pose
│
├── observability/
│   ├── per_frame_visibility
│   ├── cumulative_coverage
│   └── viewpoint_diversity
│
├── observations/
│   ├── RGB
│   ├── sonar
│   └── depth
│
├── sensor_quality/
│
├── changes/
│
└── provenance/
This is exactly what 2S needs.
## Procedural curriculum
### Tier 1
single object
clean geometry
perfect pose
full observation
### Tier 2
multiple objects
occlusion
partial observation
### Tier 3
large environment
hierarchical geometry
redundant viewpoints
### Tier 4
multimodal sensing
pose error
sensor degradation
dynamic objects
### Tier 5
counterfactual worlds
extreme partial observation
OOD geometry
persistent changes
long mission gaps
## Training UAHSM with Twin 2S
Twin gives:
Equation. S^true
and:
Equation. mathcal V_1:t^true.
Therefore we can supervise separately:
### Geometry
Equation. L_G(hat G,G^true)
### Occupancy
Equation. L_O.
### Semantics
Equation. L_S.
### Observability
Equation. L_V(hat V,V^true).
### Uncertainty
Does uncertainty correspond to actual error/evidence coverage?
### Hallucination
Penalize confident claims in regions for which evidence does not justify certainty.
## Critical three-mask supervision
For every world point/cell/region generate:
Equation. M_observed
Equation. M_inferable
Equation. M_unknown.
Observed is directly derivable from visibility.
But inferable vs unknown is harder.
We should not arbitrarily label hidden geometry “inferable.”
Instead, OCPWE counterfactual worlds give us a principled route.
If multiple substantially different hidden worlds are compatible with observations:
UNKNOWN / MULTIMODAL
is justified.
If observations + strong structural constraints collapse the plausible set substantially:
INFERRED
becomes defensible.
This is much stronger than manually assigning labels.
## Twin 2S validation
Geometry itself is mathematically known because we generated it.
But that does not mean the synthetic distribution is realistic.
Validate:
scene geometry distributions
object dimensions
clutter
topological complexity
visibility patterns
sensor statistics
trajectory distributions
against public underwater mapping/inspection datasets.
## Sensor validation
Compare synthetic and real distributions for:
RGB degradation
depth error
sonar response characteristics
occlusion patterns
pose noise
where public data supports it.
Again:
Equation. synthetic realism
and
Equation. downstream usefulness
are separate questions.
## Downstream reality-gap test
Train:
Equation. Model 2S
on different Twin 2S configurations.
Evaluate on held-out real underwater sequences.
Compare:
simple procedural twin
+ realistic geometry
+ sensor corruption
+ OCPWE
+ domain randomization
+ real adaptation
If OCPWE doesn’t improve real robustness/calibration, we don’t claim it does.
## OCPWE ablations
Full OCPWE
− observability control
− counterfactual worlds
− ambiguity generation
− viewpoint diversity control
− pose uncertainty
− modality corruption
− adaptive difficulty curriculum
Measure:
reconstruction;
uncertainty calibration;
unsupported confidence;
unknown-space detection;
synthetic→real transfer.
## Connection to Active Intelligence
There’s another major advantage.
OCPWE can eventually train/evaluate Active Intelligence.
Given belief:
Equation. B_t
and candidate viewpoints:
Equation. V_1,…,V_n,
Twin 2S knows exactly how much hidden information each viewpoint would reveal.
So we can calculate true information gain:
Equation. IG(V) = H(Smid O_1:t) - H(Smid O_1:t,O_V)
or practical approximations.
Then compare the robot’s selected viewpoint against actual information gain.
This gives us unusually strong supervision for next-best-observation.
## Full 2S loop
TWIN 2S
                    │
             exact world truth
                    │
                    ▼
                 OCPWE
                    │
        controlled observability
                    │
                    ▼
             Sensor Simulation
                    │
                    ▼
                  ECMER
                    │
                    ▼
               Model 2S
                  UAHSM
                    │
                    ▼
        Persistent Spatial Belief
                    │
                    ▼
      observed / inferred / unknown
                    │
                    ▼
             Active Intelligence
                    │
              next viewpoint
                    │
                    └──────────► Twin 2S
Eventually Unity executes that viewpoint physically in simulation.
## The complete Model 2 family
We’ve now architected all six major components:
Domain | Model | Main mechanism | Twin | Main mechanism
Technical | 2T | TCDP | Twin 2T | MCDE
Ecological | 2E | CEFD | Twin 2E | MEIFE
Spatial | 2S | UAHSM | Twin 2S | OCPWE
All inherit the common Core:
Equation. boxed ECMER+BUO+RBP+TBD+PMBL+Uncertainty
but none is merely the same neural network with different training data.
That was one of our major requirements, and the architecture now satisfies it conceptually.
# 16. Model1, MCBR and BAAC: Full Implementation Specification
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 0d82fc95. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Model 1 input contract
Model 1 never needs Twin truth.
It receives a DecisionContext:
class DecisionContext:
    timestamp_ns: int
    mission: MissionState
    beliefs_2t: list[BeliefMessage]
    beliefs_2e: list[BeliefMessage]
    beliefs_2s: list[BeliefMessage]
    robot_state: RobotState
    resource_state: ResourceState
    link_state: LinkState
    previous_decisions: list[DecisionSummary]
    active_information_needs: list[InformationNeed]
    system_health: SystemHealth
This is the complete working reality available to the decision system.
## Do not dump the entire world into Model 1
A mission may eventually contain millions of spatial beliefs.
Therefore:
Belief Bus
    ↓
Belief Query Engine
    ↓
mission-relevant subset
    ↓
Model1
Model1 requests structured context.
Example:
belief_query(
    domain="2T",
    region=pipeline_section_17,
    fields=["condition", "uncertainty", "change"],
    max_age=30.0
)
## Model 1 working state
Equation. W_t^work = (Q_t,B_t,M_t,R_t,C_t,H_t)
where:
Q_t: active questions/requirements;
B_t: retrieved beliefs;
M_t: mission;
R_t: robot/resources;
C_t: constraints/context;
H_t: compact decision history.
This state is temporary.
It is not another persistent world model.
## Decision Claim Graph
For each decision cycle construct:
Equation. mathcal G_t^D=(V_D,E_D).
Node types:
BELIEF_CLAIM
MISSION_REQUIREMENT
RESOURCE_STATE
ROBOT_STATE
CONSTRAINT
INFORMATION_NEED
CANDIDATE_ACTION
EXPECTED_OUTCOME
Edges:
SUPPORTS
CONTRADICTS
REQUIRES
BLOCKS
AFFECTS
RESOLVES
DEPENDS_ON
## Claim schema
class DecisionClaim:
    claim_id: UUID
    claim_type: ClaimType
    content_embedding: Tensor
    structured_value: dict
    source_belief_ids: list[UUID]
    evidence_refs: list[UUID]
    uncertainty: Uncertainty
    timestamp_ns: int
World-dependent claims require belief/evidence grounding.
## Grounding invariant
For every world-dependent decision claim:
Equation. Decision -> Claim -> Belief -> Evidence.
If the path does not exist:
status = UNSUPPORTED
and Model1 cannot silently treat it as fact.
This gives us a mechanically auditable version of grounding.
## Model 1 action vocabulary
Do not initially let Model1 generate arbitrary robot commands.
V1 action types:
CONTINUE_MISSION
QUERY_BELIEF
REQUEST_INFORMATION
REPLAN
CHANGE_SENSOR_MODE
REVISIT_REGION
WAIT
TRANSMIT_INFORMATION
STORE_AND_FORWARD
ESCALATE_TO_OPERATOR
RETURN_TO_SAFE_STATE
ABORT_MISSION
Potential later actions can be registered.
## Action schema
class ActionProposal:
    action_id: UUID
    action_type: ActionType
    target: TargetSpec | None
    parameters: dict
    supporting_claims: list[UUID]
    expected_outcome: dict
    uncertainty: float
    priority: float
Again:
Equation. boxedproposal!=permission
## Hard constraint layer
Every proposed action goes through:
class ConstraintEngine:
    def validate(
        self,
        action,
        robot_state,
        mission,
        resources,
        system_health
    ) -> ConstraintResult:
        ...
Constraints include:
battery
depth
collision
vehicle envelope
sensor availability
communications
hardware health
mission boundary
operator permissions
Invalid action:
never executes
regardless of Model1 confidence.
## EGDC computational pipeline
Our candidate:
Evidence-Grounded Deliberative Controller
becomes:
Mission
  ↓
Context Builder
  ↓
Belief Query Engine
  ↓
Decision Claim Graph
  ↓
Candidate Action Generator
  ↓
Consequence / Utility Estimator
  ↓
Constraint Engine
  ↓
Action Selector
  ↓
Tool / Execution Router
  ↓
Outcome Monitor
## Structured reasoning architecture
We should not freeze whether the reasoning engine is:
Transformer;
graph Transformer;
SSM;
structured language model;
neuro-symbolic;
model-based planner;
hierarchical policy.
But its I/O is frozen.
Input:
Equation. mathcal G_t^D.
Output:
Equation. _1,ldots,A_K

plus structured supporting claims.
## Candidate generation
Initially, deterministic templates can generate legal action candidates.
Example:
If:
mission requires inspection
+
target belief has high mission-relevant uncertainty
candidate set may contain:
CONTINUE
REQUEST_INFORMATION
REVISIT
CHANGE_SENSOR
ESCALATE
The learned reasoning system ranks/reasons over these.
This is much safer than generating arbitrary actions from text.
## Risk vs uncertainty
Keep separate:
Equation. U=how little we know
versus:
Equation. R=consequence/risk.
Example:
high uncertainty
low consequence
→ maybe continue
moderate uncertainty
catastrophic consequence
→ acquire evidence/escalate
Model1 needs both.
## Utility representation
Do not lock one scalar equation.
Represent consequence vector:
Equation. C(A)= [ mission, information, time, energy, risk, communication, compute ].
A policy/utility module can later learn or configure trade-offs.
Hard constraints remain outside utility.
## Decision horizons
Three:
### Immediate
seconds.
### Tactical
mission segment.
### Strategic
mission-level.
Model1 can reason over all three, but Navigation owns trajectory planning.
Model1 says:
inspect Region 4 again.
Navigation determines the path.
## Tool router
Model1 tools:
query_model2t
query_model2e
query_model2s
request_active_observation
request_navigation_goal
request_transmission
query_robot_health
query_resource_state
escalate_operator
Tools have typed schemas.
No arbitrary shell/code execution in deployed autonomy.
## Outcome monitoring
Every decision stores:
class DecisionRecord:
    decision_id: UUID
    context_hash: str
    action: ActionProposal
    expected_outcome: dict
    actual_outcome: dict | None
    supporting_claims: list[UUID]
    timestamp_ns: int
This enables:
Equation. expected-> actual
learning.
## Model1 training data
Twin truth can produce labels, but Model1 doesn’t receive truth as input.
Training episode:
Twin truth
   ↓
Model2-like noisy beliefs
   ↓
Model1
   ↓
decision
   ↓
simulated outcome
Oracle/planner can use truth to score the decision.
## Do not train Model1 only on perfect Model2
Critical.
Inject:
miscalibration
missing beliefs
stale beliefs
contradictions
wrong associations
OOD
domain disagreement
Otherwise Model1 collapses the first time upstream intelligence makes a mistake.
## Model1 curriculum
### M1
Mission/rule comprehension.
### M2
Belief querying/routing.
### M3
Single-step decisions.
### M4
Uncertainty-sensitive decisions.
### M5
Resource constraints.
### M6
Multi-domain reasoning.
### M7
Sequential missions.
### M8
Faults/OOD/contradiction.
### M9
Full simulated autonomy.
## Model1 losses
Depending architecture:
Equation. L_M1 = lambda_aL_action + lambda_vL_value + lambda_gL_grounding + lambda_rL_routing + lambda_cL_constraint + lambda_oL_outcome.
constraint is auxiliary learning only.
Hard runtime validation remains deterministic.
## Unsupported Inference Rate
Our major Model1 metric:
Equation. UIR= ( #unsupported world claims )/( #world claims ).
Target:
Equation. boxedUIR->0
while maintaining mission performance.
## Model1 baselines
M1-B0 fixed FSM
M1-B1 behavior tree
M1-B2 rule system
M1-B3 utility controller
M1-B4 classical planner
M1-B5 generic language-model planner
M1-B6 language-model + tools
M1-B7 RL policy
M1-B8 model-based RL
M1-B9 EGDC without claim graph
M1-B10 full EGDC
Not every baseline needs deployment on hardware.
## Active Intelligence input
Model1 creates:
class InformationNeed:
    need_id: UUID
    target_belief_ids: list[UUID]
    question_type: QuestionType
    target_properties: list[str]
    priority: float
    desired_uncertainty_reduction: dict
    deadline_ns: int | None
    constraints: dict
Example:
Determine whether PipelineSegment17’s apparent material loss is real enough to affect mission decision.
## Knowledge Gap
MCBR converts need into:
Equation. K_i=(B_i,U_i,V_i,Q_i).
Where:
B_i: belief;
U_i: uncertainty decomposition;
V_i: current observability/coverage;
Q_i: actual unresolved question.
## Uncertainty-aware sensing response
MCBR should distinguish:
### High U_A
Measurement noise.
Possible response:
repeat
change modality
improve sensing geometry
### High U_O
Insufficient observation.
new viewpoint
additional coverage
### High U_C
Contradiction.
discriminating observation
independent modality
### High U_E
OOD/model uncertainty.
different evidence
operator
additional modality
possibly no autonomous resolution
This is much richer than “go to highest entropy voxel.”
## Observation action
Candidate:
class ObservationAction:
    pose: PoseTarget
    sensor_id: UUID
    sensor_configuration: dict
    target_region: SpatialSupport
    duration: float
    expected_cost: ResourceCost
MCBR does not execute movement.
It proposes information acquisition.
## Candidate generation
Candidate observations can come from:
2S geometry
sensor constraints
navigation feasibility approximation
viewpoint sampler
mission constraints
Then MCBR evaluates them.
## Observability prediction
For candidate a:
Equation. hat V(a,x) = P( Observe(x)mid a,B^S ).
During synthetic training:
Twin2S gives:
Equation. V^true(a,x).
Train:
Equation. L_vis=d(hat V,V^true).
## Expected information gain
Ideal:
Equation. IG(a) = H(B_t) - mathbb E[ H(B_t+1mid a) ].
But our beliefs are complex structured objects.
So practical MCBR can learn:
Equation. widehatDelta L_belief(a).
## Oracle supervision
Twin knows truth.
For candidate:
Equation. V^oracle(a) = L(B_t,S^true) - L(B_t+1a,Strue).
This is superior to simply training:
uncertainty went down.
Because confident wrong beliefs can have low uncertainty.
## Mission-conditioned value
Observation can be informative but irrelevant.
Therefore define mission-relevant improvement:
Equation. MIG(a) = Delta L_mission-relevant beliefs.
Example:
Seeing a beautiful rock formation doesn’t help if the mission is resolving PipelineSegment17.
## MCBR candidate features
For each candidate:
Equation. x_a=[&IG,MIG,observability,travel,energy,time, &risk,sensor,uncertainty-type, &hypothesis-discrimination].
Then rank/select.
Exact utility architecture remains experimental.
## Hypothesis discrimination
Suppose:
Equation. H_1=actual corrosion
Equation. H_2=visual biofouling artifact.
For candidate observation a, predict:
Equation. P(Omid H_1,a)
and:
Equation. P(Omid H_2,a).
Prefer views/modalities maximizing discriminability.
This is a central MCBR hypothesis.
## ObservationPlan
Output:
class ObservationPlan:
    plan_id: UUID
    need_id: UUID
    target_beliefs: list[UUID]
    primary_action: ObservationAction
    alternatives: list[ObservationAction]
    expected_information_gain: float
    expected_mission_gain: float
    targeted_uncertainty: list[UncertaintyType]
    expected_cost: ResourceCost
    confidence: float
    provenance: UUID
Navigation then converts this to trajectory.
## MCBR stop condition
Do not inspect forever.
Stop when:
Equation. ExpectedValue(next observation) < Cost
or:
Equation. InformationNeedSatisfied=True.
Also stop/escalate when no feasible observation resolves the need.
## MCBR curriculum
### A1
Visibility prediction.
### A2
Single-view information ranking.
### A3
Sensor selection.
### A4
Uncertainty-type targeting.
### A5
Hypothesis discrimination.
### A6
Sequential sensing.
### A7
Mission-conditioned sensing.
### A8
Navigation-cost-aware sensing.
### A9
Integrated Model1 loop.
## MCBR baselines
A-B0 random view
A-B1 fixed inspection
A-B2 coverage planner
A-B3 frontier
A-B4 geometric NBV
A-B5 entropy NBV
A-B6 standard expected information gain
A-B7 uncertainty NBV
A-B8 RL active perception
A-B9 MCBR without mission conditioning
A-B10 full MCBR
## MCBR metrics
Primary:
Equation. Delta Error_belief
Equation. Delta Error_mission-belief
Equation. Information/Energy
Equation. Information/Time.
Also:
redundant observations
mission success
regret vs Twin oracle
number of observations
## BAAC input
Communications Intelligence receives:
class CommunicationContext:
    beliefs: list[BeliefMessage]
    mission: MissionState
    link_state: LinkState
    receiver_state: ReceiverKnowledge
    queue: list[InformationUnit]
    resource_state: ResourceState
## Receiver knowledge
This is critical.
BAAC maintains:
Equation. K_R(t)
approximation of what receiver already knows.
Otherwise it repeatedly transmits the same information.
## Information Unit
class InformationUnit:
    unit_id: UUID
    content_type: InformationType
    belief_ids: list[UUID]
    evidence_ids: list[UUID]
    semantic_delta: SemanticDelta
    priority: float
    deadline_ns: int | None
    fidelity_levels: list[FidelityOption]
    dependencies: list[UUID]
    size_bits: dict
## Semantic deltas
Transmit changes:
NEW_BELIEF
STATE_CHANGED
UNCERTAINTY_CHANGED
CONTRADICTION_ADDED
EVIDENCE_ADDED
RELATIONSHIP_CHANGED
BELIEF_RETIRED
rather than repeatedly transmitting complete state.
## Communication hierarchy
Priority classes:
### P0
Emergency/control.
### P1
Critical structured belief.
### P2
Evidence summary.
### P3
Selected raw evidence.
### P4
Bulk/raw archive.
When bandwidth collapses:
Equation. P4-> P3-> P2
can disappear before P0/P1.
## Progressive fidelity
Example structural finding:
### F0
Critical anomaly Segment17
### F1
severity + uncertainty + location
### F2
structured evidence summary
### F3
compressed supporting image/sonar
### F4
full raw evidence
BAAC selects fidelity dynamically.
## Value per bit
Basic metric:
Equation. VPB_i= (V_i)/(Size_i).
But V_i isn’t merely prediction confidence.
It depends on:
mission relevance
receiver novelty
deadline
uncertainty
decision impact
dependencies
## BAAC candidate mechanism
Belief-Aware Adaptive Communication
Scheduler considers:
Equation. x_i=[&mission value,belief change,uncertainty, &receiver novelty,size,deadline,link, &fidelity,dependencies].
Output:
Equation. send/not send, fidelity, link, order.
## Scheduler constraints
At interval:
Equation. sum_i bits_ile B_t.
Also:
deadline
link availability
energy
dependencies
packet constraints
Initially solve with deterministic optimization/priority scheduling.
Only introduce learned scheduler if it beats that.
## Persistent queue
When link disappears:
new information
    ↓
queue
    ↓
prioritize
    ↓
store
When link returns:
queue
 ↓
re-evaluate value
 ↓
drop stale/redundant
 ↓
send important deltas
This is essential underwater.
## Multi-link
Later:
acoustic
optical
tether/RF-at-surface
represented through:
class LinkState:
    link_id: UUID
    link_type: LinkType
    bandwidth_bps: float
    latency_s: float
    packet_loss: float
    bit_error_rate: float
    energy_per_bit: float
    health: LinkHealth
BAAC chooses appropriate link/fidelity.
## Channel simulator
class ChannelSim:
    def transmit(
        information_unit,
        fidelity,
        link_state
    ) -> TransmissionResult:
        ...
Supports:
bandwidth
latency
loss
bit errors
dropout windows
energy
Later calibrated using physical communications tests.
## BAAC oracle
Twin/integrated simulator can evaluate:
Equation. V_comm(I) = Performance_receiver(with I) - Performance_receiver(without I).
This gives learning/evaluation targets for communication value.
## BAAC curriculum
### C1
Fixed bandwidth.
### C2
Variable bandwidth.
### C3
Loss/latency.
### C4
Dropouts.
### C5
Fidelity choice.
### C6
Partial receiver knowledge.
### C7
Deadlines/dependencies.
### C8
Multi-link.
### C9
Integrated mission.
## BAAC baselines
C-B0 send everything
C-B1 FIFO
C-B2 fixed priority
C-B3 fixed compression
C-B4 value/bit heuristic
C-B5 generic semantic compression
C-B6 learned scheduler
C-B7 BAAC without receiver knowledge
C-B8 BAAC without belief uncertainty
C-B9 BAAC fixed fidelity
C-B10 full BAAC
## Bandwidth-collapse experiment
Evaluate at:
Equation. 100%,50%,10%,1%,0.1%,0.
Measure:
Equation. MissionRelevantInformationRetained.
We want graceful degradation.
## BAAC metrics
Equation. Information/Bits
Equation. Information/Joule
Equation. CriticalLatency
Equation. ReceiverSyncError
Equation. DeadlineSuccess
Equation. MissionPerformance.
Raw reconstruction quality is secondary.
## Distributed belief synchronization
Receiver shouldn’t simply overwrite belief state.
Messages contain:
belief ID
timestamp
model version
provenance
evidence references
semantic delta
Receiver reconciliation considers freshness and provenance.
This is effectively distributed PMBL synchronization.
## Integrated example
Robot sees ambiguous pipeline condition.
2T
Segment17:
possible material loss
UC high
Model1 asks:
Does this uncertainty matter to mission?
Yes.
Creates:
InformationNeed
MCBR determines:
side sonar view from 2 m offset best discriminates hypotheses.
Navigation executes.
New sonar evidence reaches 2T.
2T resolves:
material loss likely
uncertainty lower
Model1 determines:
finding is mission-critical.
BAAC receives:
STATE_CHANGED
UNCERTAINTY_CHANGED
Bandwidth is poor.
BAAC sends:
location
severity
uncertainty
mission significance
and queues raw sonar for later.
That is the complete intelligence loop.
## Another important case: MCBR failure
Suppose high:
Equation. U_E.
MCBR evaluates all feasible observations but predicts little information gain.
Correct behavior isn’t:
inspect forever.
It returns:
NO_SUFFICIENT_AUTONOMOUS_RESOLUTION
Model1 can then:
escalate
continue conservatively
store evidence
return
depending mission consequence.
## Model1 + MCBR joint training
Eventually:
Equation. Model1 -> InformationNeed -> MCBR -> Observation -> Model2 -> updated belief -> Model1.
But train separately first.
Otherwise failure attribution becomes impossible.
## Model1 + BAAC relationship
Model1 decides communication intent:
surface operator needs this finding.
BAAC decides:
what representation, fidelity, timing and link should carry it.
Model1 should not micromanage packet scheduling.
## Safety architecture
None of these three can bypass:
Safety Supervisor
Pipeline:
Model1 proposal
      ↓
Constraint Engine
      ↓
Navigation
      ↓
Safety Supervisor
      ↓
Controller
Emergency safety can override Model1.

## Model1 completion gates
### M1-D1
Structured context/querying works.
### D2
Every world-dependent claim is traceable.
### D3
Model1 performs constrained single-step decisions.
### D4
Handles uncertainty/contradiction/staleness.
### D5
Sequential mission reasoning works.
### D6
Cannot bypass hard constraints.
### D7
Works with imperfect Model2 beliefs.
### D8
Integrated simulated mission succeeds.

Candidate viewpoints generated correctly.
