## What is frozen
The boundaries are now strong:
Equation. boxedModel1 does not estimate domain truth
Equation. boxedMCBR does not control robot motion
Equation. boxedBAAC does not determine mission goals
Equation. boxedNavigation does not determine scientific information value
and:
Equation. boxedhard safety remains deterministic
unless we later have very strong evidence to modify individual pieces.
## What remains experimental
Equation. EGDC, MCBR, BAAC
are still research hypotheses.
Especially open:
exact Model1 reasoning architecture;
action/value learning;
MCBR information-gain estimator;
hypothesis representation;
learned vs analytical MCBR ranking;
BAAC scheduler;
learned communication value;
fidelity codec.
The interfaces are much more frozen than the algorithms.
# 17. Model1: Grounded Deliberation and Decision Semantics
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source ba0228df. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Formal mission
At time t, Model 1 receives a belief world:
Equation. hatmathcal W_t= _t2T,B_t2E,B_t^2S

plus:
Equation. M_t=mission state
Equation. R_t=robot/resource state
Equation. C_t=communications/system context
Equation. H_t=decision history.
It produces:
Equation. boxed A_t^*= pi_1( hatmathcal W_t,M_t,R_t,C_t,H_t )
where A_t^* is not necessarily a motor command.
It could be:
continue mission;
query 2T;
request more evidence;
inspect another location;
change sensing modality;
invoke Active Intelligence;
replan;
transmit information;
defer;
alert operator;
declare insufficient evidence.
## What Model 1 is NOT
It should not be:
giant prompt
   ↓
LLM
   ↓
robot command
That would destroy most of the scientific architecture we’ve built.
Nor should it consume raw RGB and suddenly decide:
“That pipe looks corroded.”
That’s 2T’s job.
Model 1 consumes structured beliefs.
## Proposed architecture
I propose:
Evidence-Grounded Deliberative Controller - EGDC
as the current research architecture/placeholder.
Its major components:
MODEL 1 / EGDC
            Mission / Objectives
                    │
                    ▼
              Context Builder
                    │
      ┌─────────────┼─────────────┐
      ▼             ▼             ▼
 Belief Query   Robot State   Resource State
   Engine           │             │
      └─────────────┼─────────────┘
                    ▼
             Working World State
                    │
                    ▼
             Reasoning Engine
                    │
                    ▼
          Candidate Action Generator
                    │
                    ▼
           Constraint / Risk Layer
                    │
                    ▼
            Utility Evaluation
                    │
                    ▼
              Action Selection
                    │
                    ▼
          Execution / Tool Router
                    │
                    ▼
              Outcome Monitor
                    │
                    └──────► next cycle
This is much more constrained than an unconstrained agent.
## Model 1’s working state
Model 1 should not load the entire persistent world.
At decision cycle t, construct:
Equation. W_t^work= ( Q_t, B_t, M_t, R_t, C_t, H_t )
where:
Q_t: current decision/query;
B_t: relevant retrieved beliefs;
M_t: mission;
R_t: robot/resources;
C_t: communication/system context;
H_t: relevant decision history.
This is decision-centric retrieval.
## Belief Query Engine
Instead of dumping 100,000 belief cells into Model 1:
Equation. Q-> Retrieve(hatmathcal W) -> B^relevant.
Example:
Determine whether inspection of Pipeline Segment 12 is complete.
Query engine retrieves:
2T:
condition + structural uncertainty
2S:
coverage + unknown regions
2E:
surface obscuration/environmental context
mission:
inspection requirements
Only relevant information enters working memory.
## Structured belief consumption
Each incoming claim carries:
state
domain
observed/inferred/predicted/unknown
UA
UE
UC
UO
timestamp
provenance
Therefore Model 1 can reason differently about:
directly observed high-quality evidence
versus:
relational inference with high epistemic uncertainty.
This is a major advantage of the architecture.
## Reasoning object
Model 1 should internally reason over claims, not just text.
Conceptually:
Equation. C_i= ( statement_i, source_i, status_i, U_i, P_i, t_i )
For example:
CLAIM C17
subject:
Pipeline Segment 12
predicate:
structural condition
value:
possible material loss
source:
Model 2T
status:
INFERRED
uncertainty:
UA = low
UE = moderate
UC = low
UO = high
evidence:
E81, E94
This becomes the atomic reasoning unit.
## Claim graph
Model 1 can build a temporary:
Decision Claim Graph
with nodes:
belief claims
mission requirements
robot state
resource constraints
operator constraints
candidate actions
and relationships such as:
SUPPORTS
CONTRADICTS
REQUIRES
BLOCKS
AFFECTS
RESOLVES
DEPENDS_ON
This gives reasoning structure beyond a hidden language-model context.
## Evidence-grounding rule
Every world-dependent conclusion should trace back to beliefs.
Conceptually:
Equation. Decision -> Claims -> Beliefs -> Evidence.
If Model 1 generates a claim without adequate support:
UNSUPPORTED
should be a valid internal state.
This is one of EGDC’s central proposed safeguards.
## Cross-domain reasoning
Now Model 1 performs the reasoning that no individual child should own.
Example:
### 2T
Structural condition uncertain.
### 2E
Dense biological surface coverage.
### 2S
Critical rear surface unobserved.
Model 1 can infer:
Current inspection evidence is insufficient for the mission objective.
But it should not infer:
the pipe is damaged.
That remains a scientific inference requiring 2T.
## Candidate actions
Model 1 should reason over a constrained action vocabulary.
Example:
Equation. mathcal A_t=  a_1,…,a_n .
Potential actions:
CONTINUE_MISSION
QUERY_MODEL_2T
QUERY_MODEL_2E
QUERY_MODEL_2S
REQUEST_INFORMATION
CALL_ACTIVE_INTELLIGENCE
REPLAN_ROUTE
CHANGE_SENSOR_MODE
REVISIT_REGION
WAIT
TRANSMIT_SUMMARY
STORE_AND_FORWARD
ESCALATE_TO_OPERATOR
ABORT / SAFE_RETURN
Actual low-level motor commands remain with navigation/control.
## Action proposal vs action permission
These must be separate.
Model 1 can propose:
Equation. a_i.
But a deterministic constraint layer checks whether:
Equation. a_iin A_allowed.
For example:
Model 1 proposes:
inspect region X
Constraint layer:
battery insufficient for route + return reserve
Result:
REJECT
We should not rely on a neural model to remember hard safety constraints.
## Hard constraint layer
Deterministic constraints can include:
battery reserve
depth limits
communication requirements
vehicle envelope
collision constraints
mission boundaries
sensor operating limits
operator rules
hardware health
Formally:
Equation. C(a,s)in,INVALID.
Invalid actions never reach execution.
## Risk is not uncertainty
Important distinction.
Model 2 gives uncertainty.
Model 1 considers consequence.
For action a:
Equation. Risk(a) = f( P(outcomemid B,a), Consequence(outcome) ).
A highly uncertain but irrelevant ecological classification may have low mission risk.
A moderately uncertain structural condition on a critical component may matter greatly.
So:
Equation. boxeduncertainty!=risk
and Model 1 is where that distinction becomes operational.
## Mission utility
Candidate actions can be evaluated using a structured utility:
Equation. J(a)= w_I I(a) -w_T T(a) -w_E E(a) -w_R R(a) -w_C C(a)
conceptually representing:
information/mission value I;
time cost T;
energy cost E;
risk R;
communications/compute cost C.
But I would not lock one scalar weighted sum.
Multi-objective or constrained optimization may prove better.
## Uncertainty-aware decision logic
Suppose:
Equation. U_O^2Tgg0.
Model 1 asks:
Does reducing this uncertainty matter to the mission?
If no:
Equation. continue.
If yes:
Equation. REQUEST_INFORMATION.
Then Active Intelligence determines:
What observation should we acquire?
This creates a clean division:
Equation. boxed Model 2: What do we know?
Equation. boxed Model 1: Does the uncertainty matter?
Equation. boxed Active Intelligence: How should we reduce it?
## Tool/model routing
Model 1 should not invoke every child every cycle.
It can decide:
Equation. Router(Q,W)-> Tool.
Example:
Question:
"Has this component deteriorated since last inspection?"
→ Model 2T
Question:
"Can I navigate behind this support?"
→ Model 2S
Question:
"Is this biological coverage changing?"
→ Model 2E
For cross-domain questions, query multiple children.
## Child models remain authoritative in their domains
If Model 1’s generic reasoning conflicts with a domain model:
Model 1 should not silently override it.
Instead:
Model 1 hypothesis
       ≠
Model 2T output
       ↓
CONFLICT
       ↓
request evidence / re-query / escalate
This prevents generic reasoning from substituting for domain intelligence.
## Planning
Model 1 can operate at several horizons:
### Immediate
Equation. a_t
next action.
### Short mission horizon
Equation. a_t,…,a_t+k.
### Strategic mission state
inspect A
then B
revisit C if uncertainty remains
surface/transmit
But detailed trajectories belong to Navigation.
Model 1 says:
inspect Region B.
Navigation solves:
exactly how do I move there?
## Replanning
After action a_t:
Equation. World_txrightarrowa_tWorld_t+1.
New beliefs arrive.
Then:
Equation. Plan_t+1 = Replan( Plan_t, B_t+1, Outcome_t ).
Plans are therefore hypotheses, not fixed scripts.
## Decision memory
PMBL handles world memory.
Model 1 needs decision memory:
DecisionRecord
decision_id
timestamp
objective
input_claims
candidate_actions
rejected_actions
constraints
selected_action
reasoning_summary
expected_outcome
actual_outcome
provenance
This allows post-mission analysis.
## Outcome learning
After execution:
Equation. ExpectedOutcome <-> ActualOutcome.
Model 1 can learn:
Which decisions tended to reduce uncertainty efficiently?
Which information requests were useless?
Which planning assumptions failed?
This provides training signal without requiring a Twin 1.
## No Twin 1
We preserve the decision:
Twin 1 does not exist.
Model 1 trains/evaluates using environments created by:
Equation. Twin 2T+Twin 2E+Twin 2S+Unity
plus mission scenarios.
The whole simulated system becomes Model 1’s environment.
That’s much cleaner.
## What should be learned?
Potentially learned:
claim representation
belief relevance/routing
candidate action proposal
soft consequence prediction
long-horizon planning priors
decision-value estimation
tool selection
experience-based strategy
## What should remain deterministic?
hard safety constraints
schema validation
action permissions
battery/depth hard limits
provenance checking
required mission rules
tool availability
command validation
And potentially some mission logic.
Again:
don’t neural-network solved engineering constraints.
## What kind of model powers the reasoning engine?
We should not freeze this yet.
Candidates include:
structured Transformer;
state-space reasoning model;
graph-based decision network;
language-model-like planner over structured tokens;
neuro-symbolic hybrid;
hierarchical policy;
model-based RL;
combinations.
A pretrained language/reasoning model could eventually be a baseline or component, but:
Equation. boxedModel 1!=API wrapper around an LLM.
Our architecture is the structured decision system around/within whatever learned reasoning component wins experimentally.
## Training curriculum
Model 1 training should be staged.
### Stage 1 - belief comprehension
Given structured Model 2 outputs, answer:
what is known?
what is uncertain?
what conflicts?
what is missing?
No action yet.
### Stage 2 - model/tool routing
Train:
Equation. Question-> Relevant Model/Tool.
### Stage 3 - constrained decision tasks
Simple worlds:
goal
+
beliefs
+
constraints
→ action
with known valid actions.
### Stage 4 - uncertainty decisions
Train situations where the correct behavior differs:
uncertainty matters
→ acquire information
uncertainty irrelevant
→ continue
This is critical.
### Stage 5 - sequential missions
Now decisions affect future information and state.
Equation. a_t-> B_t+1-> a_t+1.
Use the integrated twin environment.
### Stage 6 - adversarial/OOD scenarios
Introduce:
conflicting models
stale beliefs
missing model
sensor failure
communication loss
unseen world
bad predictions
resource shortage
Evaluate graceful degradation.
## Training methods
Different components may use different methods:
Equation. supervised learning+imitation+offline RL +model-based planning+online/simulated RL
where justified.
We shouldn’t decide “everything is RL.”
For many tasks, supervised decision traces or optimization-derived labels will be much more sample efficient.
## Where labels come from
We don’t need humans to label millions of missions.
For simple scenarios:
Equation. Optimal/nearOptimalAction
can come from deterministic search/optimization because twin truth is known during training-label generation.
Important:
Twin truth may create training labels.
Model 1 itself does not receive twin truth as input.
That preserves the information boundary.
## Training example
Twin truth says:
component:
actually damaged
critical area:
not yet observed
Model 1 only receives:
2T:
condition uncertain
UO high
2S:
critical area unknown
mission:
determine component condition
Oracle/planner using twin truth can determine that another observation has high expected value.
Training target:
Equation. REQUEST_INFORMATION.
But Model 1 never sees:
actually damaged.
Excellent separation.
## Loss families
Conceptually:
Equation. mathcal L_1= lambda_aL_action +lambda_rL_routing +lambda_gL_grounding +lambda_cL_constraint +lambda_uL_uncertainty-reasoning +lambda_pL_planning +lambda_vL_value.
For sequential learning, reward/objectives can include:
Equation. R= R_mission - C_energy - C_time - C_risk - C_unnecessary sensing.
Again, exact formulation later.
## Baselines
Model 1 needs strong comparisons:
fixed rule system
finite-state mission controller
behavior tree
utility-based controller
classical planner
generic LLM agent
generic LLM + tools
RL policy
model-based RL
EGDC without claim graph
EGDC without uncertainty decomposition
EGDC without provenance
full Model 1
This is where we determine whether the architecture actually adds value.
## Core experiments
### Grounding
Does Model 1 make unsupported world claims?
### Domain discipline
Does it defer technical/ecological/spatial inference to the correct Model 2?
### Uncertainty
Does it acquire more evidence when uncertainty matters, but avoid unnecessary sensing?
### Contradiction
Can it respond correctly to conflicting high-quality evidence?
### Constraints
Does it obey hard mission/safety/resource constraints?
### Routing
Does it invoke the right models/tools?
### Sequential planning
Does it improve mission completion over fixed policies?
### OOD
Does it escalate/defer rather than fabricate certainty?
### Failure handling
What happens if 2T, 2E, 2S, a sensor or communications channel fails?
## Unsupported-inference metric
We should explicitly measure:
Equation. UIR= frac unsupported world-dependent claims world-dependent claims .
We want:
Equation. UIR->0.
This can become one of Model 1’s defining evaluation metrics.
## Decision efficiency
Another useful metric:
Equation. IE= frac Deltamission-relevant information energy+time+communication+compute .
This will become particularly useful once Active Intelligence is connected.
## Explainability
We should not rely on chain-of-thought text.
Instead output structured decision traces:
Decision
objective:
determine structural condition
relevant beliefs:
B_2T_182
B_2S_091
problem:
2T observational uncertainty high
2S reports critical region unknown
candidate actions:
continue
reinspect
surface
selected:
request additional observation
constraint checks:
passed
supporting provenance:
...
This is inspectable without exposing or depending on hidden reasoning text.
## Human escalation
Model 1 should know when autonomy should stop.
Possible triggers:
high mission consequence + high uncertainty
unresolved credible contradiction
OOD condition
no valid safe action
model disagreement
hardware failure
operator-required decision
Output:
Equation. ESCALATE(reason,evidence,options).
## Full intelligence hierarchy
We can now state the architecture very cleanly:
RAW WORLD
    │
    ▼
SENSORS
    │
    ▼
ECMER
    │
    ▼
MODEL 2 FAMILY
"What is happening?"
│       │       │
2T      2E      2S
│       │       │
└───────┼───────┘
        ▼
 SHARED BELIEF BUS
        │
        ▼
      MODEL 1
"What should we do?"
        │
        ├────► Active Intelligence
        │      "What should we observe?"
        │
        ├────► Navigation
        │      "How do we get there?"
        │
        ├────► Communications
        │      "What should we transmit?"
        │
        ▼
     EXECUTION
        │
        ▼
     NEW WORLD
This is now a coherent autonomy architecture rather than a collection of AI models.
## Model 1 research hypothesis
Our candidate contribution becomes:
EGDC - Evidence-Grounded Deliberative Controller
Hypothesis:
A decision architecture operating over structured persistent beliefs, explicit uncertainty, provenance and constrained actions can make more reliable and information-efficient autonomous decisions than policies or generic agents operating over raw observations or unstructured model outputs.
Again: hypothesis, not novelty claim.
# 18. MCBR: Active Information Acquisition Design
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 81c5e230. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Architectural boundary
We need a very clean division:
Model 2 > What do we know, and how uncertain are we?
Model 1 > Does that uncertainty matter for the mission?
Active Intelligence > What observation would best resolve it?
Navigation > How do we physically obtain that observation?
Therefore Active Intelligence does not decide the mission objective and does not directly control thrusters.
## Input
Model 1 creates an Information Need:
Equation. N_j= (target, question, priority, desired uncertainty reduction, constraints)
Example:
InformationNeed
target:
    pipeline.segment12.weld4
domain:
    TECHNICAL
question:
    degradation_state
reason:
    Model 2T observational uncertainty high
mission_priority:
    HIGH
current_uncertainty:
    UA = low
    UE = moderate
    UC = moderate
    UO = high
constraints:
    remaining_time
    battery
    sensor availability
Active Intelligence receives this - not a vague instruction to “explore.”
## Knowledge-gap representation
For every relevant belief, derive a Knowledge Gap:
Equation. K_i= (B_i,U_i,V_i,Q_i)
containing:
belief;
uncertainty decomposition;
existing observation coverage;
missing information.
Examples:
Gap A:
rear weld surface never observed
Gap B:
RGB and sonar disagree
Gap C:
component has not been inspected recently
Gap D:
model is epistemically uncertain / OOD
These are fundamentally different gaps.
## Uncertainty type determines sensing strategy
This is where our decomposed uncertainty architecture pays off.
### High observational uncertainty U_O
Likely solution:
Equation. observe more / better viewpoint.
### High aleatoric uncertainty U_A
More identical measurements may accomplish nothing.
Maybe:
Equation. change modality/viewpoint/range.
### High contradiction U_C
Acquire discriminating evidence capable of separating competing hypotheses.
### High epistemic uncertainty U_E
More observations may or may not help.
Could require:
different modality
OOD handling
operator escalation
model/domain adaptation
So Active Intelligence should not reduce everything to “take another picture.”
## Candidate observation
Define:
Equation. a^obs_k= ( pose, sensor, configuration, target, duration ).
Example:
ObservationCandidate
target:
    weld4
viewpoint:
    position + orientation
sensor:
    sonar
configuration:
    range = ...
    angle = ...
expected_duration:
    ...
navigation_cost:
    ...
Active Intelligence evaluates many such candidates.
## Candidate generation
Candidates can come from:
geometric viewpoints
sensor-specific valid ranges
inspection templates
2S map/free-space
existing observation gaps
uncertainty regions
mission constraints
2S becomes especially important.
It tells Active Intelligence:
This surface is unknown from behind.
Candidate generator proposes viewpoints capable of seeing it.
## Observability prediction
Before physically moving, estimate:
Equation. P(Observe(x)mid a^obs).
Twin 2S gives perfect supervision during simulation.
At deployment, Model 2S/geometry/sensor models estimate:
visibility
occlusion
range
view angle
expected coverage
sensor suitability
So we avoid traveling to viewpoints unlikely to reveal useful information.
## Expected Information Gain
The obvious theoretical quantity is:
Equation. IG(a) = H(B_t) - mathbb E_Osim P(O|a,B_t) [ H(B_t+1) ].
Meaning:
How much uncertainty do we expect this observation to remove?
But computing full belief entropy may be expensive/impossible.
Therefore we need practical learned/approximate estimators.
## Mission-relevant information gain
Raw information gain isn’t enough.
Learning about an irrelevant rock might produce enormous information gain.
We need:
Equation. boxedMIG(a)=IG(a)x Relevance(a,M)
conceptually.
Model 1 supplies mission relevance.
Active Intelligence optimizes information acquisition within that target.
## Observation Utility
Now account for cost:
Equation. U_obs(a) = f( IG, mission relevance, time, energy, risk, communication, compute ).
Conceptually:
Equation. U_obs = V_info - C_travel - C_energy - C_time - C_risk.
Again, don’t prematurely lock this into one linear weighted sum.
Constrained/multi-objective optimization may work better.
## Candidate proprietary mechanism
I propose our current research architecture:
Mission-Conditioned Belief Resolution - MCBR
Hypothesis:
Next-best-observation should explicitly target specific unresolved belief components and uncertainty types, rather than maximizing generic exploration or geometric coverage.
So MCBR maps:
Equation. (B,U,N) -> observation objective.
Then candidate viewpoints are evaluated against that objective.
## Belief Resolution Target
Instead of saying:
explore Region A,
create:
BeliefResolutionTarget
belief:
    B_2T_weld17
property:
    degradation_severity
problem:
    contradiction
hypotheses:
    H1 = minor degradation
    H2 = severe degradation
desired_information:
    evidence discriminating H1 vs H2
Now Active Intelligence knows what information it actually wants.
That’s substantially stronger than ordinary exploration.
## Hypothesis discrimination
Suppose:
Equation. H_1,H_2,…,H_n
are competing beliefs.
Observation a should ideally maximize:
Equation. D( P(O|H_i,a), P(O|H_j,a) ).
In plain terms:
Choose a measurement whose possible outcomes look very different depending on which hypothesis is true.
This is especially useful for contradiction uncertainty.
## Example
2T has:
H1:
surface anomaly only
H2:
substantial material loss
RGB can’t distinguish them well.
Candidate observations:
A:
another RGB image
B:
sonar from same angle
C:
sonar from optimized side angle
MCBR might estimate:
Equation. IG(C)>IG(B)gg IG(A).
Then Navigation receives candidate C’s target viewpoint.
## Closed-loop operation
The complete cycle:
Model 2
   │
   ▼
uncertainty
   │
   ▼
Model 1
"does it matter?"
   │
   YES
   ▼
Information Need
   │
   ▼
MCBR
"What evidence would resolve it?"
   │
   ▼
Candidate Observations
   │
   ▼
Observation Utility
   │
   ▼
Selected Observation Goal
   │
   ▼
Navigation
   │
   ▼
Robot observes
   │
   ▼
ECMER
   │
   ▼
Model 2 updates belief
   │
   └──────────────► repeat
## Stop condition
Active sensing needs to know when to stop.
Possible condition:
Equation. ExpectedValue(next observation)<Cost(next observation).
Or Model 1 determines mission information requirement has been satisfied.
Otherwise the robot could inspect one weld forever trying to drive uncertainty to zero.
Zero uncertainty is neither realistic nor necessary.
## Multi-domain information acquisition
Information Need can originate from any child.
### 2T
Need better evidence of material condition.
### 2E
Need observation of unmeasured habitat region.
### 2S
Need geometry behind occlusion.
Sometimes one observation benefits several domains.
Example sonar scan:
Equation. IG_2T>0
and:
Equation. IG_2S>0.
Therefore observation utility can include cross-domain information value.
## Sensor selection
Active Intelligence chooses more than position.
It can select:
Equation. (sensor,pose,settings).
Example:
RGB:
good semantics
poor through turbidity
sonar:
better geometry
lower semantic detail
environment sensor:
local field measurement
The best action depends on the actual knowledge gap.
## Adaptive sensing
This connects directly to our hardware architecture.
Potentially:
far from target:
low-cost sensing
interesting region:
increase sensing fidelity
high uncertainty:
activate additional modality
resolved region:
reduce sampling
Thus sensing becomes mission- and belief-dependent.
## Training without real robot data
Twin architecture gives us an excellent training environment.
Twin 2S knows:
Equation. S^true
and exact visibility.
Twin 2T/E know hidden domain truth.
Therefore for candidate observation a, during training we can actually execute it in simulation and measure:
Equation. Delta Error(a)
or:
Equation. Delta Uncertainty(a).
This gives ground-truth observation value.
## Oracle information gain
During training/evaluation, because twin truth is available, compute an oracle-like value:
Equation. V^oracle(a) = L(B_t,S^true) - L(B_t+1a,Strue).
This answers:
How much did this observation actually improve the belief?
MCBR predicts:
Equation. hat V(a).
Train:
Equation. hat V(a)~= V^oracle(a).
Very powerful.
## OCPWE synergy
Twin 2S’s OCPWE can deliberately generate scenarios where:
viewpoint A reveals nothing
viewpoint B gives redundant evidence
viewpoint C resolves ambiguity
viewpoint D reveals irrelevant geometry
Then MCBR learns to distinguish them.
This is one reason our twin architecture fits together unusually well.
## Training stages
### Stage 1 - observability prediction
Equation. (candidate,world belief) -> expected visibility.
### Stage 2 - information-value prediction
Equation. (candidate,belief,knowledge gap) ->hat V_info.
### Stage 3 - sensor selection
Choose modality/configuration.
### Stage 4 - viewpoint ranking
Rank candidate observations.
### Stage 5 - sequential active sensing
Observation changes belief, requiring a new choice.
### Stage 6 - integrated mission acquisition
Model 1 + MCBR + Navigation.
## Baselines
Compare against:
fixed inspection route
random viewpoints
coverage maximization
frontier exploration
nearest unknown region
geometric next-best-view
entropy-based next-best-view
standard information-gain planner
RL exploration policy
MCBR without uncertainty decomposition
MCBR without mission conditioning
full MCBR
## Critical experiments
### Same coverage, different usefulness
Does MCBR choose mission-relevant observations?
### Redundant observations
Does it avoid repeated nearly identical views?
### Contradiction
Does it choose observations that discriminate hypotheses?
### Sensor choice
Does it switch modalities intelligently?
### Cost
Does it avoid huge travel for tiny information gain?
### Multi-domain benefit
Can one observation resolve several belief gaps?
### OOD
Does it avoid pretending it knows expected information gain when candidate conditions are unfamiliar?
## Evaluation metrics
Important ones:
Equation. Belief Error Reduction
Equation. Uncertainty Reduction
Equation. Mission-Relevant Error Reduction
Equation. fracInformation GainEnergy
Equation. fracInformation GainTime
Equation. Mission Completion Rate
Equation. Redundant Observation Rate
and:
Equation. Regret = V(a^*)-V(a_selected)
against oracle candidate choice in simulation.
## Important uncertainty caveat
We must prevent:
uncertainty reduction = automatically good.
A badly calibrated model could become more confidently wrong.
Therefore measure:
ground-truth belief error reduction
alongside predicted uncertainty reduction in twin experiments.
That’s essential.
## Failure behavior
If MCBR cannot confidently estimate observation value:
low confidence in observation utility
        ↓
Model 1
        ↓
conservative inspection policy
or
operator escalation
No forced cleverness.
## Output contract
Active Intelligence returns:
ObservationPlan
information_need_id
target_beliefs
selected_sensor
selected_viewpoint
sensor_configuration
expected_information_gain
expected_uncertainty_effect
expected_domain_benefits
estimated_time
estimated_energy
estimated_risk
alternative_candidates
confidence
provenance
Navigation then determines whether/how that viewpoint is physically reachable.
# 19. BAAC: Receiver Knowledge and Semantic Communication
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 2cb65fd3. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Architectural boundary
Keep the layers separate:
Communications hardware > Physically transmit bits.
Channel intelligence > What can this link currently support?
Communications Intelligence > What information should use those bits?
Model 1 > What communication outcome matters to the mission?
So:
Model 1 decides communication intent; Comms Intelligence optimizes information transfer.
## Inputs
At time t:
Equation. C_t= (B_t,M_t,L_t,R_t,Q_t)
where:
B_t: information available for transmission;
M_t: mission priorities;
L_t: channel/link state;
R_t: receiver state/knowledge;
Q_t: queued messages.
This last point is important.
If the surface station already knows something, transmitting it again may have little value.
## Channel state
Maintain explicit:
Equation. L_t= [ b_t,ell_t,p_t,e_t,j_t,… ]
representing things like:
available bandwidth
latency
packet loss
bit error characteristics
jitter
connection availability
estimated link duration
energy per transmitted bit
depending on physical communication technology.
This comes from the communications subsystem/channel estimator.
## Receiver knowledge state
Maintain an approximation:
Equation. K_R(t)
of what the receiver already possesses.
Example:
surface station knows:
    spatial map v31
    structural beliefs through t=140s
does NOT know:
    new contradiction at Weld 17
    new ecological observation
This enables delta communication.
Transmit:
Equation. Delta B
rather than repeatedly transmitting entire state.
## Communication object
Everything transmissible becomes an Information Unit:
Equation. I_k= (content,value,size,deadline,dependencies,fidelity).
Example:
InformationUnit
type:
    BELIEF_UPDATE
target:
    weld17
domain:
    2T
change:
    contradiction uncertainty increased
priority:
    HIGH
payload_size:
    ...
deadline:
    ...
provenance:
    ...
## Not all information has equal value
Suppose the robot has:
4K inspection video       2 GB
2S map update             20 MB
2T belief update          5 KB
critical alert            500 bytes
Under poor bandwidth, transmitting the 500-byte alert may provide vastly greater mission value than attempting raw video.
Therefore define:
Value per Bit
Equation. VPB(I)=(V(I))/(Size(I)).
This should become a central concept.
## Candidate research architecture
I propose:
Belief-Aware Adaptive Communication - BAAC
Hypothesis:
Under constrained underwater communication, transmitting prioritized structured belief updates and adaptively selected supporting evidence preserves more mission-relevant information per bit than fixed-fidelity or raw-data-first communication.
Again: research hypothesis, not novelty claim yet.
## Information hierarchy
BAAC should recognize several levels:
### Level 0 - emergency/control
vehicle fault
abort
critical event
Tiny, highest priority.
### Level 1 - structured belief
state update
uncertainty
change
alert
Very compact.
### Level 2 - evidence summary
feature/embedding
thumbnail
small sonar region
measurement summary
### Level 3 - selected raw evidence
specific image
specific sonar segment
### Level 4 - bulk mission data
full video
full sonar
full logs
Poor channel:
Equation. 0->1->2
Good channel:
Equation. 0->1->2->3->4.
## Progressive fidelity
Information should be refinable.
For example:
Stage 1
"Weld 17 structural uncertainty increased."
Stage 2
+ numerical belief state
Stage 3
+ compressed evidence crop
Stage 4
+ full-resolution source observation
Receiver obtains useful information even if the connection disappears halfway through.
## Belief delta encoding
Instead of sending:
Equation. B_t
send:
Equation. Delta B_t=B_t-B_receiver.
But beliefs aren’t simple vectors, so semantic delta may mean:
NEW_BELIEF
STATE_CHANGED
UNCERTAINTY_CHANGED
CONTRADICTION_ADDED
EVIDENCE_ADDED
RELATIONSHIP_CHANGED
BELIEF_RETIRED
This aligns naturally with PMBL.
## Mission-conditioned value
Value shouldn’t be global.
Model 1 supplies:
Equation. Priority(B_imid M).
Suppose mission objective is infrastructure inspection.
A major 2T change may receive high value.
A routine ecological update may receive lower immediate communication priority - not because ecology is inherently less important, but because of the current mission objective.
Different mission:
Equation. Priority
changes.
## Uncertainty-conditioned communication
Uncertainty itself can determine transmission value.
Examples:
### High-confidence critical change
Transmit quickly.
### High contradiction on critical belief
Also potentially transmit quickly because operator input may be valuable.
### High aleatoric uncertainty
Maybe don’t waste bandwidth transmitting enormous corrupted raw data.
### High epistemic uncertainty
Potentially transmit selected evidence for human/external-model review.
Again, uncertainty decomposition becomes useful beyond the models themselves.
## Evidence-on-demand
Instead of automatically sending raw evidence:
ROBOT
"possible structural change at W17"
SURFACE
"send supporting sonar evidence"
ROBOT
transmits selected evidence
This is much more bandwidth-efficient.
The belief message includes provenance IDs, allowing the receiver to request exact evidence.
## Communication scheduler
Given information units:
Equation. I_1,ldots,I_n
and channel budget:
Equation. B_available,
choose:
Equation. S^*= argmax_S V(S)
subject to:
Equation. Size(S)le B_available.
This resembles constrained scheduling/knapsack, but with:
deadlines;
dependencies;
changing channel;
changing mission value;
progressive fidelity;
information redundancy.
So it becomes a dynamic optimization problem.
## Redundancy-aware scheduling
Suppose two images support the same belief and are nearly identical.
Sending both has low marginal value.
Define:
Equation. V(I_kmid S)
rather than independent:
Equation. V(I_k).
Then BAAC considers marginal information value.
This mirrors ECMER’s evidence novelty concept.
## Channel prediction
Underwater links may appear/disappear or vary strongly.
Maintain:
Equation. P(L_t+Delta tmid L_1:t,C).
Then decisions can differ:
poor channel now but likely strong surface link in 5 minutes
versus:
communication may be lost for an hour.
This affects store-vs-transmit decisions.
## Store-and-forward
Everything that cannot be transmitted immediately enters persistent queue:
CommunicationQueue
information_id
priority
deadline
size
dependencies
current_fidelity
transmission_status
When bandwidth improves, the scheduler revisits it.
Priorities can change over time.
## Adaptive compression
Compression level:
Equation. q_k=f( V_k, L_t, mission, receiver need ).
Example:
critical evidence:
higher fidelity
routine map region:
aggressive compression
already-known information:
don't transmit
This applies to imagery, sonar, spatial data and potentially embeddings.
## Semantic compression
Potentially transmit:
Equation. belief + compact evidence representation
instead of raw sensor stream.
But we need to be careful:
semantic compression must not destroy auditability.
Raw evidence remains onboard when possible.
Provenance identifies exactly what produced the transmitted belief.
## Distributed belief synchronization
Eventually both robot and surface station may maintain belief stores:
ROBOT BELIEF STORE
        ↕
   BAAC sync
        ↕
SURFACE BELIEF STORE
Synchronization messages:
CREATE
UPDATE
MERGE
RETIRE
CONTRADICTION
EVIDENCE_AVAILABLE
This is essentially distributed PMBL.
## Conflict after reconnection
Suppose robot and surface system independently update the same belief.
After reconnection:
Equation. B_R!= B_S.
Do not simply overwrite one.
Use provenance + timestamps + evidence:
robot belief
      +
surface belief
      ↓
belief reconciliation
      ↓
updated uncertainty / contradiction
BUO principles can actually help here.
## Hardware/software co-design
This is where your hardware side becomes particularly important.
The software needs a hardware abstraction like:
LinkState
link_type
available_bandwidth
latency
loss_rate
energy_cost
range_estimate
health
Then BAAC doesn’t care whether the physical link is:
acoustic
optical
short-range RF near surface
tether
surface Wi-Fi
The communications hardware exposes capability; software adapts.
## Multi-link selection
If multiple links exist:
Equation. l_1,l_2,…,l_n,
BAAC can choose:
Equation. (I_k,l_j,fidelity).
Example:
tiny critical alert
→ acoustic
large image
→ store
robot surfaces
→ high-bandwidth link
→ bulk upload
This could become a strong HW/SW co-design feature.
## Communications simulator
Before physical hardware, build:
Equation. ChannelSim
with configurable:
bandwidth
latency
packet loss
bit errors
dropouts
link windows
energy cost
and later more physically grounded underwater channel models.
This plugs directly into Unity/integrated simulation.
## Training BAAC
Much of the scheduler may not initially require neural learning.
Start with optimization/rules.
Then learn components where beneficial.
Potential learned pieces:
information-value estimation
channel prediction
compression-quality/value prediction
receiver-knowledge estimation
The scheduler itself can initially be deterministic optimization.
## Oracle training signal
In simulation, we know what happens after communication.
We can measure:
Equation. V(I)= Performance(receiver with I) - Performance(receiver without I).
This is far stronger than arbitrary priority labels.
For example:
Did transmitting this belief/evidence improve the surface system’s reconstruction or decision?
That becomes communication value ground truth.
## Training curriculum
### Stage 1
Fixed channel + static priorities.
### Stage 2
Changing bandwidth.
### Stage 3
Packet loss/dropouts.
### Stage 4
Progressive fidelity.
### Stage 5
Receiver already has partial knowledge.
### Stage 6
Multiple links.
### Stage 7
Integrated mission where communication affects Model 1/operator outcomes.
## Baselines
Compare:
send everything FIFO
raw-data-first
fixed compression
priority queue
standard bandwidth-aware scheduler
belief-only communication
value-per-bit scheduling
BAAC without receiver knowledge
BAAC without uncertainty
full BAAC
## Evaluation
Important metrics:
Mission-Relevant Information per Bit
Mission-Relevant Information per Joule
plus:
critical-message latency
belief synchronization error
information loss
raw-data fidelity
deadline success
queue age
communication energy
mission outcome
## Graceful degradation experiment
Reduce bandwidth:
Equation. 100%->50%->10%->1%->0.1%.
Compare systems.
Ideal BAAC behavior:
HIGH BANDWIDTH
beliefs + evidence + raw data
↓
MEDIUM
beliefs + selected evidence
↓
LOW
critical belief updates
↓
EXTREME
alerts / minimal state
↓
ZERO
store locally and continue safely
That’s a very clean Conrad demonstration.
## BAAC research hypothesis
Our candidate contribution is therefore:
Belief-Aware Adaptive Communication
with three core ideas:
Mission Value
Equation. +
Belief/Uncertainty Awareness
Equation. +
Adaptive Fidelity
to maximize useful information transfer under constrained channels.
And unlike generic semantic communications, it plugs directly into our persistent belief architecture.
## Full system now
We can extend our intelligence hierarchy:
MODEL 2
                 understand
                     │
                     ▼
                   MODEL 1
                    decide
                     │
          ┌──────────┼──────────┐
          ▼          ▼          ▼
       ACTIVE      COMMS     NAVIGATION
    INTELLIGENCE INTELLIGENCE
      observe     communicate    move
          │          │          │
          └──────────┼──────────┘
                     ▼
                  HARDWARE
This is the right software/hardware boundary for your division of work as well.
# 20. Navigation, Control, Unity and Hardware Interfaces
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 54c66632. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Ownership and Physical Interface Boundary
HARDWARE OWNER: BURAK
SOFTWARE STATUS: REFERENCE / INTERFACE CONTEXT
Burak owns physical actuators, sensor hardware, motor controllers, power delivery, wiring and the measured limits this chapter consumes. Kerem owns state estimation, planning, trajectory generation, control, allocation, safety software and the common software-side RobotHardwareInterface. Thruster geometry, allocation matrix, curves, deadzones, saturation, latency, asymmetry, health and power constraints are configuration/characterization inputs, not hardware fabrication tasks for software. Unity and physical adapters implement the same contract wherever technically possible; adapter-specific branching is confined to concrete boundary implementation.
OPEN / HUMAN REVIEW: ownership of any low-level firmware/driver layer is determined by the selected physical interface. It must expose declared RHI semantics and timing/health data without moving physical hardware responsibility to software.
## Runtime architecture
Model 1
   │
   ├── Mission objective
   │
MCBR
   │
   └── Observation objective
            ↓
       Goal Manager
            ↓
      State Estimator
            ↓
       Global Planner
            ↓
        Local Planner
            ↓
    Trajectory Generator
            ↓
     Motion Controller
            ↓
     Thruster Allocator
            ↓
 RobotHardwareInterface
       ↙           ↘
   Unity V2     Physical Robot
Safety Supervisor observes EVERYTHING
## Coordinate frames
We need these frozen early.
WORLD
ASSET
COMPONENT
ROBOT_BODY
CAMERA
SONAR
IMU
DEPTH_SENSOR
Transform:
Equation. T_A<- B(t)in SE(3)
means transform coordinates from frame B into A.
Never pass an unlabeled position vector.
## Pose
Use internally:
Equation. p=[x,y,z]^T
and quaternion:
Equation. q=[q_w,q_x,q_y,q_z].
Avoid Euler angles for core computation.
Euler:
Equation. [phi,theta,psi]
can remain for display/logging.
## Robot state
Equation. X_t^R= [p_t,q_t,v_t,omega_t,b_t,h_t].
Computational schema:
class RobotState:
    timestamp_ns: int
    position_world: np.ndarray       # [3]
    orientation_world_body: np.ndarray  # quaternion [4]
    linear_velocity_body: np.ndarray # [3]
    angular_velocity_body: np.ndarray # [3]
    covariance: np.ndarray
    battery_state: BatteryState
    health: RobotHealth
## Ground truth vs estimated state
Unity owns:
Equation. X_t^true.
Navigation receives:
Equation. hat X_t, P_t.
Hard invariant:
Equation. boxednavigation/control evaluation mode cannot read X^true
except designated evaluation/logging modules.
Same truth-leakage principle as the twins.
## State estimator interface
class StateEstimator:
    def predict(
        self,
        imu,
        control,
        dt
    ) -> PoseEstimate:
        ...
    def update_depth(self, depth): ...
    def update_visual(self, visual): ...
    def update_sonar(self, sonar): ...
    def update_map_constraint(self, constraint): ...
    def get_state(self) -> PoseEstimate: ...
Exact estimator is not frozen.
Candidates:
Equation. EKF, UKF, factor graph, VI/sonar SLAM, hybrid.
## Navigation and Model2S
Important distinction:
Equation. boxedStateEstimator=where am I?
Equation. boxedModel2S=what does the world look like?
They communicate.
Estimator provides pose + covariance to 2S.
2S provides landmarks/map constraints/spatial beliefs to estimator and planners.
## Navigation goal
Model1 or MCBR outputs:
class NavigationGoal:
    goal_id: UUID
    target_pose: Pose | None
    target_region: SpatialSupport | None
    position_tolerance_m: float
    orientation_tolerance_rad: float
    observation_constraints: dict
    deadline_ns: int | None
    risk_limit: float
    energy_budget_j: float | None
MCBR can additionally specify:
camera must face component within incidence range X.
Navigation must respect that.
## Goal Manager
Converts semantic goals into executable navigation objectives.
Examples:
GO_TO
STATION_KEEP
FOLLOW_STRUCTURE
CIRCUMNAVIGATE
INSPECT_SURFACE
SONAR_SWEEP
REVISIT
RETURN_HOME
## Global planner
Input:
Equation. B^S,hat X,G.
Output:
Equation. P=_0,ldots,p_n.
Cost conceptually considers:
Equation. C(P)= f( distance, energy, risk, current, uncertainty, mission ).
Do not immediately turn that into one arbitrary weighted equation.
## Knowledge-aware planning
Model2S distinguishes:
OBSERVED
INFERRED
UNKNOWN
Navigation must consume that.
For example:
OBSERVED free
→ normal traversal
INFERRED free
→ additional margin
UNKNOWN
→ policy-dependent caution
Safety policy - not Model2S - determines whether unknown space is traversable.
## Global planner baselines
Start:
A*
Dijkstra
RRT*
depending representation.
Then compare uncertainty/current/energy-aware variants.
No need to invent a neural global planner immediately.
## Local planner
Runs faster and responds to:
new obstacles
sonar returns
current
trajectory deviation
dynamic objects
map updates
Input:
Equation. hat X_t+B_local^S+P_global.
Output short horizon trajectory.
## Trajectory object
class Trajectory:
    timestamps: np.ndarray
    positions: np.ndarray
    orientations: np.ndarray
    linear_velocities: np.ndarray
    angular_velocities: np.ndarray
    tolerances: dict
Trajectory must be dynamically feasible.
## Inspection primitives
Implement reusable generators:
PIPELINE_FOLLOW
CONSTANT_STANDOFF
CIRCUMNAVIGATION
WELD_ARC
RASTER_SCAN
SONAR_SWEEP
MULTI_VIEW_INSPECTION
STATION_KEEP
This is especially useful for MCBR.
It can request:
obtain another 45° view
without planning raw thruster behavior.
## Observation geometry
Inspection trajectory generator consumes sensor parameters:
Equation. FOV, range, orientation, standoff.
Thus:
Equation. ObservationPlan -> NavigationGoal -> Trajectory.
MCBR’s desired viewpoint remains physically meaningful.
## Controller input
Desired trajectory state:
Equation. X_d(t).
Estimated:
Equation. hat X(t).
Error:
Equation. e(t)=X_d-hat X
with proper quaternion attitude error rather than naïve quaternion subtraction.
Controller outputs desired wrench:
Equation. tau_d= [F_x,F_y,F_z,M_x,M_y,M_z]^T.
## Controller V1
We need a serious classical baseline before RL.
Recommended progression:
Equation. PID -> cascaded PID -> MPC
with disturbance estimation.
Candidate research later:
Equation. u=u_classical+Delta u_theta.
But only if learned residual demonstrably helps.
## Why MPC matters
Underwater robot constraints include:
thruster saturation
vehicle dynamics
current
energy
trajectory constraints
which makes MPC a strong comparison.
But it may be too computationally expensive depending onboard hardware.
Therefore benchmark:
Equation. performance/latency/energy.
## Thruster allocation
Desired wrench:
Equation. tau_dinmathbb R^6.
For n thrusters:
Equation. finmathbb R^n.
Configuration matrix:
Equation. tau=Bf.
Each column derives from thruster position and direction:
Equation. B_i= d_i r_ix d_i .
This matrix should come directly from RobotConfig.
## Allocation problem
Basic:
Equation. f^* = argmin_f |Bf-tau_d|^2
subject to:
Equation. f_minle fle f_max.
Later include:
energy
deadzone
failed thruster
asymmetry
slew limits
This becomes constrained allocation.
## Thruster model
Initial Unity model:
Equation. T_i=k_i u_i|u_i|.
Dynamics:
Equation. tau_idot T_i+T_i=T_cmd.
Add:
deadzone
saturation
forward/reverse asymmetry
latency
noise
using hardware characterization.
## Thruster state
class ThrusterState:
    thruster_id: UUID
    command: float
    estimated_thrust_n: float
    rpm: float | None
    current_a: float | None
    temperature_c: float | None
    health: ThrusterHealth
## Fault-tolerant allocation
If thruster i fails:
Equation. B-> B_remaining.
Allocator recomputes feasible wrench.
Model1 does not decide individual motor outputs.
Safety/Nav can report:
DEGRADED_MANEUVERABILITY
to Model1.
## Safety Supervisor
Independent runtime layer.
Monitors:
collision
depth
leak
battery
control instability
pose uncertainty
thruster health
sensor health
compute watchdog
Can override autonomy.
## Safety states
NORMAL
DEGRADED
HOLD
RETURN
SURFACE / RECOVER
EMERGENCY_STOP
Exact physical recovery behavior depends on robot design.
## Collision envelope
Not just vehicle radius.
Safety margin:
Equation. D_safe=f(&vehicle geometry,speed,stopping distance, &pose uncertainty,map uncertainty).
Thus:
Equation. U_poseuparrow => marginuparrow.
## Localization-loss behavior
Trigger:
LOCALIZATION_LOST
based on covariance/consistency thresholds.
Possible deterministic response:
slow
hold
attempt relocalization
use alternative sensing
return if unresolved
rather than letting Model1 improvise motor behavior.
## RobotHardwareInterface
This is probably the most important hardware/software contract for your side.
class RobotHardwareInterface:
    def get_imu(self): ...
    def get_depth(self): ...
    def get_camera(self): ...
    def get_sonar(self): ...
    def get_thruster_state(self): ...
    def get_power_state(self): ...
    def get_health(self): ...
    def set_thruster_commands(self, commands): ...
Optional sensors expose capability discovery.
## Capability interface
RobotCapabilities:
    cameras
    sonars
    imu
    depth
    environmental_sensors
    thruster_count
    controllable_dof
    communication_links
    onboard_compute
Software must not assume a sensor exists because a developer laptop configuration had it.
## RobotConfig
Single canonical configuration:
robot:
  mass_kg: ...
  displaced_volume_m3: ...
  center_of_mass: [...]
  center_of_buoyancy: [...]
  inertia: [...]
  dimensions: [...]
  thrusters:
    - id: T1
      position: [...]
      direction: [...]
      thrust_curve: ...
  sensors:
    ...
  battery:
    ...
  compute:
    ...
  communications:
    ...
This feeds both Unity and physical software.
## Unity 6-DOF dynamics
Use:
Equation. Mdotnu+ C(nu)nu+ D(nu)nu+ g(eta) = tau+tau_env.
Where:
Equation. M=M_RB+M_A.
State:
Equation. eta=[x,y,z,phi,theta,psi]^T
Equation. nu=[u,v,w,p,q,r]^T.
Internally Unity can use quaternions even if equations use Euler notation.
## Buoyancy
Equation. F_B=rho gV.
Weight:
Equation. F_G=mg.
CoB-CoG offset produces restoring moment.
These parameters must come from measured robot properties eventually.
## Hydrodynamic drag
Start directional:
Equation. F_D= -D_1v_r -D_2|v_r|v_r.
Separate coefficients for axes.
Do not pretend initial coefficients are accurate.
Unity V2.0:
approximate physics
until identification.
## Added mass
Represent:
Equation. M_A.
Initial engineering estimates/literature ranges.
Later parameter identification.
No CFD requirement for V1.
## Current
Relative water velocity:
Equation. v_r=v_robot-v_c(x,t).
Twin2E can supply:
Equation. v_c^true(x,t)
to Unity.
Robot does not receive that truth unless it has appropriate measurements/estimates.
## Power model
Equation. P_total = P_thrusters + P_compute + P_sensors + P_comms.
Energy:
Equation. E(t+Delta t) = E(t) - P_totalDelta t.
Later:
voltage sag
battery discharge curve
temperature
BMS constraints
can be added from actual hardware.
## Sensor base class
Conceptually in Unity:
interface ISimSensor
{
    SensorId Id { get; }
    double FrequencyHz { get; }
    SensorObservation Sample(
        WorldState world,
        RobotState robot
    );
}
Every simulated sensor has:
pose
rate
latency
noise
health
calibration
## Camera
Parameters:
intrinsics
extrinsics
resolution
FPS
exposure
latency
Underwater degradation:
attenuation
color shift
backscatter
turbidity
blur
low light
noise
fouling
Statistical correspondence to real data matters more than cinematic visuals.
## IMU
Equation. a_m=a+b_a+n_a
Equation. omega_m=omega+b_g+n_g.
Model:
bias
bias random walk
white noise
rate
latency
dropout
vibration
## Depth sensor
Equation. z_m=z+b+n.
Include:
bias
noise
drift
latency
failure
## Sonar
Do not start by attempting perfect acoustic physics.
V1:
range
bearing/beam
intensity proxy
attenuation
noise
false returns
dropout
V2:
material response
multipath approximation
angle effects
environment dependence
Validate statistical/task usefulness against real sonar datasets.
## Sensor timestamps
Every observation:
Equation. (t_measurement,t_delivery).
Latency matters.
Do not stamp every sensor packet with “time received” and pretend synchronization is perfect.
## Runtime frequencies
Different subsystems need different rates.
Example starting ranges - not frozen:
System | Approximate range
Unity physics | 100-500 Hz
IMU | 100-400 Hz
control | 50-200 Hz
state estimator | 50-200 Hz
depth | 10-50 Hz
camera | 10-30 Hz
sonar | sensor dependent
local planning | 5-20 Hz
global planning | event / ~1 Hz
Model2 updates | asynchronous
Model1 | event-driven / low Hz
Exact values come from hardware.
## Asynchronous architecture
Do not make:
camera waits for Model1
Model1 waits for sonar
sonar waits for planner
Use timestamped message/event architecture.
Model2 belief bus is asynchronous already.
Robot stack should follow the same principle.
## Unity external interface
Python-facing:
reset(config)
step(control)
get_observations()
get_estimated_state()
get_mission_state()
get_link_state()
inject_fault(...)
get_metrics()
Training/evaluation only:
get_ground_truth()
Again physically separated if possible.
## Technology bridge
Candidate transports:
gRPC
ZeroMQ
ROS 2
shared memory
custom sockets
Don’t freeze yet.
For first prototype, choose the simplest reliable transport that supports timestamps and typed messages.
Architecture should remain transport-independent.
## Unity environment contract
Unity consumes:
Twin2S geometry
Twin2T visible structural effects
Twin2E environmental/ecological state
but does not evolve their scientific truth independently.
This boundary stays frozen.

Given:
Equation. seed+versions+config
we should reproduce the same experiment as closely as practical.

goal success

## Inspection metrics
For structure-following:
Equation. e_standoff = |d_actual-d_desired|.
Also:
surface coverage

These connect navigation performance to intelligence performance.
## Controller baselines
CTRL-B0 PID
CTRL-B1 cascaded PID
CTRL-B2 LQR where suitable
CTRL-B3 MPC
CTRL-B4 adaptive/robust controller
CTRL-B5 RL controller
CTRL-B6 classical + learned residual
No assumption RL wins.
## RL’s proper role
Use targeted RL for:
current-aware maneuvering
inspection trajectories
local obstacle avoidance
recovery
Not:
Equation. RGB-> thrusters
for the entire mission.
That would destroy most of the architecture we’ve carefully built.
## Learned residual dynamics
Candidate:
Equation. hat f = f_physics + r_theta(x,u).
Only add if physical model leaves systematic error.
Compare:
Equation. Physics
versus:
Equation. Physics+Residual.
If no meaningful gain, remove it.
## Hardware characterization pipeline
Your physical branch eventually produces:
mass
CoM
CoB
inertia estimate
thruster curves
deadzone
latency
battery behavior
sensor noise
bias
latency
communications behavior
These feed Unity.
## Thruster characterization
For each thruster:
Equation. command -> RPM/current/thrust.
Measure forward and reverse separately.
Fit:
Equation. T=f(u)
with uncertainty.
Do not assume manufacturer thrust curve exactly matches installed vehicle behavior.
## Static buoyancy/trim experiment
Measure:
mass
displaced volume
neutral buoyancy behavior
pitch/roll equilibrium
Infer:
Equation. CoB-CoG.
This directly affects Unity restoring forces.
## Dynamic parameter identification
Collect controlled trajectories:
straight acceleration
vertical
lateral
yaw
pitch where controllable
station keeping
Fit:
Equation. theta= [ drag, added mass, thruster dynamics, … ].
## Identification objective
Conceptually:
Equation. theta^* = argmin_theta sum_t |x_treal-x_tsim(theta)|_W^2.
Use different trajectories for identification and validation.
Otherwise we simply overfit Unity.
## Parameter uncertainty
Don’t store:
drag coefficient = 0.83 EXACT
Store:
Equation. thetasim distribution/range.
Controller training/randomization samples from identified uncertainty.
## Simulation validity levels
### L0 - Functional
Software behaves.
### L1 - Approximate Physics
Engineering model.
### L2 - Characterized
Real subsystem measurements incorporated.
### L3 - Identified
Vehicle parameters fitted.
### L4 - Validated Envelope
Held-out real trajectories match within defined tolerances.
Only L4 deserves “validated digital twin” within that operating envelope.
## Sim-to-real controller training
Train across:
Equation. thetasim P(theta).
Randomize:
mass
CoM/CoB
drag
added mass
thruster effectiveness
latency
current
sensor noise
but within physically justified distributions.
## HIL architecture
This is critical.
Twin2T/E/S
     ↓
Unity
     ↓ simulated sensors
REAL ONBOARD COMPUTER
     ↓
real autonomy stack
     ↓ thruster commands
Unity
     ↓
robot dynamics
Only vehicle is simulated.
Compute is real.
## Why HIL matters
It exposes:
latency
memory limits
GPU/CPU contention
driver behavior
message timing
dropped frames
watchdog failures
before putting the robot underwater.
## HIL gate
Require:
full mission software runs onboard
sensor rates sustained
control deadlines met
no unacceptable memory growth
fault recovery works
Model2 inference fits compute budget
before serious physical autonomy.
## Physical software swap
Simulation:
hardware = UnityRobotHardware(...)
Physical:
hardware = PhysicalRobotHardware(...)
Everything above:
StateEstimator
Navigation
Control
Model2
Model1
MCBR
BAAC
should remain largely unchanged.
That is the core sim→real engineering principle.

all the way back to:

# 21. Unity V2: Physics, Sensors, Faults and Experiment Design
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source 1587848f. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Ownership and Physical Parameter Provenance
Unity V2 is KEREM-OWNED SOFTWARE. Its physical parameters are not invented simulator facts: Burak supplies measured RobotConfig, RobotCapabilities, calibration and characterization records. These include dimensions, mass/displaced volume/inertia/CoM/CoB, actuator geometry and response, sensor calibration/noise/bias/drift/latency, power limits and failure behavior. Kerem owns ingestion, distributions, scenario/fault configuration, the Python bridge, replay and simulator validation. A parameter remains OPEN when the physical record is unavailable or unvalidated.
## Exact responsibility
Unity V2 owns:
Equation. boxed vehicle physics + robot/environment interaction + sensor viewpoint + sensor rendering + mission execution
The domain twins own:
Equation. Twin 2T &: structural truth Twin 2E &: ecological/environmental truth Twin 2S &: spatial truth
The models estimate those truths.
Conceptually:
SHARED SCENARIO
      │
 ┌────┴────────────────────────────┐
 ▼                                 ▼
DOMAIN TWINS                    UNITY V2
what exists / state            robot physics
 │                              viewpoint
 └──────────────┬───────────────┘
                ▼
         SENSOR OBSERVATIONS
                │
                ▼
              ECMER
                │
                ▼
          MODEL 2 FAMILY
## Core simulator architecture
I want Unity V2 divided into eight subsystems:
UNITY ROBOT DIGITAL TWIN V2
1. Vehicle Dynamics
2. Hydrodynamics
3. Propulsion / Actuation
4. Environment Interaction
5. Sensor Simulation
6. Robot Hardware Simulation
7. Mission / Experiment Runtime
8. External Interfaces + HIL
This modularity matters because individual components will progressively be replaced with measured hardware models.
## Six-degree-of-freedom dynamics
Robot state:
Equation. eta= [x,y,z,phi,theta,psi]^T
and body velocity:
Equation. nu= [u,v,w,p,q,r]^T.
General underwater vehicle dynamics:
Equation. boxed Mdotnu + C(nu)nu + D(nu)nu + g(eta) = tau+tau_env
where:
M: rigid-body + added-mass terms;
C: Coriolis/centripetal terms;
D: hydrodynamic damping;
g: restoring forces from gravity/buoyancy;
tau: propulsion;
tau_env: environmental disturbance.
This should be the physics foundation - not Unity’s default Rigidbody alone.
## Mass properties
Represent:
Equation. m, I, r_G, r_B
for:
mass;
inertia tensor;
center of gravity;
center of buoyancy.
These become configurable parameters.
Later your physical robot measurements replace initial estimates.
## Buoyancy
Gravity:
Equation. F_g=mg.
Buoyancy:
Equation. F_b=rho gV.
The separation:
Equation. r_B-r_G
creates restoring moments.
Therefore Unity should allow us to experiment with:
ballast placement
battery position
payload position
sensor modules
buoyancy foam
before physically rebuilding the vehicle.
This directly supports your hardware work.
## Hydrodynamic drag
At minimum:
Equation. F_D= -frac12rho C_DA|v|v.
But the robot isn’t isotropic.
We need separate approximate coefficients for:
Equation. x,y,z
and rotational damping.
Eventually:
Equation. D(nu)
can contain linear + quadratic terms.
Initial coefficients can be engineering estimates; later identified experimentally.
## Added mass
Accelerating underwater also accelerates surrounding water.
Therefore:
Equation. M=M_RB+M_A.
We shouldn’t ignore added mass if we’re calling this a meaningful digital twin.
Initial M_A can be approximate.
Later:
Equation. hat M_A
can be estimated through parameter identification.
## Currents
Environmental velocity field:
Equation. v_c(x,y,z,t).
Relative water velocity:
Equation. v_r=v_robot-v_c.
Hydrodynamic forces depend primarily on v_r, not merely world velocity.
Support:
constant current
depth-dependent current
spatial gradient
time-varying current
gust/disturbance
turbulent approximation
We don’t need full CFD in Unity.
## Environmental fields
Eventually Twin 2E may provide:
Equation. Phi(x,t).
Unity consumes physically relevant components such as current/turbidity.
Example:
Twin 2E
current field
     ↓
Unity
physical force on robot
Twin 2E
turbidity
     ↓
Unity
RGB visibility degradation
Same environmental truth affects both physics and sensing.
## Thruster model
Each thruster i:
Equation. T_i=f_i(command,omega,v_inflow,V_battery,…)
Initially approximate:
Equation. T_i=k_i u_i|u_i|.
But architecture must support measured lookup curves.
Eventually your bench testing provides:
Equation. command-> thrust
and possibly:
Equation. command-> current.
## Thruster dynamics
Don’t model:
Equation. command-> instantaneous thrust.
Use response dynamics:
Equation. tau_Tdot T_i+T_i=T_command.
Also support:
dead zone
saturation
forward/reverse asymmetry
latency
noise
These become extremely important for control sim-to-real.
## Thruster geometry
For thruster i:
Equation. tau_i= F_i r_ix F_i .
Therefore actual hardware:
position
orientation
maximum thrust
directly determines simulated control authority.
This lets you evaluate thruster layouts before finalizing hardware.
## Thruster failures
Inject:
complete failure
reduced thrust
stuck command
increased latency
intermittent failure
Navigation/control must detect and recover.
These become deterministic benchmark scenarios.
## Power simulation
Maintain approximate:
Equation. E_battery(t).
Thruster and compute loads consume power:
Equation. P_total = P_thrusters + P_compute + P_sensors + P_comms.
Then:
Equation. E(t+Delta t)=E(t)-P_totalDelta t.
This allows mission planning to reason about actual energy cost.
## Battery behavior
Initially simple:
capacity
SOC
voltage
power limit
Later potentially include:
discharge curve;
voltage sag;
temperature;
measured BMS behavior.
Again: progressively calibrated, not overengineered on Day 1.
## Sensor framework
Every simulated sensor implements a common interface:
SimSensor
sensor_id
modality
pose
frequency
latency
noise_model
health
sample(world, robot_state)
This should mirror the physical sensor abstraction.
## RGB camera
Model:
intrinsics
extrinsics
resolution
FPS
exposure
latency
Underwater degradation can include:
attenuation;
color shift;
backscatter;
turbidity;
blur;
low illumination;
sensor noise.
But photorealism is secondary to statistically useful sensor behavior.
## Depth/range
Support progressively:
ideal ray depth
noisy depth
range-dependent error
dropout
surface-dependent error
Useful early for 2S development.
## Sonar
Sonar deserves its own abstraction.
Early implementation can produce:
Equation. range/intensity
from geometry with simplified:
beam width;
range;
noise;
attenuation;
multipath approximation;
false returns.
Later fidelity can increase.
We should not pretend simplified Unity sonar is physically exact sonar.
## IMU
Generate:
Equation. a_m=a+b_a+n_a
Equation. omega_m=omega+b_g+n_g
with:
white noise;
bias;
bias drift;
sampling frequency;
latency.
Twin knows true state; navigation receives corrupted measurement.
Perfect for estimator evaluation.
## Pressure/depth sensor
Generate:
Equation. z_measured=z_true+b_z+n_z.
Support:
bias
noise
drift
latency
failure
## Sensor degradation
Sensor health evolves:
Equation. H_s(t).
Possible injected problems:
camera fouling
increasing blur
sonar noise increase
IMU drift
depth bias
packet dropout
This feeds ECMER’s sensor-quality reasoning.
## True state vs reported state
Unity maintains:
Equation. X_t^true.
Robot software receives only:
Equation. O_t.
Same rule as our domain twins.
Navigation cannot query:
unity.transform.position
during a realistic evaluation and call that localization.
Truth APIs must be restricted to training/evaluation infrastructure.
## Shared Scenario Loader
Unity loads the same:
Equation. ScenarioDefinition
used by domain twins.
Example:
scenario_0042
world:
    pipeline P3
    seabed
    rocks
2S:
    geometry
2T:
    degradation states
2E:
    ecology + environment
robot:
    configuration
mission:
    inspect P3
This is how we maintain one world.
## Unity ↔ Twin 2S
Twin 2S owns canonical spatial truth.
Unity consumes geometry needed for:
collision
rendering
raycasting
sensor visibility
navigation environment
Unity can instantiate a render/physics representation of that truth.
But Twin 2S remains canonical.
## Unity ↔ Twin 2T
Twin 2T says:
Weld17:
material loss = ...
surface state = ...
Unity uses whatever part is sensor-visible to alter:
surface geometry
appearance
sensor response
when scientifically justified.
Unity does not independently decide how corrosion progresses.
## Unity ↔ Twin 2E
Twin 2E provides:
ecological entities
environmental fields
bio-growth
turbidity
current
Unity maps these into:
visible geometry
camera conditions
sonar effects
robot disturbances
where relevant.
Again, ecological evolution remains Twin 2E’s job.
## Mission runtime
A mission object:
Mission
mission_id
objectives
targets
start_conditions
completion_conditions
time_limit
energy_limit
allowed_region
required_inspections
failure_conditions
Model 1 receives the mission contract.
Unity evaluates physical execution.
## Experiment configuration
Every experiment gets a reproducible configuration:
ExperimentConfig
scenario_seed
robot_config
physics_config
sensor_config
noise_config
failure_config
mission_config
model_versions
controller_version
Run:
Equation. Experiment(config,seed).
## Deterministic replay
Where feasible, identical:
Equation. seed+config
should reproduce identical environmental initialization and fault schedules.
This lets us compare:
Controller A
vs
Controller B
on exactly the same conditions.
Critical for research.
## Domain randomization
Randomize:
Equation. thetasim P(Theta)
including:
mass
CoM
CoB
drag
added mass
thruster strength
thruster latency
current
sensor noise
sensor latency
sensor calibration
lighting
turbidity
This trains robustness against imperfect simulation.
## Structured randomization
But not everything independently random.
Example:
increasing battery payload changes both mass and CoG.
Parameters should preserve physical relationships where known.
Otherwise domain randomization generates impossible robots.
## Parameter identification
One of Unity V2’s most important features.
Unknown parameters:
Equation. theta= [ C_D,M_A,k_T,tau_T,… ].
Collect physical experiment data:
Equation. D_real= _t,x_t.
Then solve:
Equation. theta^* = argmin_theta sum_t | x_t^real - x_t^sim(theta) |^2.
Potentially with more sophisticated system identification later.
## Parameter uncertainty
Don’t pretend identified parameters are exact.
Store:
Equation. P(theta)
or practical confidence intervals/ranges.
Then domain randomization samples around experimentally plausible values.
This is much stronger than one manually tuned “realistic” robot.
## Hardware characterization pipeline
Your hardware side feeds the twin through:
PHYSICAL ROBOT
     │
     ├── mass
     ├── inertia estimate
     ├── CoM / CoB
     ├── thruster curves
     ├── power curves
     ├── sensor noise
     └── latency
          │
          ▼
PARAMETER IDENTIFICATION
          │
          ▼
      UNITY V2
Then Unity becomes progressively closer to the actual robot.
## Hardware-in-the-loop
Eventually:
UNITY WORLD
    │
 simulated sensors
    ▼
REAL EMBEDDED COMPUTER
    │
real navigation software
    │
thruster commands
    ▼
UNITY ROBOT
This tests the actual deployed software stack before underwater trials.
Later individual real hardware components can enter the loop.
## Same RobotHardwareInterface
This is critical.
Simulation:
UnityRobotHardware : RobotHardwareInterface
Physical robot:
PhysicalRobotHardware : RobotHardwareInterface
Everything above the abstraction:
navigation
control
Model 1
Model 2
logging
should require minimal/no architectural change between them.
## Python interface
Unity needs an external interface to our ML/research stack.
Conceptually:
reset(config)
step(action)
get_observations()
get_robot_state_estimate()
get_mission_state()
get_link_state()
request_sensor(...)
inject_fault(...)
get_metrics()
Training/evaluation-only API additionally gets:
get_ground_truth()
Strictly separated.
Implementation technology can later be:
sockets;
gRPC;
shared memory;
ROS2 bridge;
Unity ML-Agents where appropriate.
We don’t lock that yet.
## Simulation frequencies
Different systems operate at different rates.
Conceptually:
Equation. f_physicsgg f_controlgg f_Model1.
Example architecture:
Physics           very high rate
Control           high rate
State estimation  high rate
Sensors           modality-specific
2S                medium/high
2T/E              event/sensor dependent
Model 1           lower/event-driven
Exact numbers come later.
## RL interface
Unity exposes:
Equation. s_t,a_t,r_t,s_t+1.
But RL environments should be purpose-specific.
Examples:
station keeping
pipeline following
current rejection
local obstacle avoidance
inspection trajectory
recovery
Don’t train one giant “underwater AGI policy.”

## Active Intelligence integration
MCBR outputs desired observation.
Unity provides candidate physical execution.
We can measure actual:
Equation. IG_actual.
Thus:

      ↓

      ↓

      ↓

      ↓

This closes our active sensing research loop.
## Simulation truth logging
Every timestep can log:
true robot state

This produces extremely rich experiment traces.
## Simulation validity levels
We should explicitly classify scenarios:
### L0 - functional simulation
Enough to test software interfaces.
### L1 - approximate physics
Engineering estimates.
### L2 - characterized simulation
Major hardware parameters measured.
### L3 - identified twin
Parameters fitted against physical trajectories.
### L4 - validated operating envelope
Simulation errors quantified against physical experiments across defined conditions.
We should never casually call L1 “validated digital twin.”
## Reality-gap metrics
For trajectory:
Equation. E_traj = RMSE( X^sim, X^real ).
For sensors:
Equation. D_sensor = D( P(O^sim), P(O^real) ).
For energy:
Equation. E_P= |Psim-Preal|.
For control:
Equation. Delta Performance = Performance_sim - Performance_real.
Quantify the gap instead of saying “sim-to-real worked.”

This is a much more executable roadmap.
## What is proprietary here?
Unity itself obviously isn’t our IP.

plus integration with:

and potentially learned residual dynamics/control if experiments support it.
Don’t try to claim “we built an underwater simulator” as novelty.
