# 22. Hardware Characterization, HIL and Sim-to-Real
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source d11b5930. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Ownership Classification and Handoff
HARDWARE OWNER: BURAK
SOFTWARE STATUS: REFERENCE / INTERFACE CONTEXT
Physical characterization, bench work, calibration, hardware access and physical debugging are Burak-owned. The retained measurements are mandatory software inputs. Kerem owns parameter-data ingestion, Unity/sensor-model configuration, estimation and control use of those parameters, HIL harness software, logging, replay, evaluation and experiment analysis. HIL, sim-to-real and controlled physical validation are shared closed-loop activities; their respective physical and software deliverables must be separately recorded.
The required handoff is PHYSICAL ROBOT + ROBOT CONFIGURATION + CALIBRATION + CHARACTERIZATION DATA + HARDWARE INTERFACE. Every record must state units, frames, acquisition time, uncertainty/range, method and provenance. Missing measurements remain OPEN; vendor nominal values are initial labeled references only.
## Architecture
REAL WORLD
                         ▲
                         │
              ┌──────────┼──────────┐
              │          │          │
           DOMAIN      SENSOR      ROBOT
             GAP         GAP        GAP
              │          │          │
              ▼          ▼          ▼
          Twin 2T/E/S   SensorSim  Unity V2
              └──────────┼──────────┘
                         │
                         ▼
                  SYNTHETIC SYSTEM
We need separate mechanisms and metrics for each gap.
## Fundamental rule
Synthetic data is not evidence of real-world performance.
Therefore:
Equation. boxed Synthetic Training != Real Validation
The proper sequence is:
Equation. synthetic development -> public real adaptation -> held-out real validation.
For the physical robot:
Equation. simulation -> bench -> controlled water -> broader operating envelope.
## Two transfer branches
Our architecture should explicitly separate:
### Intelligence transfer
Equation. Twin 2T/E/S -> Model 2T/E/S -> real public data
### Robotics transfer
Equation. Unity -> Navigation/Control -> physical robot.
They later meet in full-system validation.
## Domain-gap decomposition
For each child d:
Equation. G_d= D(P_syntheticd,P_reald).
But we shouldn’t reduce this to one number.
For 2T, for example:
geometry gap
defect/degradation gap
material gap
environment gap
temporal-dynamics gap
relationship/topology gap
2E:
ecological distribution gap
environmental-field gap
interaction gap
temporal gap
disturbance/recovery gap
2S:
geometry gap
clutter gap
topology gap
visibility gap
dynamic-object gap
trajectory gap
This gives us something we can actually debug.
## Sensor-gap decomposition
For modality m:
Equation. G_sensor^m.
### RGB
Potential gaps:
attenuation;
color response;
backscatter;
lighting;
blur;
fouling;
camera response;
compression.
### Sonar
noise;
multipath;
beam behavior;
material response;
range effects;
artifacts.
### IMU
bias;
drift;
vibration;
timing.
### Depth
bias;
noise;
latency.
Each sensor needs its own calibration strategy.
## Robot-gap decomposition
For Unity:
Equation. G_R=  G_mass, G_hydro, G_thruster, G_power, G_latency, G_disturbance .
This connects directly to our parameter-identification architecture.
Instead of asking:
Is Unity realistic?
ask:
Which physical parameter family is causing trajectory disagreement?
## Candidate overall mechanism
I would call the architecture internally:
Reality-Gap Decomposition and Adaptation - RGDA
Not necessarily something we’d ever market or patent.
Its principle is:
Measure reality gaps at the subsystem level, apply targeted adaptation/calibration, and evaluate whether each intervention actually improves held-out real performance.
This is much stronger than indiscriminate domain randomization.
## Synthetic randomization
For synthetic parameter vector:
Equation. thetasim P(Theta).
Randomize known sources of variability.
But:
Randomize uncertainty, not nonsense.
Ranges should come from:
published literature;
public real data;
manufacturer specifications;
physical measurements;
justified engineering bounds.
## Structured domain randomization
Parameters often correlate.
Example:
Equation. payloaduparrow => massuparrow, CoM changes, power demand may change.
Therefore sample from:
Equation. P(theta_1,theta_2,…)
rather than independent uniform distributions everywhere.
Same for ecology and structural worlds.
## Progressive randomization
Don’t start training in maximum chaos.
Curriculum:
Equation. Theta_0subsetTheta_1subset…subsetTheta_n.
Example:
clean nominal simulation
        ↓
small parameter variation
        ↓
sensor noise
        ↓
environment variation
        ↓
failures
        ↓
large domain shift
        ↓
OOD conditions
This helps distinguish learning failure from robustness failure.
## Domain randomization is not enough
A common trap:
randomize everything and hope reality is somewhere inside.
We need several complementary strategies:
Equation. boxed Randomization + Calibration + Adaptation + OOD Detection + Real Validation
## Synthetic → real representation transfer
For Model 2 encoders, synthetic and real observations should ideally produce useful compatible representations.
Potential strategies to experimentally compare:
### Synthetic pretrain → real fine-tune
Equation. D_syn-> D_real.
### Mixed training
Equation. D=D_syncup D_real.
### Self-supervised real adaptation
Use unlabeled real sequences.
### Feature alignment
Encourage synthetic/real latent distributions to align.
### Domain-adversarial training
Learn representations that make domain identity harder to distinguish where appropriate.
### Style/sensor randomization
Increase synthetic observation diversity.
We do not lock one before experiments.
## Why self-supervised adaptation matters
Public underwater datasets may have huge quantities of observations but limited labels.
That’s fine.
ECMER can learn from:
temporal correspondence
cross-view correspondence
cross-modal correspondence
geometry consistency
masked reconstruction
without needing manual defect/ecology labels for everything.
This is important for our no-self-collected-dataset constraint.
## Keep the real test set untouched
For each domain:
PUBLIC REAL DATA
├── adaptation/train
├── validation
└── FINAL REAL TEST
The final real test:
must never drive architecture selection.
Otherwise we simply overfit our research decisions to the test set.
## Synthetic splits
Likewise:
SYNTHETIC
├── training worlds
├── validation worlds
└── OOD worlds
OOD worlds deliberately contain combinations not present during training.
Example 2S:
unseen infrastructure geometry
+
unseen turbidity
+
pose degradation
## Synthetic-real mixing ratio
We should explicitly experiment with:
Equation. r= fracN_syntheticN_real.
Try regimes such as:
Equation. 100:0, 90:10, 50:50, 10:90, 0:100
where datasets make this feasible.
Question:
Does synthetic data actually add value beyond public real data?
That is a very important experiment.
## Twin usefulness metric
For child d:
Equation. TU_d= Performance_real(Real+Twin) - Performance_real(RealOnly).
If:
Equation. TU_dle0,
our twin isn’t helping that task yet.
That’s a powerful falsification criterion.
## Synthetic pretraining usefulness
Likewise compare:
Equation. RandomInit
vs:
Equation. GenericPretrain
vs:
Equation. TwinPretrain
vs:
Equation. Generic+Twin
followed by identical real adaptation.
This tells us whether the domain twin contributes meaningful representations.
## Mechanism-level transfer
We should not only evaluate final task performance.
For BUO, test whether synthetic-trained behavior transfers:
good evidence → stronger appropriate update
bad evidence → weak update
credible contradiction → contradiction uncertainty
redundant evidence → limited certainty gain
on public real observations.
Similarly for RBP, TBD, PMBL and ECMER.
## Uncertainty is our reality-gap sensor
This is particularly important.
When encountering real data far from synthetic training:
Equation. U_E
should ideally rise.
Thus:
Domain shift should appear as epistemic uncertainty before it becomes silent failure.
This gives uncertainty architecture a real operational purpose.
## OOD benchmark
Create controlled shifts:
Equation. D_train-> D_shift.
Measure:
task error;
U_E;
calibration;
selective prediction.
A good model should show:
Equation. Erroruparrow => U_Euparrow
approximately.
A dangerous model shows:
Equation. Erroruparrow, U_Edownarrow.
## Selective prediction
Model can abstain:
Equation. Predict or INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE/OOD.
Evaluate risk-coverage:
Equation. Risk(Coverage).
As we permit the model to abstain on increasingly uncertain samples, error on retained predictions should decrease.
## Sensor calibration pipeline
Once physical sensors exist:
REAL SENSOR
    │
    ▼
controlled measurements
    │
    ▼
noise / bias / latency statistics
    │
    ▼
fit sensor model
    │
    ▼
Unity / Twin observation model
Then rerun synthetic→real comparison.
## Camera calibration
Measure:
intrinsics
distortion
noise
exposure behavior
latency
underwater color/attenuation behavior
where practical.
Simulation parameters become distributions rather than guesses.
## IMU/depth calibration
Estimate:
Equation. bias, noise, drift, latency.
Use stationary and controlled-motion experiments.
Feed results to:
Unity;
state-estimation simulation;
domain randomization.
## Sonar calibration
When sonar hardware exists, compare real vs simulated:
range accuracy
noise
resolution
response vs distance
angle dependence
artifact frequency
Then refine the sensor model.
Sonar is likely one of our larger sensor reality gaps.
## Robot parameter identification
Already architected:
Equation. theta^* = argmin_theta L( X^real, X^sim(theta) ).
Parameters might include:
Equation. theta= [ C_D, M_A, k_thruster, tau_thruster, CoM, CoB,… ].
## Identification experiment sequence
Don’t attempt to fit everything from arbitrary trajectories.
Use targeted experiments.
### Experiment A - static buoyancy/trim
Estimate buoyancy balance and restoring behavior.
### B - individual thrusters
Thrust/current response.
### C - straight-line acceleration
Estimate longitudinal dynamics.
### D - lateral/vertical motion
Other-axis drag/dynamics.
### E - rotational maneuvers
Rotational damping/inertia.
### F - station keeping/current
Disturbance behavior.
This makes parameter identification much better conditioned.
## Validation trajectories
Critically:
Equation. D_identify!= D_validate.
After fitting parameters using one set of maneuvers, evaluate on different trajectories.
Otherwise we’re only measuring curve fitting.
## Residual dynamics
After physics calibration:
Equation. x_t+1 = f_physics(x_t,u_t;theta) + r_phi(x_t,u_t).
Only introduce learned residual:
Equation. r_phi
if systematic real-vs-sim error remains and sufficient data exists.
Then compare:
Equation. physics
against:
Equation. physics+learned residual.
## Robust controller transfer
Controller should train/tune over:
Equation. thetasim P(Theta_identified)
rather than only theta^*.
This helps ensure:
Equation. Controller
works across plausible real robot variation.
## Sim-to-real ladder
For the physical autonomy stack:
### R0 - pure simulation
Unity only.
### R1 - software-in-the-loop
Actual autonomy software against Unity.
### R2 - hardware-in-the-loop
Actual embedded computer/controllers.
### R3 - dry bench
Real sensors/compute/thrusters where safe.
### R4 - controlled water
Pool/tank-like environment.
### R5 - controlled representative environment
Increasing complexity.
### R6 - target operating envelope
Only after earlier stages pass.
This should become part of the eventual production roadmap.
## Hardware acceptance gates
Example:
Navigation doesn’t advance simply because:
it worked in Unity.
Gate might require:
Equation. RMSE_real<theta_p
Equation. collision rate=0
under defined controlled conditions, plus bounded sim-real degradation.
Exact thresholds later.
## Model 2 transfer ladder
Similarly:
### M0
Synthetic-only training/evaluation.
### M1
Synthetic training → public real zero-shot.
### M2
Synthetic + public real adaptation.
### M3
Held-out public real evaluation.
### M4
Controlled robot-generated data later.
### M5
Integrated physical inspection evaluation.
Claims should always state which level we’ve reached.
## Domain adaptation must preserve uncertainty
Potential danger:
A feature-alignment method may improve average accuracy but destroy epistemic uncertainty.
Therefore every adaptation experiment measures:
Equation. Accuracy
and:
Equation. Calibration/OOD.
We reject adaptations that make the model confidently wrong.
## Domain adaptation must preserve provenance
Same for ECMER.
We should not introduce an adaptation architecture that turns:
RGB + sonar + pose
into an opaque fused latent with no source distinction.
Synthetic→real improvements cannot violate our core evidence architecture.
## Twin calibration loop
For each domain twin:
PUBLIC REAL DATA
       │
       ▼
distribution analysis
       │
       ▼
TWIN PARAMETERS
       │
       ▼
synthetic dataset
       │
       ▼
MODEL TRAINING
       │
       ▼
held-out real evaluation
       │
       ▼
gap analysis
       │
       └──────► refine twin
But never refine against the final test set.
## Reality-gap registry
I strongly want this in the project.
RealityGapRecord
gap_id
domain
synthetic_distribution
real_distribution
metric
current_gap
suspected_causes
intervention
before_result
after_result
confidence
Instead of vaguely saying:
sonar simulation isn’t realistic,
we track actual engineering/research problems.
## Dataset provenance
Every real and synthetic sample should know:
source
license
dataset_version
scenario/twin version
sensor
domain
split
transformations
corruptions
adaptation use
Absolutely essential once we start mixing many public datasets.
## Prevent synthetic leakage
If procedural generator creates nearly identical worlds across train/test, synthetic performance becomes misleading.
Split by:
world/scenario families
not individual frames.
Same for sequential public data:
Don’t randomly split adjacent video frames across train and test.
Split by mission/site/sequence where possible.
## Cross-domain reality gap
Eventually the models interact.
Potential issue:
2T individually works.
2E individually works.
2S individually works.
But cross-domain context causes failure.
Therefore integrated evaluation needs scenarios like:
marine growth
    ↓
reduced visual observability
    ↓
2S uncertainty
    ↓
2T inspection uncertainty
and test whether real/public evidence produces the same sensible interactions.
## Model 1 sim-to-real
Model 1 is different.
It isn’t primarily transferring visual features.
Its gap is:
Equation. boxedsimulation decision distribution->real mission distribution
Therefore train with enormous scenario diversity and evaluate under:
belief errors;
miscalibration;
missing models;
sensor failures;
unexpected events.
Model 1 must never assume Model 2 outputs are perfect merely because twin training made them clean.
## Inject imperfect Model 2 outputs
During Model 1 training:
Equation. tilde B=B+epsilon_B.
Inject:
miscalibration
missing beliefs
false associations
stale beliefs
contradictions
OOD flags
incorrect predictions
within plausible ranges.
Otherwise Model 1 learns to operate only with unrealistically perfect child models.
## Active Intelligence transfer
MCBR trains heavily with oracle Twin information.
Danger:
Real deployment lacks perfect visibility/value predictions.
Therefore perturb:
Equation. hat V(a)
and observability predictions during training.
Evaluate whether active sensing remains better than classical next-best-view methods on held-out real/semi-real scenarios.
## BAAC transfer
Likewise channel simulator parameters must eventually be calibrated using physical communications tests.
Train BAAC over distributions of:
Equation. bandwidth, latency, loss, dropout.
Then evaluate on channel traces not used for tuning.
## Full integrated validation matrix
Eventually:
 | Synthetic world | Public real data | Physical robot
ECMER | ✓ | ✓ | ✓
2T | ✓ | ✓ | later ✓
2E | ✓ | ✓ | later ✓
2S | ✓ | ✓ | ✓
Model 1 | ✓ | scenario-based | ✓
Active Intelligence | ✓ | partial | ✓
Navigation | Unity | public where useful | ✓
BAAC | channel sim | traces where available | ✓
Not every component needs identical validation sources.
## Claims ladder
This will protect the project scientifically.
We distinguish:
Implemented
Validated in simulation
Validated on public real data
Validated on controlled hardware
Validated in representative environment
These are not interchangeable.
## Acceptance philosophy
A module does not pass because:
loss went down.
It passes only if:
Equation. boxed Performance + Calibration + Robustness + Transfer
meet predefined criteria.
For physical modules add:
Equation. boxedSafety+Reality Gap.
## RGDA experimental loop
The full process becomes:
DEFINE REALITY GAP
        │
        ▼
MEASURE BASELINE
        │
        ▼
SELECT INTERVENTION
        │
 ┌──────┼────────┐
 ▼      ▼        ▼
randomize calibrate adapt
 └──────┼────────┘
        ▼
HELD-OUT EVALUATION
        │
        ▼
DID THE GAP ACTUALLY SHRINK?
      /   \
    YES    NO
     │      │
   keep   reject/revise
This is exactly how we avoid novelty theater in sim-to-real.
## Full synthetic-real architecture
We can now connect everything:
PUBLIC REAL DATA
                    │       │
             calibration  adaptation
                    │       │
                    ▼       ▼
SHARED SCENARIO → DOMAIN TWINS → MODEL 2
      │                              │
      ▼                              ▼
   UNITY V2                     BELIEF WORLD
      │                              │
      ▼                              ▼
Synthetic Sensors                MODEL 1
      │                         /    |    \
      └──────► ECMER        MCBR   BAAC   NAV
                                      │      │
                                      └──┬───┘
                                         ▼
                                    UNITY / ROBOT
                                         │
                                         ▼
                                  PHYSICAL TESTING
                                         │
                                         ▼
                                REALITY-GAP REGISTRY
                                         │
                                         └──► calibration /
                                              adaptation
# 23. Training Architecture and Synthetic/Public-Real Supervision
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source c8fbc0e5. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Three categories of data
### A. Public real-world data
This prevents us from becoming simulator-dependent.
For 2T, we should source public datasets covering underwater infrastructure imagery where available, corrosion, cracks, structural defects, NDT/SHM measurements, pipelines/offshore structures, materials degradation and longitudinal structural monitoring.
For 2E, public sources should cover coral/benthic imagery, marine species/habitats, seabed surveys, oceanographic time series, temperature/salinity/turbidity/current data, ecological monitoring and longitudinal environmental observations.
For 2S, we want public underwater SLAM/visual-inertial datasets, sonar datasets, underwater RGB/depth imagery, AUV/ROV trajectories, 3D reconstruction/mapping datasets and bathymetric/seafloor data.
We will need a proper dataset survey before implementation to select exact datasets based on licensing, sensor modalities, labels, geographic diversity and quality. I don’t want to invent dataset names now and lock ourselves into bad choices.
### B. Twin-generated synthetic data
This is where we get something public data usually cannot provide:
complete hidden-state ground truth
For every synthetic sequence we can store:
scenario ID
true state S₀
observation O₀
true state S₁
observation O₁
...
true state Sₙ
observation Oₙ
plus:
which areas were visible
which evidence was corrupted
sensor quality
true relationships
true uncertainty-generating conditions
environment
events
time intervals
That is essentially the laboratory in which we teach BUO/RBP/TBD.
### C. Procedurally corrupted data
Take both real and synthetic observations and deliberately create difficult evidence:
blur
noise
occlusion
low visibility
missing modality
sensor dropout
temporal gaps
redundant observations
contradictory observations
pose error
variable quality
domain shift
And importantly, we know what corruption we introduced.
That’s useful for training trust and uncertainty mechanisms.
## Stage I - Representation pretraining
We don’t immediately train the whole PBA.
First teach modality encoders useful representations.
For example:
RGB ───► Visual Encoder ──► embedding
Sonar ─► Sonar Encoder ───► embedding
Sensor vector ─────────────► embedding
We can use existing pretrained encoders as baselines or initialization where justified, but the proprietary contribution should not be “we fine-tuned an existing model.”
Training can use self-supervision heavily because public datasets don’t all need perfect labels.
Potential objectives:
masked reconstruction,
temporal correspondence,
cross-view correspondence,
contrastive learning,
cross-modal alignment,
geometry consistency.
This gives us useful evidence representations before belief learning begins.
## Stage II - Train the Belief Update Operator
Now Twin data becomes extremely useful.
Construct controlled sequences.
Example:
TRUE STATE = X
Observation 1:
good RGB
Observation 2:
bad sonar
Observation 3:
same RGB viewpoint
Observation 4:
high-quality contradictory evidence
Observation 5:
independent confirmation
At every point, the twin still knows:
Equation. S_t.
Train:
Equation. B_t=BUO(B_t-1,E_t)
such that decoded belief approaches:
Equation. hat S_t~= S_t.
But also teach expected behavior under controlled evidence conditions.
This is how trust, innovation and contradiction handling become learned rather than arbitrary rules.
## Stage III - Train RBP
Now create worlds with known relationships.
Hide information about some cells.
For example:
B1 ─── B2 ─── B3
observe B1 strongly
observe B2 weakly
do not observe B3
The model must determine whether information about B1/B2 legitimately helps estimate B3.
Because the twin knows all three true states, we can calculate whether propagation actually helped.
Training encourages:
Equation. useful propagation
while penalizing:
Equation. unsupported propagation.
This is important - we don’t want RBP learning that spreading information everywhere reduces average loss.
## Stage IV - Train TBD
Now generate trajectories:
Equation. S_0-> S_1-> S_2->…S_n.
But hide intermediate observations:
O₀      ?      ?      O₃      ?      O₅
Ask TBD to predict:
Equation. hat S_1,hat S_2,hat S_3,…
from previous beliefs/context.
Randomize Delta t.
Randomize environmental events.
Randomize change rates.
This teaches Model 2 how uncertainty and predictions should behave over time.
## Stage V - Joint training
Only after individual mechanisms behave properly do we run:
Observation
    ↓
BUO
    ↓
RBP
    ↓
TBD
    ↓
next observation
    ↓
BUO
    ↓
...
end-to-end across long sequences.
Now gradients can optimize the complete system.
But I’d probably retain auxiliary losses on intermediate components so everything doesn’t collapse into an uninterpretable end-to-end latent solution.
## Stage VI - synthetic → real
This is absolutely essential.
Initially:
Equation. P_synthetic!= P_real.
So after twin-heavy training, expose Model 2 to real public datasets.
Potential methods we should experimentally compare include:
mixed real + synthetic batches
synthetic pretrain → real fine-tune
domain-adversarial alignment
self-supervised real-data adaptation
feature-distribution alignment
pseudo-labeling where appropriate
sensor-style randomization
I would not decide today that one particular domain-adaptation technique is correct. That’s experimental.
## The children follow the same curriculum
But their twins provide different supervision.
### 2T
Twin 2T
    ↓
structural histories
component states
relationships
degradation trajectories
failure scenarios
partial inspections
    ↓
2T training
Then adapt/validate using public structural/infrastructure data.
### 2E
Twin 2E
    ↓
ecological states
environmental fields
entity relationships
disturbances
recovery/change
partial surveys
    ↓
2E training
Then public marine/ecological/oceanographic data.
### 2S
Twin 2S
    ↓
exact geometry
semantics
topology
visibility
occlusion
dynamic objects
trajectories
    ↓
2S training
Then public underwater mapping/SLAM/sonar data.
## What exactly is supervised?
This is important.
We don’t want one giant:
Equation. L=MSE.
Eventually the training objective will resemble:
Equation. mathcal L = lambda_sL_state +lambda_bL_belief +lambda_uL_uncertainty +lambda_dL_dynamics +lambda_rL_relations +lambda_cL_consistency +lambda_pL_provenance +lambda_realL_domain
Exact terms depend on the child.
For instance, 2S will need geometry/spatial losses that make no sense for 2E.
We’ll define these when architecting each child.
## Real data is used twice
An important methodological point:
We should split suitable public real datasets into:
REAL DATA
├── adaptation/training subset
│
├── validation subset
│
└── final untouched test subset
The final test data should never participate in architecture decisions.
Otherwise we’ll gradually optimize against our supposed validation benchmark.
## Twin data also needs held-out worlds
Same rule.
Don’t train on procedural generator settings and then evaluate on nearly identical generated worlds.
We need:
Synthetic Training Worlds
Synthetic Validation Worlds
Synthetic OOD Worlds
OOD worlds deliberately contain unseen:
geometries
relationships
environment conditions
degradation combinations
ecosystem configurations
sensor conditions
depending on the child.
## Overall training system
So the production architecture should ultimately contain:
DATA SOURCES
 PUBLIC REAL               DOMAIN TWIN
      │                        │
      ▼                        ▼
Real observations       Synthetic worlds
      │                        │
      └──────────┬─────────────┘
                 ▼
         DATASET REGISTRY
                 │
                 ▼
      corruption/randomization
                 │
                 ▼
       representation pretrain
                 │
                 ▼
            BUO training
                 │
                 ▼
            RBP training
                 │
                 ▼
            TBD training
                 │
                 ▼
          joint training
                 │
                 ▼
        synthetic→real adaptation
                 │
                 ▼
       ┌─────────┴─────────┐
       ▼                   ▼
Synthetic/OOD        Held-out real
evaluation             evaluation
# 24. Dataset Production Registry
Candidate names below are inherited from the design discussion. The registry is a procurement and adapter specification, not a claim that data has been downloaded, licensed, labeled or accepted for training. Dataset-specific validation is required before a manifest becomes APPROVED. Repository software licenses must not automatically be applied to separately hosted data assets.
## Verified Source Pointers
The following primary sources were checked on 17 September 2026. They establish the identity and broad purpose of the candidates; they do not complete the production manifest audit.
Candidate | Intended Conrad role | Required remaining verification
SubPipe | Underwater pipeline segmentation, visual-inertial localization and sonar perception; candidate 2S/ECMER data. | Pin release/assets; verify data terms, timestamp alignment, calibration and split boundaries. Do not infer true material loss or fatigue labels from pipeline segmentation.
AQUALOC | Visual-inertial-pressure localization; candidate state-estimation and spatial sequence evaluation. | Inspect sequence truth quality, units, time offsets, calibration, availability and license.
BenthicNet seafloor imagery | Ecological representation and benthic classification; candidate ECMER-E/2E pretraining. | Pin subset and annotations; audit contributor-level rights and site-separated splits. Static imagery does not establish longitudinal entity-field dynamics.
FathomNet terms and data-use policy | Marine visual recognition and open-world ecological representation. | Record image and annotation rights separately at asset level; licenses vary by contribution. Confirm intended training, redistribution and deployment uses against applicable terms.
The BenthicNet above is the seafloor-image compilation described in Scientific Data, not an unrelated freshwater occurrence portal with the same name.
## Candidates Requiring Source Audit
Candidate from discussion | Candidate task | Status / audit task
UVVID | Underwater visual/visual-inertial sequences | OPEN: identify exact release, sensors, labels, calibration and terms.
Underwater Caves sonar datasets | Spatial mapping, sonar geometry and sequence robustness | OPEN: identify exact dataset and truth support; document operating-domain mismatch.
UW-SLAM | Underwater localization/spatial evaluation | OPEN: resolve exact publication/release and available truth.
Seaview | Ecological imagery and habitat representation | OPEN: exact dataset version, task labels, geography and asset rights.
SUIM | Underwater semantic segmentation | OPEN: inspect label taxonomy, split independence and license before adapter approval.
SeaClear | Potential marine-object perception baseline | OPEN: task-specific relevance and labels; never treat collection/annotation availability as structural hidden-state supervision.
Corrosion/crack/material-loss datasets | Direct structural representation and condition heads | OPEN: dedicated 2T search; inspect physical quantity, units, specimen/site IDs and label reliability.
NDT/SHM/fatigue datasets | Structural dynamics, hidden condition, temporal progression | OPEN: measurement modality, load history, failure censoring, sequence identity and domain transfer.
Oceanographic field/time-series datasets | 2E temperature, salinity, oxygen, current, turbidity/light | OPEN: spatial/depth/time support, missingness, calibration, rights and whether paired ecological observations exist.
No dataset in this table is silently substituted with a similarly named resource. DATA-VERIFY tasks produce a source URL, paper/release identifier, asset inventory, license evidence, task-label audit and adapter smoke test. Reject a candidate for a particular claim if its labels cannot support that claim, even if it is useful for representation pretraining.
## Manifest and Adapter Contract
dataset_id: OPEN
release_id: OPEN
source_url: OPEN
retrieved_at: OPEN
manifest_version: 1
status: CANDIDATE
asset_inventory_ref: OPEN
asset_checksums_ref: OPEN
license_evidence_ref: OPEN
rights_by_asset_ref: OPEN
modalities: []
label_schema_ref: OPEN
label_quality_report_ref: OPEN
time_units: OPEN
coordinate_frames_ref: OPEN
calibration_ref: OPEN
missingness_policy: OPEN
split_manifest_ref: OPEN
split_unit: site_or_mission_or_asset_or_sequence
duplicate_audit_ref: OPEN
adapter_version: OPEN
transform_config_hash: OPEN
permitted_tasks: []
unsupported_claims: []
class DatasetAdapter:
    def inspect(self, manifest) -> DatasetAudit: ...
    def iter_sequences(self, split) -> Iterator[SequenceSample]: ...
    def normalize_observation(self, raw) -> Observation: ...
    def map_labels(self, raw_labels) -> PartialTruth: ...

class PartialTruth:
    targets: dict[str, Tensor]
    availability_masks: dict[str, BoolTensor]
    label_source_refs: dict[str, str]
    quality_metadata: dict
Each adapter must test decoding, determinism, units, timestamp ordering, coordinate conversion, missing modalities, label masks and split isolation. A randomly shuffled frame split from a single dive is not an independent real-world generalization test. Synthetic worlds need held-out world families, seeds, geometry templates and trajectories; nuisance textures alone are an insufficient OOD split.
Maintain three distinct purposes: twin calibration, model adaptation and final held-out evaluation. The final real test cannot be reused to tune twin parameters. Paired ecology/field training requires genuinely paired observations or explicitly labeled synthetic pairs; unrelated public image and oceanographic records must not be presented as synchronized measurements.
## Dataset Acceptance Packages
DATA-VERIFY-2T closes the structural degradation/NDT/SHM manifest. DATA-VERIFY-2E closes the environmental and longitudinal manifest. DATA-VERIFY-2S closes spatial/sonar/localization candidates. DATA-RIGHTS-01 records asset-level terms. DATA-SPLIT-01 validates independence and duplicates. DATA-ADAPTER-01 verifies that real and synthetic data converge to the same Observation/Evidence interfaces.
These package IDs are production additions for organizing unresolved work, not claims that the tasks were completed during architecture design.

## Workstreams
Run six parallel streams.
Stream | Scope | Primary
A | Core intelligence infrastructure | Software

Your side also owns business/project integration.
The co-founder should be able to execute A-E without waiting for physical hardware.
## Definition of Done
Every component uses the same maturity ladder:
Equation. D0-> D8.
Level | Meaning
D0 | Specified
D1 | Implemented
D2 | Unit validated
D3 | Baseline validated
D4 | Integrated
D5 | Synthetic validated
D6 | Real-data validated
D7 | Physical validated
D8 | Research validated
Do not call D1 “done.”
## Freeze terminology
We also preserve three different freezes.
### Concept Freeze
Architecture/boundary stable.
### Interface Freeze
Other teams can safely code against API.
### Implementation Freeze
Exact algorithm selected after experiments.
Most of our system is now:
Concept Frozen
while many interfaces can soon become frozen.
Almost none of the frontier algorithms should yet be Implementation Frozen.

## PHASE 16 - Research Validation
Only now do we make serious claims.

## Mechanism validation matrix
Mechanism | Must beat / justify against
PBA | RSSM/recurrent/persistent graph
BUO | standard fusion/update
RBP | generic GNN/no propagation
TCDP | temporal/static GNN
MCDE | independent engineering degradation
CEFD | uncoupled/simple fusion
MEIFE | independent entity/field sim
UAHSM | conventional spatial memory
OCPWE | ordinary procedural worlds
EGDC | rules/planner/general agent baselines
MCBR | standard NBV/IG
BAAC | raw/FIFO/fixed semantic scheduling
A mechanism that doesn’t win anything meaningful gets simplified or removed.
## Synthetic → real gate
For each Model2 child:
Equation. TU_d = Perf_real(Twin+Real) - Perf_real(RealOnly).
We want:
Equation. TU_d>0
for at least the tasks used to justify that twin’s training contribution.
If not:
improve the twin or stop claiming it helps transfer.
## Research claims ladder
Every result is labeled:
IMPLEMENTED
VALIDATED IN SIMULATION
VALIDATED ON PUBLIC REAL DATA
VALIDATED ON CONTROLLED HARDWARE
VALIDATED IN REPRESENTATIVE ENVIRONMENT
This will keep the Conrad submission technically defensible.

              │                          │
              ├─ Model1/MCBR ────────────┤
              │                          │
              └─ BAAC ───────────────────┘
with interfaces providing synchronization points.

## Critical path
Despite the huge project, the actual critical path is:
Equation. Schemas -> Core -> 2S -> Unity -> Navigation -> 2T -> MCBR -> Model1 -> Integrated Mission
2E and BAAC can progress in parallel without blocking early physical autonomy.
This matters for scheduling.

## Remaining unresolved choices
The consolidation pass does not reveal another missing major subsystem.

exact pass thresholds after baseline measurements.
Those should not be artificially frozen before experimentation/hardware decisions.
# 26. Executable Phase 0-16 Work Packages
## Ownership Enforcement for the Roadmap
Every roadmap task is classified as Burak-owned physical hardware, Kerem-owned software/R&D, or shared integration. Physical construction, assembly, bench testing, calibration and physical characterization are Burak-owned. Intelligence, simulation, schemas, training/evaluation, data adapters, software state estimation/navigation/control/allocation/safety and experiment infrastructure are Kerem-owned. HIL, sim-to-real and controlled physical validation are shared, with the physical/software deliverable split recorded in acceptance evidence.
Critical dependencies are explicit: thruster characterization produces data for RobotConfig, Unity and software validation; sensor calibration produces data for sensor models, ECMER/state estimation and Unity; physical configuration/characterization constrain parameter identification, navigation/control and sim-to-real. No phase may treat a missing hardware measurement as a confirmed parameter.
OPEN / HUMAN REVIEW: firmware/driver ownership, exact physical test operator roles and any custom SoC boundary must be frozen in Interface V1 after selected hardware is known. They do not change the architectural ownership of the physical robot or software stack.
The phase numbers below match the final source roadmap. These are execution cards, not a serial staffing plan. Work proceeds in parallel where the dependencies allow it. Numeric tolerances and superiority margins remain OPEN until preregistered.
## Phase 0: Repository and Contracts
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. Burak reviews hardware-facing contract fields.
Inputs. Frozen architecture; source schemas; hardware interface requirements
Outputs. Versioned repository, schemas, CI, config/logging and RobotConfig validators
Implementation tasks. Implement the complete Phase 0 contract list. Test serialization, versions, UUIDs, provenance DAG, frames, time and uncertainty.
Training / test data. Schema fixtures only; no training
Baselines. Round-trip/reference fixtures
Experiments. INV-001..008; CC-01..10
Pass criteria. P0: contract suite passes; owners review Interface V1.
Dependencies. None; software leads with hardware review
## Phase 1: Fake Full System
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. Burak reviews representative hardware constraints only.
Inputs. P0 contracts
Outputs. Executable TEST_FIXTURE end-to-end loop and replay trace
Implementation tasks. Implement FakeTwin, sensors, ECMER, Model2, bus, Model1, MCBR, navigation, robot and BAAC; exercise an observation and command round trip.
Training / test data. Deterministic fixtures; no learned-model claims
Baselines. Expected event sequence
Experiments. I0 fixture run
Pass criteria. I0: every boundary carries valid IDs/time/frames/provenance; run terminates and replays.
Dependencies. Phase 0; software
## Phase 2: Model2 Core Sandbox
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. No physical build work; Burak supplies later measured constraints.
Inputs. I0; evidence and belief schemas
Outputs. Association/BUO/RBP/TBD/PMBL modules and baseline reports
Implementation tasks. Generate hidden-state sequences; implement no-match identity, credible contradiction, typed propagation, prediction/correction and persistence.
Training / test data. Abstract noisy/missing/conflicting observations with truth separate
Baselines. Latest-only; weighted fusion; GRU; generic GNN; hold-last
Experiments. CORE-ASSOC/BUO/UNC/RBP/TBD/PERSIST-E001; CORE-FULL-E001
Pass criteria. C1: all listed core behaviors and provenance work; comparisons recorded; no research win assumed.
Dependencies. Phase 1; software Core stream
## Phase 3: Domain Pairs Begin
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. Parallel domain R&D; Burak supplies no hidden truth or fabricated parameters.
Inputs. C1 API; domain state contracts
Outputs. Initial 2S/Twin2S, 2T/Twin2T and 2E/Twin2E packages
Implementation tasks. Prioritize procedural spatial worlds, observability, sparse memory and counterfactual pairs; scaffold the other two domain pairs.
Training / test data. Abstract domain observations before expensive rendering
Baselines. Occupancy/sparse map; independent components/entities
Experiments. 2S-E01..05; domain smoke tests
Pass criteria. 2S first gate: observed/inferred/unknown are distinct; ambiguity does not cause unjustified confidence.
Dependencies. Phase 2; parallel domain streams
## Phase 4: Unity V2.0
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. Burak supplies nominal then measured physical parameter inputs and reviews the adapter boundary.
Inputs. P0 RobotHardwareInterface; nominal parameter distributions
Outputs. Stable 6-DOF world, sensors and Python bridge
Implementation tasks. Implement buoyancy, basic drag, thrusters, camera/IMU/depth; reset, seed, timestamps and frame conversion.
Training / test data. Analytical/reference dynamics and sensor fixtures; no ML requirement
Baselines. Known-force and static-equilibrium reference cases
Experiments. U-D1..D3; U0
Pass criteria. U0: correct force/motion directions, stable integration, correct frames/time and replay within declared tolerance.
Dependencies. Can run alongside Phases 2-3; shared robotics ownership
## Phase 5: Spatial Intelligence and Robot
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. Burak supplies physical constraints through the declared contract.
Inputs. U0 and 2S first gate
Outputs. Twin2S -> Unity -> ECMER -> Model2S loop
Implementation tasks. Load canonical geometry; ingest simulated sensors through production interfaces; persist map and inspect truth separation.
Training / test data. Held-out synthetic worlds/trajectories

Pass criteria. I1: geometry, coverage, knowledge status, uncertainty and provenance exported without truth leakage.
Dependencies. Phases 3-4; software
## Phase 6: Navigation and Control
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. Burak supplies characterized actuator/sensor/power constraints; physical operation is not a software task.
Inputs. I1; estimated state; RobotConfig
Outputs. Goal/planner/trajectory/controller/allocator/safety stack

## Phase 7: Structural Intelligence
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. Burak provides physical inspection/sensor constraints when available.
Inputs. Core API; structural schema; 2S/Unity interfaces
Outputs. MCDE, 2T heads, TCDP, TBD-T and structural loop

## Phase 8: Active Intelligence
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. Burak provides feasible vehicle/sensor constraints through RobotCapabilities.
Inputs. 2S observability; 2T uncertainty; feasible motion cost
Outputs. InformationNeed, candidates, value ranking and ObservationPlan
Implementation tasks. Train oracle-supervised ranking; implement hypothesis discrimination, uncertainty-specific choices and stop conditions.
Training / test data. OCPWE counterfactual worlds and structural ambiguity sequences
Baselines. Fixed/random/coverage; geometric and entropy NBV; expected IG
Experiments. I4; H-ACT-01; X-E04
Pass criteria. I4: viewpoint execution acquires evidence and improves measured belief; beat simple views under matched time/energy.
Dependencies. Phases 6-7 for integration; candidate training earlier
## Phase 9: Model1 Decision Autonomy
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. Burak provides only declared physical/environmental sensor constraints.
Inputs. Belief queries; mission context; constraint engine
Outputs. Claim graph, grounded proposals, router and outcome monitor
Implementation tasks. Train decisions on correct, missing, stale, wrong, conflicting and OOD beliefs; preserve independent hard constraints.
Training / test data. Generated belief scenarios, then actual imperfect Model2 outputs
Baselines. FSM/BT/rules/utility/planner; generic agent; no-claim-graph ablation
Experiments. I5; H-M1-01; X-E01/02
Pass criteria. I5: traceable constrained decisions, low measured UIR and competitive mission outcomes; invalid proposals cannot execute.
Dependencies. Training parallel after P0; integrates after I4
## Phase 10: Ecological/Environmental Intelligence
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. Burak supplies measured channel characteristics when available.
Inputs. Core API; spatial context; audited ecology/field data
Outputs. Entity/field beliefs, MEIFE and CEFD
Implementation tasks. Qualify entity and field models separately; then coupling, confounding controls, multiscale dynamics and cross-domain messages.
Training / test data. Real ecological imagery/field records; explicitly paired synthetic worlds
Baselines. Entity-only; field-only; uncoupled; concatenation/cross-attention
Experiments. 2E-E01..12; I6
Pass criteria. I6: three domain beliefs coexist on one world. CEFD requires coupling benefit without unsupported ecological inference.
Dependencies. Parallel after Phase 3; full-domain integration after I5
## Phase 11: BAAC
Owner. Burak
Support / boundary input. Kerem owns schemas, ingestion, provenance validation and use in Unity/software; physical measurement is Burak-owned.
Inputs. Belief events, receiver model and channel interface
Outputs. Semantic deltas, adaptive fidelity, durable queue and scheduler
Implementation tasks. Implement acknowledgments/reconciliation policy, blackout recovery and mission-critical prioritization.
Training / test data. Synthetic beliefs/channel traces; measured channels when available
Baselines. Raw/FIFO/fixed priority/fixed fidelity/value per bit
Experiments. I7; H-COM-01; X-E05
Pass criteria. I7: useful receiver state persists across bandwidth sweep and outages; compare critical latency and sync error.
Dependencies. Develop independently after P0; integrated belief stream required
## Phase 12: Hardware Characterization
Owner. Kerem
Support / boundary input. Burak provides repeatable maneuvers, raw measurements, calibration and physical debugging; acceptance interpretation is shared.
Inputs. Available physical subsystems and measurement plan
Outputs. Measured RobotConfig parameters with units, ranges and provenance
Implementation tasks. Measure mass/CoM/CoB, thruster curves, power, camera/IMU/depth/sonar, latency and communication; retain raw logs.
Training / test data. Bench measurements; no fictitious nominal values
Baselines. Nominal vendor values only as labeled initial comparison
Experiments. S2R-CHAR-01; bench acceptance
Pass criteria. Parameter evidence recorded and configuration validated; unresolved measurements remain OPEN.
Dependencies. Hardware branch runs from project start; hardware owner leads
## Phase 13: Parameter Identification
Owner. Shared
Support / boundary input. Kerem owns simulator/RHI/HIL harness/logging/evaluation; Burak owns onboard hardware access, physical drivers as applicable, configuration and hardware debugging.
Inputs. Characterized components; repeatable vehicle maneuvers
Outputs. Fitted dynamics distributions and held-out validation report
Implementation tasks. Separate identification/validation trajectories; fit drag/added mass/thruster effectiveness and evaluate uncertainty.
Training / test data. Acceleration, translation, rotation, trim and station-keeping logs
Baselines. Nominal engineering model
Experiments. S2R-ID-01; S2R-VALIDATE-01
Pass criteria. Held-out errors meet predeclared envelope; only validated envelope earns L4 twin status.
Dependencies. Phase 12 and functioning vehicle; joint ownership

Outputs. Full onboard mission controlling simulated vehicle

## Phase 15: Controlled Physical Robot

Owner. Kerem

Baselines. Registry comparators; identical adaptation budgets
Experiments. H-CORE/2T/2E/2S/T2T/T2E/T2S/M1/ACT/COM/XFER/NAV; X-E01..05; SYS-E01..04

Dependencies. Research starts earlier; final claims require the relevant evidence level
# 27. Research and Experiment Registry
Design source: Conrad V2 discussion, source d6fc5b9c. Candidate mechanisms are EXPERIMENTAL until validated.
## Registry rules
Every hypothesis gets an ID:
Equation. H-DOMAIN-NN
and experiments:
Equation. E-DOMAIN-NN.x.
Every experiment must record code commit, configuration, dataset/twin versions, seed, checkpoint, metrics and hardware/compute environment.
The claim ladder remains:
Equation. Implemented -> Synthetic -> Public Real -> Controlled Hardware -> Representative Environment.
And no result is “better” merely because one metric improved. We care about:
Equation. boxedPerformance+Calibration+Robustness+Transfer
plus Safety where physical autonomy is involved.
## H-CORE-01 - Persistent Belief Architecture
### Hypothesis
A persistent structured belief representation containing identity, uncertainty, relationships, temporal state and evidence provenance will improve hidden-state reconstruction under partial observation relative to conventional history-based and persistent-memory baselines.
### Why
Ordinary sequence models can encode history but do not necessarily preserve explicit world identity, knowledge status, contradiction or evidence lineage.
### Baselines
latest-only
GRU/LSTM
Transformer history
SSM
RSSM/world model
persistent scene graph
PBA
### Experiments
E-CORE-01.1 - Observation sparsity
Equation. 100,50,20,5%.
01.2 - Long temporal gaps
01.3 - Re-observation after dormancy
01.4 - Entity disappearance/reappearance
01.5 - Contradictory history
01.6 - Long sequence persistence
### Metrics
Equation. StateError, Calibration, Memory, Latency.
Also identity consistency and unsupported-confidence rate.
### Success
PBA demonstrates a meaningful advantage on persistent/partially observable tasks, especially under sparse observations, without unacceptable compute overhead.
### Failure interpretation
If RSSM/scene-graph/recurrent baselines match it, the general PBA claim is weak. Preserve useful explicit memory/provenance engineering but narrow the research claim.
### Potential claim
A structured persistent belief architecture improves state estimation under selected partially observable inspection conditions.
Only after appropriate evidence.
## H-CORE-02 - BUO
### Hypothesis
Separating:
Equation. boxedtrust!= innovation!= contradiction
improves belief updating compared with confidence-weighted fusion or generic recurrent updates.
### Critical scenario
High-quality new evidence contradicts existing belief.
Correct response may initially be:
Equation. U_Cuparrow
rather than:
Equation. trustdownarrow
or immediate overwrite.
### Baselines
weighted averaging
Bayesian/Kalman-style fusion where applicable
confidence gating
GRU update
attention update
BUO without contradiction memory
full BUO
### Experiments
E-BUO-02.1 reliable supporting evidence.
02.2 unreliable supporting evidence.
02.3 reliable contradiction.
02.4 unreliable contradiction.
02.5 repeated independent contradiction.
02.6 contradiction eventually resolved.
02.7 sensor degradation.
### Metrics
State error, calibration, contradiction detection, resolution time and confident-wrong rate.
### Required ablations
Remove trust head.
Remove innovation head.
Remove explicit contradiction state.
Collapse trust + innovation.
### Success
Full BUO handles credible contradiction and changing state better than simpler fusion while remaining calibrated.
### Failure
If explicit trust/innovation decomposition adds nothing, simplify.
## H-CORE-03 - RBP
### Hypothesis
Typed relational propagation over persistent uncertain beliefs improves hidden-state inference while explicit provenance prevents relational inference from being confused with direct evidence.
### Baselines
no propagation
generic GNN
GAT
temporal GNN
untyped RBP
full typed RBP
### Experiments
E-RBP-03.1 hidden related component.
03.2 unrelated neighboring component.
03.3 wrong relationship.
03.4 uncertain relationship.
03.5 multiple-hop propagation.
03.6 missing direct evidence.
### Metrics
Hidden-state error and:
Equation. RC=P(false inferencemid misleading neighbor).
Also calibration by inference depth.
### Success
Relational inference improves hidden states while contamination remains controlled.
### Failure
If generic GNN performs equally well, RBP may remain an implementation structure rather than a novel mechanism.
## H-CORE-04 - TBD

## H-XFER-01 - Twin pretraining
This is the system-level hypothesis connecting all three twins.

For domain
Equation. d

### Experimental groups
Keep downstream adaptation identical:
G0 Random initialization

G2 Twin pretraining
G3 Generic + Twin pretraining
G4 Real-only task training
Then identical real adaptation budget.
### Required
Same architecture.
Same real training data.
Same compute budget as closely as practical.
Untouched final real test.
### Success
Equation. TU_d>0
with statistical/repeated-seed evidence and no major calibration regression.

− BAAC

## SYS-E03 - Failure cascade

+

+

+

## SYS-E04 - OOD mission
Train on:
pipeline families A/B/C
test:
unseen family D
plus changed environmental regime and sensor characteristics.
Expected behavior isn’t necessarily full success.
Acceptable intelligent behavior can be:
Equation. U_Euparrow -> more evidence -> escalation.
That is far better than confident failure.
## Statistical protocol
For stochastic experiments, one run means very little.
Use repeated seeds.
Report:
Equation. meanpm CI
or appropriate distributional summaries.
Predefine primary metrics before final comparisons where practical.
Don’t cherry-pick the best seed.
## Experiment tiers
To control compute:
### Tier 0 - Smoke
Minutes.
Does it execute?
### Tier 1 - Development
Small datasets, few seeds.
Does hypothesis look plausible?
### Tier 2 - Benchmark
Full validation configuration.
### Tier 3 - Research
Baselines + ablations + repeated seeds + held-out/OOD.
### Tier 4 - Final
Frozen code/config + untouched final test.
This prevents wasting huge GPU budgets on broken hypotheses.
## Mechanism kill rule
This matters.
For each proposed mechanism:
If complexity does not buy measurable value, remove it.
Examples:
If:
Equation. TCDP~= generic temporal GNN,
don’t force TCDP into the paper.
If:
Equation. CEFD~= simple cross-attention,
simplify.
If:
Equation. hierarchical UAHSM~= flat sparse memory,
use the simpler representation.
If:
Equation. BAAC~= good deterministic scheduler,
the deterministic scheduler may be the better product.
This protects us from building novelty theater.
## Research priority
Not every hypothesis deserves equal resources.
### Tier A - central
Equation. boxedPBA/BUO
Equation. boxedTCDP
Equation. boxedUAHSM/OCPWE
Equation. boxedMCBR
These directly support the project’s core hidden-state/autonomous-inspection thesis.
### Tier B - strong extensions
Equation. CEFD/MEIFE, EGDC, BAAC.
### Tier C - supporting research
Equation. RGDA, belief-aware navigation, hybrid control.
Do not let Tier C delay the central demonstration.

This is useful both scientifically and for future IP work.
Do not equate this registry with a patentability conclusion.

Maintain:

    prohibited_wording
Example:
Before experiment:
“TCDP is a proposed mechanism for topology-conditioned structural inference.”
After synthetic success:
“TCDP improved hidden-state reconstruction in our synthetic benchmark.”
After held-out real validation:
“TCDP improved X on dataset Y relative to baselines Z.”
Never jump straight to:
“world’s first revolutionary infrastructure intelligence.”
## Master experiment dependency
The R&D order now becomes:
Equation. Core -> Domain pair -> Real transfer -> Downstream autonomy -> Integrated system.
Specifically:
Equation. PBA/BUO -> UAHSM -> TCDP -> MCBR -> EGDC -> SYS.
2E and BAAC run largely in parallel.
## What the first research milestone should be
Not all 13 mechanisms.
The first coherent paper-quality technical story could be:
Equation. Persistent Belief+Spatial/Structural Hidden State +Active Information Acquisition
Mission:
reconstruct an underwater infrastructure state from incomplete observations and autonomously seek the observations that matter.
That is a much stronger coherent story than throwing every subsystem into one “AI underwater robot” claim.

# 28. Production Contract Closure and Open Decisions
PROPOSED IMPLEMENTATION CLARIFICATIONS. These items resolve ambiguities in the existing interfaces. They require Phase 0 contract review and tests before Interface Freeze V1. The source chapters remain the authority for module scope; no numerical threshold below is claimed to have been measured.
## Time, Frames and Identity
Use an envelope that distinguishes acquisition time from processing and publication time. Evidence about an observation retains its measurement timestamp. Processing latency belongs in a separate field. The earlier informal condition that evidence time follows observation time applies to creation/publication time, not a change in the time the physical measurement occurred.
class MessageEnvelope:
    schema_version: str
    message_id: UUID
    scenario_id: UUID | None
    mission_id: UUID
    run_id: UUID
    measurement_time_ns: int
    created_time_ns: int
    clock_domain: str
    time_uncertainty_ns: int | None
    producer_version: str
    correlation_id: UUID
    causation_ids: list[UUID]
Frame transforms follow the source convention T_A<- B: coordinates expressed in B become coordinates expressed in A. Quaternion order is the source’s (w,x,y,z). WORLD handedness, vertical axis, geodetic origin and Unity conversion are OPEN until the frame contract is tested. Use SI at software boundaries and declare every field’s units; any sensor-native units are converted by its adapter.
A belief ID is an inference-owned identifier. A known asset registry ID may be associated with it when justified. A simulator’s hidden entity ID must not become an oracle identity feature. Training labels may retain truth identity in a separate supervision object. Unknown entities begin without a known world-entity association.
def transform_point(p_B, T_A_from_B):
    return T_A_from_B.rotation @ p_B + T_A_from_B.translation

# Contract tests:
# T_A_from_B @ T_B_from_A approximately equals identity.
# Unit-axis probes match the documented Unity adapter.
# Round-trip pose preserves position and orientation.
# Acquisition timestamps survive encode/publish/replay unchanged.
## Belief Updates and Provenance
An evidence ID denotes a specific encoded observation contribution. Retried messages must not create independent corroborating observations. Revisioned belief updates should reference their predecessor; a stale update cannot silently replace a newer state. Independent supporting evidence and duplicate delivery are different cases.
class BeliefRevision:
    belief_id: UUID
    revision: int
    predecessor_revision: int | None
    measurement_time_ns: int
    consumed_evidence_ids: list[UUID]
    provenance_root: UUID
    update_kind: str  # DIRECT, RELATIONAL, PREDICTED, CONTEXT

def commit_update(store, update):
    validate_schema(update)
    validate_provenance_dag(update.provenance_root)
    reject_duplicate_evidence_contributions(update)
    check_expected_predecessor(update)
    return store.atomic_commit(update)
The exact persistence backend, transaction implementation and delayed-measurement policy remain OPEN. Before freeze, choose and test bounded rewind/replay or explicit late-evidence handling. Silently applying an old measurement as though it were current is invalid. Merge/split events must preserve old IDs as resolvable lineage, rather than destroying their evidence history.
Knowledge status is property-level when a belief mixes direct and inferred content. OBSERVED means supported by direct observations, not guaranteed true. PREDICTED marks temporal extrapolation. UNKNOWN is an epistemic status, not a numerical occupancy value. MIXED is a summary status; consumers still need the status of the property used in a decision.
## Tensor and Mask Semantics
The source defaults are D_e=256, D_z=256, D_u=64, D_t=64, D_h=128 and D_r=64. They remain configuration values. Support ragged evidence and graph batches through offsets or masks; padding must not affect updates, association or loss denominators. Association supports NO_MATCH. Availability masks are distinct from class labels.
class SequenceBatch:
    evidence: Tensor       # [B, T, Kmax, De]
    evidence_mask: BoolTensor  # [B, T, Kmax]
    delta_t_s: Tensor      # [B, T]
    sequence_mask: BoolTensor  # [B, T]
    labels: dict[str, Tensor]
    label_masks: dict[str, BoolTensor]
    graph_offsets: LongTensor
    provenance_refs: list
This batched wrapper is a proposed implementation shape around the source’s per-step E[K,D_e] and Z[N,D_z] contracts. The exact collator is OPEN. Empty observation batches still permit temporal prediction; they must not fabricate direct evidence.
For target j with availability mask m_bj, an implementable masked objective is:
Equation. L_j=fracsum_b m_bj ell_j(hat y_bj,y_bj)max(1,sum_b m_bj).
Return zero contribution with an explicit no-supervision count when all masks are false. Log denominators so datasets with sparse labels cannot appear better simply because difficult targets were absent. Loss weights, task normalization and the exact likelihood family are EXPERIMENTAL.
Uncertainty outputs need units/semantics and calibration metadata. Four scalar fields do not imply four mutually exclusive probabilities or quantities that sum to one. Do not add them to create a universal risk score unless that mapping is explicitly calibrated for the consuming decision.
## Runtime and Safety Contract
Each module exposes availability (AVAILABLE, DEGRADED, UNAVAILABLE, STALE), version and freshness. Query replies should be coherent snapshots or carry enough revisions for the consumer to detect mixed-time inputs. Model1 proposals, accepted goals, trajectories, requested wrench and allocated thruster commands remain distinct records.
def execute_proposal(proposal, belief_snapshot, robot_state):
    claims = validate_claim_support(proposal, belief_snapshot)
    decision = constraints.check(proposal, claims, robot_state)
    if not decision.accepted:
        return RejectedAction(decision.reason_codes)
    goal = route_accepted_action(proposal)
    return navigation.submit(goal)
The Safety Supervisor independently monitors freshness, health, deadlines and permitted motion. Timeout behavior, degraded modes, emergency action and recovery must be defined per physical configuration. A generic always-surface instruction is not a valid universal fallback in obstructed environments. These settings are OPEN hardware/mission contracts.
## Acceptance Records
The design deliberately leaves many numerical pass thresholds unresolved. Define them before the final benchmark, using baseline measurements and the mission envelope. An unfilled required threshold yields NOT EVALUABLE, never PASS.
acceptance:
  gate_id: I4
  primary_metric: hidden_state_error
  direction: lower
  baseline: fixed_views
  minimum_effect: OPEN
  uncertainty_method: OPEN
  evaluation_split_hash: OPEN
  seeds: OPEN
  budgets:
    time_s: OPEN
    energy_j: OPEN
  guardrails:
    unsupported_confidence_max: OPEN
    safety_violations_max: OPEN
  evidence_artifact: null
  status: NOT_EVALUABLE
Deterministic contract gates can require zero violations in their defined suite. Research superiority needs matched budgets, baseline implementation evidence, effect sizes and uncertainty intervals. No fixed seed count, percentage improvement or physical tolerance is invented here.
## Contract Closure Tests
ID | Fixture and expected result
CC-01 | Duplicate evidence delivery produces one contribution, with unchanged independent-observation count.
CC-02 | Late observation is replayed or explicitly handled as late; it never masquerades as a current observation.
CC-03 | Failed belief write leaves no half-written graph/provenance revision.
CC-04 | Merge/split preserves traceability of every predecessor and raw observation.
CC-05 | All-missing labels yield zero masked loss and a reported zero denominator.
CC-06 | Padding/permutation leaves set aggregation and valid-sample loss unchanged.
CC-07 | Hidden truth IDs and truth payloads are absent from inference inputs and deployment imports.
CC-08 | Expired or invalid command is rejected at the configured execution boundary.
CC-09 | Working-memory reset preserves persistent belief store and evidence archive.
CC-10 | Replay reproduces decisions within the declared numerical tolerance and preserves event lineage.

## Requirements Closure
Core equations, tensor shapes, forward passes and losses are in Chapters 3-9. Full 2T/2E/2S implementations and twin design are in Chapters 10-15.
