  ci.yml contract.yml nightly.yml
docs/
  adr/ IMPLEMENTATION_MAP.md RUNBOOK.md DATASET_REGISTER.md MODEL_CARDS/
artifacts/                       # gitignored; never source authority
No notebook is a production import path. Exploratory notebooks may read immutable run artifacts but may not write production schemas, manifests, model weights or acceptance records.
## Configuration, run identity and artifact rules
All configuration is YAML validated into frozen Pydantic models. Composition is explicit: base -> mode -> experiment -> local override. A local override is gitignored, may contain only machine paths/device selectors, and is copied into a redacted run snapshot. Unknown keys fail validation.
run:
  run_id: UUIDv7                 # generated once before any work
  parent_run_id: null
  architecture_id: conrad_v2_impl_freeze_v0_2
  stack_id: conrad_v2_stack_freeze_v0_2
  git_commit: REQUIRED_CLEAN_COMMIT
  config_digest: sha256
  seed: 2026201
  mode: sim_train                # sim_train | eval | replay | runtime | hil
data:
  manifest_ids: [REQUIRED]
  split_hash: REQUIRED
runtime:
  persistence_mode: sqlite_wal_single_writer
  object_store_uri: file://artifacts/objects
  command_mode: disabled          # disabled | simulated | hardware
A run directory is immutable after terminal state except for a separate notes/ append-only area:
artifacts/runs/<run_id>/
  config.resolved.yaml  environment.json  git.json  manifests.json
  metrics.jsonl         events.jsonl      checkpoints/  reports/
  figures/              replay/           logs/         notes/
environment.json records OS, Python, lockfile digest, CUDA/driver visibility, device names, effective precision and determinism flags. git.json records the commit and rejects dirty tracked source for benchmark, acceptance and physical runs. Development runs may be dirty only when dirty_diff.patch is retained and status is NONCOMPARABLE.
## Persistence, replay and retention reference implementation
V0.1 uses SQLite in WAL mode with a single writer process for belief revisions, lineage, provenance indexes, run metadata and command/event receipts. All blobs larger than 1 MB, raw sensor files, Unity frames, point clouds and checkpoints use a content-addressed local object store at artifacts/objects/sha256/<digest>. SQLite stores only immutable object digests and metadata. This is the reference implementation for simulation, HIL and one robot. It is not a claim of fleet scalability.
The only writer is runtime.supervisor. Workers submit validated messages to its in-process queue. Database writes use an IMMEDIATE transaction and implement idempotency on (message_id, producer_version) plus evidence-deduplication rules from Chapter 28. Schema migrations are Alembic revisions; application startup refuses a newer database schema than its code supports.
Required tables are runs, scenarios, observations, evidence, beliefs, belief_revisions, provenance_nodes, provenance_edges, commands, events, artifact_refs, dataset_manifests and migration_history. Store UTC integer nanoseconds, UUID strings and SI values only. A raw object is never updated in place.
Back up the SQLite database plus referenced object digests as one manifest. Recovery is valid only when: the database integrity check passes; every referenced digest exists and hashes correctly; replay of a retained scenario reproduces its event sequence; and the restore report is attached to the run record. Default retention is: raw physical/HIL data retained indefinitely until an explicit data-policy ADR; synthetic raw frames 90 days; checkpoints and final reports indefinitely; intermediate temporary caches 14 days. A retention operation must never delete an object still referenced by a retained run.
## Runtime process model and state machine
V0.1 is deliberately a single supervised Python runtime, with bounded worker pools for compute. It avoids distributed state races while contracts are being proven. Unity, datasets and RobotHardwareInterface are adapters outside the core process boundary. The process state machine is:
BOOT -> SELF_TEST -> CONFIGURED -> CONNECTING -> READY -> RUNNING
RUNNING -> DEGRADED -> RUNNING
RUNNING | DEGRADED -> SAFE_HOLD -> STOPPING -> STOPPED
any state -> FAILED
SELF_TEST checks schemas, migrations, disk headroom, object-store write/read hash, required adapter availability, frame contract fixture, RobotConfig checksum and all configured model checkpoint metadata. READY allows simulation/replay. A runtime cannot enter hardware RUNNING until the hardware adapter, safety supervisor and command gateway report healthy and the HIL gate declared by the mission config has passed.
Every event is an append-only MessageEnvelope from Chapter 28 plus event_type, severity, availability, payload_digest and trace_id. Event types include OBSERVATION_RECEIVED, EVIDENCE_CREATED, BELIEF_COMMITTED, PLAN_PROPOSED, ACTION_REJECTED, GOAL_ACCEPTED, COMMAND_SENT, COMMAND_ACK, FAULT_DETECTED, SAFE_HOLD_ENTERED and RUN_TERMINATED. Logs must be structured JSONL and must not contain raw credentials or unredacted personal data.
The command gateway is the sole code path allowed to invoke RobotHardwareInterface.send. It accepts only a safety-approved, non-expired AllocatedCommand carrying mission_id, run_id, command_id, belief_snapshot_id, robot_config_digest, issued/deadline times and a provenance root. Raw PWM, untyped UDP payloads and direct model outputs are rejected before transport.
## Network, secrets and operator safety boundaries
The reference deployment has no public internet control endpoint. Robot, Unity and operator clients run on an explicitly configured private network. Bind adapters to configured addresses only; fail closed on an unknown peer, schema version or clock-domain mismatch. Remote access, fleet control and user authentication are out of scope for V0.1 and require an ADR rather than a convenient exposed port.
Secrets are loaded only from environment variables or a local OS-managed secret store. .env, credential files, SDK tokens, private keys and raw physical logs are gitignored. The doctor command reports missing secret names only. Artifact redaction removes secret-shaped values and camera/location metadata where policy requires it before an artifact is exported.
An operator command can request start, pause, safe_hold, resume or stop, but never bypasses the safety gateway. safe_hold has a RobotConfig- and mission-specific action selected by the hardware safety contract; it is not hard-coded as a surface command. Hardware enable is a physical/machine-local configuration switch, default false.
## CI, release and reproducibility gates
Every pull request runs these mandatory jobs on a clean CPU environment:
format-and-lint: ruff format --check; ruff check
type-check:       mypy conrad tests
unit-property:    pytest tests/unit tests/property
contract:          pytest tests/contract
security-hygiene:  secret scan; dependency-lock verification; no tracked artifacts
schema-compat:     old fixture decode and current fixture round trip
main additionally runs deterministic Twin smoke scenarios, integration/replay tests and all CC-01 through CC-10. A scheduled GPU workflow runs one fixed-seed ECMER/Core forward-backward test and each V0.1 module shape/mask/leakage test. Nightly does not redefine research success; it detects regressions against fixed fixtures. Physical hardware is never controlled by CI.
A release is a signed git tag plus lockfile digest, migration revision, schema versions, Unity adapter version, RobotConfig digest and a completed release checklist. There are three release lanes: dev for noncomparable development; candidate for reproducible sim/HIL evidence; physical for a candidate that has passed the required I-gate. No checkpoint is promoted merely because it has the newest timestamp.
## Required operator commands and success criteria
The CLI is a contract, not a collection of ad hoc scripts:
uv run conrad doctor --config configs/runtime/default.yaml
uv run conrad sim run --scenario <scenario_id> --config <config.yaml>
uv run conrad train run --config <config.yaml>
uv run conrad eval run --run <run_id> --config <config.yaml>
uv run conrad replay run --run <run_id>
uv run conrad runtime start --config <config.yaml>
uv run conrad runtime safe-hold --mission <mission_id>
uv run conrad data verify --manifest <manifest_id>
uv run conrad db migrate
doctor must finish with a machine-readable report and nonzero exit on a missing contract, incompatible checkpoint, invalid migration, unavailable required adapter, insufficient disk threshold, invalid RobotConfig or forbidden command_mode. sim run writes a complete run directory. replay run verifies object digests before processing. runtime start defaults to simulation unless command_mode: hardware, local physical enable and the required safety preconditions all hold.
## Closure tests for the software stack
ID | Required result
SS-01 | A fresh supported host completes uv sync, lint, types and all CPU tests without undeclared dependency imports.
SS-02 | A fixed simulation config creates two byte-equivalent manifests and equivalent event/revision sequences under the declared deterministic tolerance.
SS-03 | Restart during a belief write yields either no commit or one complete idempotent commit, never a partial revision/provenance graph.
SS-04 | Deleting a referenced object causes replay and evaluation to fail closed with its digest named.
SS-05 | Checkpoint, schema, config, architecture and stack IDs must all match before model loading; an incompatible pair is rejected.
SS-06 | A model, adapter or database health failure reaches SAFE_HOLD without sending an unapproved command.
SS-07 | Raw model output, expired command, wrong RobotConfig digest and unknown peer are all rejected by the gateway.
SS-08 | A clean restore passes integrity, digest verification and the registered replay fixture.
SS-09 | CI fails on a schema compatibility break, untracked migration, missing lock update, tracked credential or direct vendor import from core.
SS-10 | A physical run cannot begin without local enable, HIL evidence reference, safety configuration, hardware health and an explicit command_mode: hardware.
This closure freeze makes the specification sufficient for an agent to construct the complete software stack as a reproducible candidate system. It does not erase legitimate external prerequisites: Burak’s RobotConfig and hardware characterization, dataset rights/verification, target-environment calibration, and experiment evidence remain required before physical or scientific-success claims.
# 35. V0.2 Execution Priorities and Evidence Program
## Authority
EXECUTION PRIORITY FREEZE. This chapter adds no model family, no new intelligence subsystem and no replacement for Chapters 1-34. It specifies the order in which the existing Conrad V0.2 architecture must be built, tested and defended. Work may proceed in parallel only when it does not bypass a listed dependency or fabricate evidence.
## Priority 1: Minimum serious 2S plus 2T vertical slice
The first serious integrated demonstration is the existing 2S plus 2T loop, not a broad but disconnected collection of child models:
Twin2S/OCPWE + Twin2T/MCDE
  -> Unity V2 observations
  -> ECMER + association + BUO + RBP + TBD + PMBL
  -> UAHSM + TCDP
  -> MCBR observation plan
  -> navigation and safety constraints
  -> new observation and belief revision
  -> EGDC claim-grounded decision + BAAC message
2E may advance through its own contracts and data program, but it does not block this vertical slice. The vertical-slice run must start with a deliberately occluded or degraded region, preserve an explicit UNKNOWN or multi-hypothesis belief, select a feasible informative viewpoint, acquire the observation through Unity, update the affected 2S/2T beliefs with provenance, and produce a grounded decision or a justified abstention.
Pass evidence. A replayable run bundle contains scenario/twin versions, seed, all observations/evidence/revisions, MCBR candidate table, rejected candidates with reasons, chosen trajectory, safety decisions, before/after uncertainty, claim graph and BAAC output. A video alone is not pass evidence.
## Priority 2: Conrad-owned representation corpus
Build DATA-CONRAD-SSL-01 before large supervised training. Its corpus is not merely a folder of images. Every capture unit has a manifest row with source, owner/rights status, sensor calibration, timestamp/clock domain, pose/frame information, scenario/mission ID, augmentation eligibility, and split lineage.
Required corpus partitions are:
Partition | Allowed content | Purpose
ssl_synthetic_train | Versioned Twin2S/Twin2T/Twin2E plus Unity observations. | High-volume self-supervised correspondence and corruption training.
ssl_real_train | Rights-cleared Conrad-collected unlabelled RGB/sonar/point-cloud/sensor sequences. | Real-domain representation adaptation without fabricated labels.
val_in_domain | Held-out scenarios, assets, sessions and capture trajectories. | Checkpoint selection only.
test_in_domain | Sealed lineages never used for architecture or threshold selection. | Final in-domain report.
test_ood | Held-out structure, degradation, water/sensor and mission regimes. | Robustness and calibration report.
Do not split adjacent frames, views of one physical asset, derived augmentations or one Twin lineage across train and test. Record the full preprocessing graph and source-object digest. The first corpus acceptance report must state coverage counts by modality, scenario regime, structural condition, visibility, turbidity/noise, viewpoint and missing-modality pattern; a raw image count alone is insufficient.
## Priority 3: Golden scenario suite
Create immutable GOLDEN-V0.2 fixtures before mechanism tuning. Each fixture has one scenario ID, fixed seed, Twin versions, Unity/RobotConfig version, manifest digest, expected invariant(s), permitted numerical tolerance and an oracle-restricted truth payload that cannot enter inference.
The minimum suite is:
ID | Controlled condition | Required assertion
GS-01 | Occluded critical spatial region. | Model2S retains UNKNOWN rather than confident invented occupancy.
GS-02 | Two plausible counterfactual spatial worlds. | MCBR chooses a feasible discriminating observation or explicitly reports no feasible option.
GS-03 | High-quality direct supporting evidence. | BUO improves the correct belief and records direct provenance.
GS-04 | Reliable contradictory evidence. | BUO raises contradiction uncertainty; RBP does not erase direct evidence.
GS-05 | Duplicate/retried observation delivery. | Exactly one evidence contribution is committed.
GS-06 | Corrosion/fatigue state hidden behind incomplete coverage. | 2T distinguishes observed condition from propagated/predicted condition.
GS-07 | Sonar/RGB degradation and modality dropout. | Quality head degrades calibration and unsupported claims remain bounded.
GS-08 | Unsafe or infeasible high-information viewpoint. | MCBR/navigation rejects it before any command gateway request.
GS-09 | Stale pose, delayed evidence or clock uncertainty. | Runtime applies the declared late-evidence policy and preserves chronology.
GS-10 | Reboot/replay after persistent belief activity. | Revisions, lineage, evidence digests and decision sequence remain reproducible.
Every pull request runs a compact deterministic subset. Candidate-model changes run the full suite. No run may overwrite a golden expected result without a reviewed ADR and a new fixture version.
## Priority 4: Measured sim-to-real and randomization ledger
Create SIMREAL-LEDGER-01. Each randomized parameter must have a semantic name, unit, prior/range, source of range, sampling law, affected simulator module, physical measurement status and calibration revision. Randomize only meaningful physical or sensing quantities: lighting/attenuation/backscatter/turbidity; sonar noise and artifacts; camera intrinsics/noise/latency; pose error; currents; mass/inertia/buoyancy; thruster curves/deadzones/latency; power limits; communication loss/latency; and sensor faults.
UNMEASURED is a valid ledger state but cannot be silently treated as calibrated. Once Burak supplies characterization, the ledger attaches raw-log provenance and updates the simulation prior through a versioned parameter-identification report. A randomization range that cannot be explained in physical terms is rejected. Synthetic success never becomes a physical-success claim without the matching HIL/physical gate.
## Priority 5: Mandatory comparison and kill protocol
For ECMER, run the three matched encoder conditions required by Chapter 33: CONRAD-SSL, BASELINE-ECMER-IMGNET, and BASELINE-ECMER-RANDOM-SUPERVISED. For system mechanisms, run matched removals of RBP, BUO, TBD/PMBL and MCBR, plus the fixed-viewpoint inspection baseline. Each comparison uses the same data manifest, scenario set, seed set, compute budget, interface version and metric code.
An experiment report must answer four separate questions: does the mechanism improve task error; does it improve calibration/unsupported-confidence behavior; does it improve OOD performance; and does its benefit justify compute, latency and memory cost. A mechanism with no repeatable benefit, a calibration regression, leakage risk, or a safety/real-time regression is marked KILL_CANDIDATE; retaining it requires an ADR with a documented mission-specific reason. No demonstration may use a stronger baseline only in an unfavorable configuration.
## Priority 6: Provenance and uncertainty demonstration contract
The minimum serious demo must present every significant conclusion as a queryable claim: the current value/status, direct/relational/predicted provenance, source evidence IDs, uncertainty decomposition, alternatives when relevant, freshness, and the action/observation that would reduce the decision-relevant uncertainty. The UI/report may simplify presentation, but the underlying query response must remain available in the replay artifact.
The demo fails its scientific-intelligence claim if it only produces an attractive reconstruction, detector overlay, trajectory or final answer without this chain of justification. A justified abstention is a valid successful outcome when evidence is insufficient.
## Priority 7: Invention and experiment record
Maintain an append-only research_notebook/ and experiment registry from the first implementation commit. Each entry records date/time, contributor, architecture/stack ID, hypothesis, source sections, scenario/data versions, method, assumptions, implementation commit, result, raw-artifact locations, limitation, decision and next action. Preserve negative and inconclusive results.
This is an engineering and research provenance requirement, not a patent or novelty claim. Before any external IP claim, obtain qualified legal advice and keep claims separate from measured technical results.
## V0.2 completion order
DATA-CONRAD-SSL-01 + GOLDEN-V0.2
  -> ECMER Conrad self-supervised pretraining comparison
  -> 2S + 2T vertical slice in simulation
  -> SIMREAL-LEDGER-01 + parameter identification inputs
  -> HIL integration
  -> controlled physical validation
  -> 2E expansion and full multi-domain demonstration
Completion of a later line never retroactively proves an earlier missing gate. This order is designed to produce a defendable Conrad capability as early as possible while protecting the custom architecture from unsupported claims.
# 36. V0.2 Software Assurance, Operations and Governance Requirements
## Authority and non-expansion rule
V0.2 ASSURANCE FREEZE. This chapter operationalizes the existing architecture. It introduces no new core model, twin, cloud service, LLM controller, detector dependency or intelligence subsystem. The V0.2 work is to make the frozen system measurable, replayable, safe, secure and defensible. A proposal for an additional core subsystem requires a separate architecture review after the Chapter 35 vertical slice and evidence gates, not during implementation convenience.
## Model governance and promotion policy
Every canonical learned component remains Conrad-built: custom system architecture, Conrad-owned self-supervised representation pretraining, versioned Twin/Unity supervision and rights-cleared real capture. Standard primitives such as convolutions, attention, graph operations, GRUs and U-Nets are permitted implementation tools; externally imported pretrained weights are not part of the canonical V0.2 model path.
Every retained module must have a matched baseline, ablation, OOD, calibration, latency, memory and truth-leakage report. A module is promoted only when its result is repeatable across the declared seeds and data/scenario releases, improves the declared mission metric without violating guardrails, and has an ADR recording the retained configuration. Demonstration footage, a single seed, a single scenario, synthetic-only success or an uncalibrated score cannot promote a mechanism.
The following are automatic KILL_CANDIDATE conditions: no repeatable benefit over the matched baseline; worse calibration or unsupported-confidence behavior; truth/provenance leakage; a safety or deadline regression; or resource cost outside the RobotConfig-specific budget without a documented mission benefit. An ADR may retain a kill candidate only by naming the specific mission condition and compensating control.
## Data, labels and provenance requirements
DATA-CONRAD-SSL-01 and the Chapter 24 dataset registry are mandatory production systems. Every raw object, derived sample, label, augmentation, embedding, feature cache, checkpoint and report must be traceable to an immutable source digest, approved manifest, preprocessing version and split lineage. A sample never becomes independent merely because it was augmented, cropped, rerendered or exported in a new format.
Store raw RGB, sonar, point cloud/range, IMU/depth/power/health, pose, timestamp/clock domain, calibration revision, mission/scenario ID and capture configuration whenever available. Missing fields are represented as missing; they are never silently filled with a nominal value. Public datasets enter only through verified adapters with explicit rights/redistribution/deployment review. Do not manufacture labels, synchronized pairs, ground truth or calibration from a dataset that does not provide them.
The data acceptance report must tabulate modality, scenario regime, asset/lineage, condition/degradation, visibility, water/sensor condition, viewpoint, missing-modality pattern and label availability. Train, validation, test and OOD partitions must separate physical asset, mission/capture session, Twin lineage, trajectory and adjacent temporal views as applicable.
## Interface, configuration and compatibility policy
Core packages may import only Conrad schemas and approved generic dependencies. Unity, ROS, vendor SDKs, camera/sonar drivers and thruster drivers are adapter-only dependencies. ROS remains optional in V0.2 and may be introduced only where an actual hardware driver or integration requires it; the Model 2 Core, domains, twins and decision stack must remain runnable without ROS.
All public messages carry schema version, IDs, time/clock domain, SI units, coordinate frame, availability/freshness, provenance and compatibility information required by Chapters 2 and 28. Untyped payloads, raw UDP control payloads and direct model-to-thruster calls are prohibited.
Before loading a checkpoint or starting a mission, validate the compatibility tuple:
schema_version + database_migration + architecture_id + stack_id
+ checkpoint_id + data_manifest/split_hash + Twin/Unity adapter version
+ RobotConfig digest + RobotHardwareInterface capability version
Any incompatible, unknown or unverified element fails closed with an explicit reason code. Every irreversible interface, persistence, frame, safety, data, model or migration decision has a short ADR: context, alternatives, decision, evidence, compatibility/migration plan and rollback behavior.
## Execution lanes, safety and human authority
The four execution lanes are dev, simulation, HIL and physical. They share schemas and replay behavior but never share implicit authority. dev cannot send commands; simulation can command only simulated adapters; HIL requires its declared hardware and safety fixture; physical requires explicit local hardware enable, a compatible measured RobotConfig, required HIL evidence, live health, safety configuration and command_mode: hardware.
The command gateway remains the sole process with the capability to call RobotHardwareInterface.send. Perception, Model 2, Model 1, MCBR, BAAC, navigation and UI processes may propose or request work but cannot bypass the gateway. The gateway rejects raw/untyped commands, stale or expired commands, unknown peers, mismatched RobotConfig digests, invalid health state and unsupported capabilities.
Human actions start, pause, safe_hold, resume and stop are append-only events with operator identity, reason, time, active run/mission, belief snapshot, health state, resulting command/action and provenance root. Human override is not an invisible correction path. safe_hold is mission- and RobotConfig-specific; it is never assumed to mean an unconditional surface maneuver.
## Test, evaluation, verification and validation program
Each public module needs unit, property, contract, integration, replay, fault-injection and truth-leakage tests. Property tests cover masks, permutation invariance, ragged batches, IDs, timestamps, frames, units, idempotency, merge/split lineage and bounded late-data behavior. Contract tests exercise every public schema and adapter. Full vertical-slice integration tests must use the golden scenario suite.
Fault injection is normal development work. The minimum injected faults are corrupt/missing/delayed/duplicated observations; stale or inconsistent clocks; invalid pose/frame; missing calibration; sensor dropout; database restart; missing artifact object; object hash mismatch; disk-pressure threshold; network loss; communication delay; low power; thruster mismatch; and a command-gateway rejection. Every discovered production or HIL defect creates a retained regression fixture.
Evaluation reports always separate task performance, calibration, unsupported-confidence behavior, OOD performance, latency, memory, energy/thermal cost, safety outcomes and known failures. Tests and benchmarks identify their manifests, splits, seeds, metric implementation and resource budget. Acceptance claims require repeatable test, evaluation, verification and validation evidence; they do not inherit validity from a more visually impressive demo.
## Replay, observability and operator review
Replay is a prerequisite to an operator dashboard. Every run emits correlated structured events with at least run_id, mission_id, trace_id, belief_revision, evidence_id, checkpoint_id, command_id, module version, availability and monotonic/measurement time. Logs, metrics and traces must be queryable together. The initial local artifact backend may remain simple; the correlation contract must not.
Build a local operator/review console only after replay passes. Its mandatory views are: robot health and mission state; 2S/2T belief with observed/inferred/predicted/unknown status; uncertainty and provenance for a selected claim; MCBR candidates, exclusions and selected observation; safety/command outcomes; fault-injection controls in simulation; and replay controls. The console is an explanatory and debugging surface, not an independent decision authority.
Performance budgets are measured per RobotConfig and mission rather than invented universally. Record end-to-end observation-to-belief latency, belief-update rate, planning deadline, VRAM/RAM, disk/object-store use, compute utilization, battery/power impact and thermal behavior. A model that exceeds the actual vehicle envelope is not a deployable physical model regardless of offline task score.
## Security, release and recovery requirements
Commit lockfiles; scan source/config/artifacts for secrets; use local secret storage or environment injection; prohibit credentials in manifests, logs and exports; and verify content hashes before loading data, artifacts or weights. Every candidate/physical release has a signed tag or equivalent immutable release ID, dependency lock digest, schema/database migration version, model/checkpoint IDs, manifest IDs, RobotConfig digest, Unity adapter version, acceptance evidence and software bill of materials.
The V0.2 reference deployment has no public remote-control endpoint. It binds configured private interfaces only and fails closed on unknown peer, certificate/credential failure where applicable, schema mismatch or invalid command envelope. Remote/fleet control is outside V0.2 unless later designed under an ADR and security review.
Backups include the belief/revision store and all referenced immutable objects. Restore acceptance requires database integrity, object-digest verification, compatibility validation and successful replay of a registered fixture. Retention may remove only objects unreferenced by retained run, experiment, incident or release records.
## Change impact, incident and research record
Before merging an interface/model/data/runtime change, generate a change-impact report naming affected schemas, adapters, models, tests, golden scenarios, baselines, gates, data manifests, physical assumptions and required reruns. Changes that affect a physical boundary cannot enter a physical lane without its updated HIL evidence.
An incident is opened for unsafe-command rejection that was not expected, unexpected belief revision, sensor/adapter failure, replay mismatch, corrupted artifact, safety violation or simulation-to-physical discrepancy. Preserve the complete run bundle, classify the violated invariant, identify root cause/containment, add a regression fixture, record verification evidence and close only after the fixture passes.
The append-only research notebook records contributors, hypotheses, sources, architecture/stack versions, data/scenario versions, assumptions, commit, results, limitations and decision. It retains failed and inconclusive work. It is engineering/research provenance; legal novelty or patent claims require qualified legal advice and remain separate from measured results.
## V0.2 operational definition of done
The V0.2 software stack is operationally ready for the first controlled HIL/physical gate only when the Chapter 35 vertical slice, DATA-CONRAD-SSL-01, GOLDEN-V0.2, SIMREAL-LEDGER-01, compatibility validation, command-gateway policy, replay, observability, fault-injection suite, restore fixture, release manifest and incident workflow all exist and pass at the applicable lane. This is not a claim that the robot is already validated in water; it defines the evidence required before making that claim.
# 37. Operator and Replay Console: Complete Implementation Specification
## Scope and authority
SOFTWARE SURFACE FREEZE. This chapter completes the user-facing portion of the V0.2 software stack. The Operator and Replay Console is a local, read-mostly operational interface for simulation, HIL and approved physical missions. It visualizes authoritative runtime data; it does not own a second belief store, recompute model truth, bypass safety or issue raw actuator commands.
The console is required for operational use and demonstration. It is not a marketing site, a cloud product, a generic chat interface or a new intelligence model. The runtime and replay artifacts remain the source of truth.
## Chosen technology and repository layout
Use TypeScript with React 19, Vite, React Router, TanStack Query, Zustand and Tailwind CSS for the local console. Use FastAPI inside the existing supervised Python runtime for the typed read API, replay API and local event stream. Static console assets are built once and served by the runtime in packaged operation. There is no separate production frontend server, no cloud dependency and no microservice boundary.
conrad/
  api/
    app.py                 # FastAPI creation, local/static mounting, health route
    routes/                # runtime, missions, beliefs, plans, replay, artifacts, operator
    ws.py                  # typed read-only event subscriptions
    models.py              # Pydantic API DTOs; never ORM objects directly
  dashboard/
    package.json  vite.config.ts  tsconfig.json
    src/
      app/                 # router, query client, app shell, error boundary
      api/                 # generated/handwritten typed API client and WebSocket client
      state/               # UI-only Zustand state; no duplicate belief state
      pages/               # page components below
      components/          # reusable dense operational components
      features/            # mission, belief, map, claims, plan, replay, faults, incidents
      styles/
      test/
    public/
    playwright/
  tests/
    api/ dashboard/        # Python API and browser acceptance tests
The Python dependency group adds fastapi, uvicorn and websockets; the dashboard uses an exact lockfile committed under conrad/dashboard/. The frontend must be compatible with Node LTS selected in .tool-versions and must fail CI if its lockfile changes without package.json review. The backend API is versioned at /api/v1; a breaking change requires /api/v2 or an explicit compatibility migration.
## Runtime serving and access boundary
The console is served only by runtime.supervisor after SELF_TEST and is bound to 127.0.0.1 by default. An explicitly configured private-network bind is allowed only in simulation, HIL or physical mode with an operator-approved network allowlist. There is no public bind, internet authentication flow or remote command endpoint in V0.2.
The runtime exposes:
GET  /healthz                    # process health, no mission data
GET  /api/v1/meta                # schema/API/architecture/stack/runtime versions
GET  /api/v1/runtime/snapshot    # coherent health and active mission snapshot
GET  /api/v1/missions
GET  /api/v1/missions/{id}
GET  /api/v1/beliefs/{id}
GET  /api/v1/beliefs/{id}/lineage
GET  /api/v1/claims/{id}
GET  /api/v1/plans/{id}
GET  /api/v1/runs/{id}
GET  /api/v1/runs/{id}/timeline
GET  /api/v1/replays/{id}/snapshot?time_ns=
GET  /api/v1/artifacts/{digest}/metadata
GET  /api/v1/incidents
POST /api/v1/operator/{start|pause|safe-hold|resume|stop}
POST /api/v1/replays/{id}/seek
POST /api/v1/replays/{id}/speed
WS   /api/v1/events              # read-only typed runtime/replay deltas
Operator POST routes create an OperatorRequest; they never call navigation or hardware directly. The runtime applies identity/role, lane, safety, RobotConfig, freshness and command-gateway policy, then returns ACCEPTED, REJECTED or PENDING with reason codes. The UI disables unavailable actions but must not rely on UI behavior for enforcement.
class OperatorRequest(BaseModel):
    request_id: UUID
    action: Literal["start", "pause", "safe_hold", "resume", "stop"]
    mission_id: UUID | None
    run_id: UUID | None
    operator_id: str
    reason: str
    issued_time_ns: int
    expected_runtime_revision: int

class OperatorResponse(BaseModel):
    request_id: UUID
    status: Literal["ACCEPTED", "REJECTED", "PENDING"]
    reason_codes: list[str]
    resulting_event_id: UUID | None
    runtime_revision: int
## DTOs, coherence and event stream
API DTOs are Pydantic models derived from existing schemas. They include no raw secret, private key, hidden Twin truth field, unredacted physical location or raw actuator payload. Each read response includes api_version, runtime_revision, measurement_time_ns, published_time_ns, availability, freshness_ms, run_id, mission_id, architecture_id, stack_id and schema_version as applicable.
GET /runtime/snapshot is a coherent runtime revision. The event stream sends compact deltas only after a committed revision:
{
  "event_id": "uuid",
  "event_type": "BELIEF_COMMITTED",
  "runtime_revision": 841,
  "measurement_time_ns": 0,
  "published_time_ns": 0,
  "run_id": "uuid",
  "mission_id": "uuid",
  "entity_id": "uuid-or-null",
  "payload_ref": "/api/v1/beliefs/uuid",
  "availability": "AVAILABLE"
}
If a client detects a revision gap, reconnects, receives RESYNC_REQUIRED, or sees an incompatible API version, it discards transient UI state and reloads the coherent snapshot. WebSocket events are notification/delta hints; REST snapshots remain authoritative. The FastAPI implementation supports native WebSocket JSON send/receive and disconnect handling; it is used only for this local event subscription. FastAPI WebSocket reference
## Information architecture and routes
The console uses a dense three-region desktop layout: fixed left navigation, central operational workspace and contextual right inspector. It is responsive down to 1024 px, but physical mission operation requires a full desktop viewport. No decorative hero, marketing cards or nested-card layout is permitted.
Route | Purpose | Required content
/ | Mission overview | lane, runtime state, RobotConfig, health, active mission, alerts, resource budget, latest safety decision.
/mission | Live mission | map/scene, vehicle pose, mission timeline, sensor availability, active observation plan, action state.
/beliefs | Belief browser | filterable entity/cell list, domain/status/uncertainty/freshness filters, selected-belief inspector.
/beliefs/:id | Belief explanation | value, observed/inferred/predicted/unknown status, uncertainty decomposition, evidence, lineage, graph relations, revisions.
/spatial | 2S workspace | occupancy/SDF/semantic/status layers, coverage, uncertainty, pose uncertainty, selected spatial cell.
/structural | 2T workspace | structural graph, condition/degradation heads, direct versus propagated support, MCDE context.
/ecological | 2E workspace | entity/field layers, CEFD links, field scale/time view, disturbance/recovery state.
/plans/:id | Active intelligence | MCBR candidates, observability, value, discrimination, risk/cost, rejected reasons, chosen observation/trajectory.
/claims/:id | EGDC explanation | Decision Claim Graph, supporting/contradicting claims, constraints, proposed/accepted/rejected action.
/communications | BAAC | receiver state, semantic delta queue, fidelity, bits, link conditions and delivery/reconciliation status.
/replay/:id | Deterministic replay | timeline, speed, seek, revision/event selection, before/after compare, artifact links.
/faults | Simulation/HIL faults | approved fault templates, active fault schedule, injected-event history; disabled in physical lane.
/runs | Runs and artifacts | config, manifests, versions, checkpoints, metrics, report links and acceptance state.
/incidents | Incident review | severity, affected run, violated invariant, status, evidence, regression fixture and closure record.
/settings | Read-only configuration | API/meta versions, adapter capabilities, retention, lane and access state; no secret display.
## Required component contracts and states
AvailabilityBadge, KnowledgeStatusBadge, UncertaintyBreakdown, ProvenanceTree, RevisionTimeline, CandidateObservationTable, ConstraintDecision, ReplayTransport, HealthStrip, ArtifactLink and FaultSchedule are reusable components. Buttons use familiar icons for replay transport and tooltips for unfamiliar symbols; dangerous commands retain explicit text labels and confirmation state.
Every data surface implements these states: loading, empty, available, degraded, stale, unavailable, incompatible, permission_denied, resync_required and error. Missing data says why it is missing; it never renders as zero, safe, observed or healthy. Unknown, inferred and predicted data are visually distinct and never combined under one generic “confidence” color.
Map and graph visualizations must support selection-to-inspector linking. Selecting an entity/cell/claim/candidate opens its metadata, provenance, uncertainty, freshness and artifact links without losing the current mission/replay time. All visualizations use the same runtime_revision or replay time. If a coherent state cannot be obtained, show RESYNC_REQUIRED rather than mixing revisions.
## Visual and interaction requirements
Use a restrained operational palette: neutral surface/background; green only for healthy/accepted; amber for degraded/stale; red for rejected/fault/unsafe; cyan for observed; violet for inferred; gray for unknown; and dashed treatment for predicted. Color is never the only status signal. Use a legible system sans font, 14 px body minimum, fixed-width numeric/ID fields, 8 px maximum radius for repeated item cards, and stable dimensions for timelines, toolbars, tables and map panes.
Tables support stable column widths, sort, filter, virtualized rows above 200 items, copy of IDs/digests, and a detail action. The console must not show raw secret data, raw hardware commands, hidden Twin truth or any action as accepted before the authoritative response arrives.
## Client state, caching and offline behavior
TanStack Query owns server data with query keys containing route parameters plus run_id/replay_id and runtime revision where necessary. Zustand owns only local view state: selected item, panel widths, table filters, visible map layers, replay speed, drawer state and last acknowledged revision. It must not mirror or mutate belief/mission truth.
The client retains the most recent complete snapshot in memory only for rendering during a transient disconnect. It labels it STALE; it does not write it back, issue commands from it or pretend it is live. Browser storage may retain layout preferences and recent non-sensitive route IDs only. Physical mission data, credentials, command payloads and raw sensor data are never stored in browser local storage.
## Build, test and acceptance commands
cd conrad/dashboard
npm ci
npm run lint
npm run typecheck
npm run test
npm run build
npx playwright test

uv run pytest tests/api tests/dashboard
uv run conrad runtime start --config configs/runtime/simulation.yaml
Frontend unit tests use Vitest and React Testing Library. API tests use FastAPI TestClient including WebSocket connection/disconnect/resync tests. Browser acceptance tests use Playwright with seeded golden run artifacts. Playwright provides isolated browser end-to-end execution and trace/reporting support appropriate for this console. Playwright documentation
The mandatory browser acceptance suite is:
ID | Required result
UI-01 | Console renders a coherent simulation snapshot with lane, health and revision visible.
UI-02 | Selecting a belief exposes status, uncertainty, provenance and evidence links matching the API DTO.
UI-03 | Unknown/inferred/predicted/observed are distinct in text and visual treatment.
UI-04 | MCBR view shows chosen and rejected candidates with authoritative reason/value/cost fields.
UI-05 | Replay seek changes every view to one coherent replay time/revision; a gap forces resync.
UI-06 | A rejected operator request never appears accepted; reason codes remain visible.
UI-07 | Fault injection is enabled only in simulation/HIL and unavailable in physical mode.
UI-08 | No hidden Twin truth, raw command or secret appears in DOM, API response or browser storage fixture.
UI-09 | API/WebSocket version mismatch yields a safe incompatible/resync state without stale action availability.
UI-10 | Desktop and 1024 px viewport render without clipped controls, overlap or text overflow.
## Completion definition
The dashboard/console is complete when all routes above exist or explicitly render NOT_AVAILABLE_FOR_THIS_LANE; typed API and event schemas are versioned; all UI-01 through UI-10 pass; a Golden V0.2 replay can be inspected end to end; operator requests are recorded and cannot bypass the runtime; and the console is served from the same packaged local runtime as the configured V0.2 stack.
Together with Chapters 1-36, this chapter makes the document a build authority for the entire Conrad software technical stack: core ML, twins, data, training, evaluation, navigation/control, Unity, hardware adapters, runtime, persistence, safety, APIs, operator/replay console, testing, security, release and operational support. Physical measurements, rights-cleared data and validation results remain external evidence inputs, not missing software specifications.
